TITLE 6. CERTAIN STATE OFFICERS AND DEPARTMENTS. 

CHAPTER 26. CERTAIN STATE OFFICERS. 

450. Particular records; copies. All deeds, contracts of sale, leases, and other documents or copies of same conveying land or an interest therein to the state, except for highway rights of way, leases and conveyances, shall be filed in the office of the secretary of state. All deeds, contracts of sale, leases, and other documents conveying land or an interest in land from the state as grantor, shall be made out in duplicate by the authorized agent of the state. The original shall be delivered to the grantee and the duplicate copy, so marked, shall be filed in the office of the secretary of state. The secretary shall also record the state treasurer's bonds and other documents required to be recorded in his office and give copies of the same upon tender of his legal fees. 

1953, No. 110. P. L. § 406. 

R. L. § 159. G. S. 8, § 29. 

G. L. § 377. P. S. § 294. V. S. § 222. 

R. S. 8, § 24. R. 1797, p. 382. 

489. Location. The following officers, commissioners, commissions, bureau, boards and departments shall have their offices in Montpelier in quarters to be designated from time to time by the governor, as and when he shall direct: The governor, state treasurer, secretary of state, auditor of accounts, attorney general, adjutant general, social welfare, institutions and corrections, highways, agriculture, education, public service, motor vehicles, industrial relations, purchasing agent, taxes, banking and insurance, Vermont aeronautics commission, Vermont unemployment compensation commission, Vermont development commission, liquor control board, and legislative reference bureau. 

1947, No. 202, § 493. P. L. § 451. 1923, No. 7, § 35. G. L. § 662. 1917, No. 35, § 1. 

CHAPTER 27. ADMINISTRATIVE DEPARTMENTS. 

General Provisions. 

492. Departments created. The following administrative departments are hereby created, through the instrumentality of which the governor, under the constitution, shall exercise such functions as are in this chapter assigned to each department, respectively:

I. The department of highways, which shall be administered by the state highway board and in which shall be a commissioner of highways. 

1947, Nos. 163, 187. 1945, No. 5, § 7. 1943, No. 9, § 2. 1939, No. 11, §§, 1, 2.

114 Vt. 316. 

504. Department of highways; powers. As herein or elsewhere specified, the department of highways shall have power to supervise and direct the execution of all laws relating to highways, and the members of such board shall, by virtue of their office, be toll bridge commissioners. 

P. L. § 473. 1933, No. 157, § 414. 1923, No. 7, § 32. 

TITLE 8. PUBLIC REVENUE. 

CHAPTER 60. GASOLINE TAX 

1257. Rate of tax; penalty. In all cases not exempt from such tax under the laws of the United States, at the time of filing such report, each distributor shall pay to the commissioner a tax of five cents per gallon upon each gallon of such motor fuel sold by the distributor. The distributor shall collect such tax from the dealer and the dealer from the consumer. The distributor shall also pay to the commissioner a tax of five cents per gallon upon each gallon of such motor fuel used within the state by him. When a distributor neglects or refuses to make 801154

the statement or return herein required, an addition of five per cent of the amount of the tax shall be added and collected as a part of the tax due. 

Distribution of revenue. The revenue obtained by virtue of a one-half cent increase in the tax on motor fuel, as provided by section 2 hereof, shall be allocated and used solely for the purposes and in the manner set forth in sections 

4 and 5 of this act.

Basis of distribution. There is hereby annually appropriated a sum equal to the one-half cent tax upon each gallon of motor fuel, as provided in section 2 hereof, which shall be apportioned to the several municipalities upon a basis of the ratio of the total combined state aid and town highway mileage in each municipality as compared with the total combined state aid and town highway mileage of the whole state, excluding unorganized towns and gores. The mileage used for this purpose shall be that measured and certified to the state highway board as provided in sections 4922 and 4923 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

Use of funds; approval. The expenditure by each municipality of its proportionate share of the sum or sums provided in section 4 hereof shall be for construction on either the state aid or town highway system as determined by a committee composed of the selectmen and road commissioners, city council or trustees, as the case may be, and the district highway commissioner as representative of the state highway board. The plans and specifications of all construction under this act shall be approved by the district highway commissioner. Application for the proposed construction shall be made to the board on forms provided by the board in such detail as it may require. 

Report; payment. A report of the work done in accordance with such application shall be made upon forms furnished by the state highway board and in such detail as such board may require. Such report shall be subscribed and sworn to, shall be examined and approved by the district highway commissioner of the district and forwarded to the board. The board shall examine the report and issue a certificate on the auditor of accounts in favor of such municipality for such amount as it finds due. The auditor shall issue his warrant in payment of such certificate.

Construction. It is hereby declared to be the intent of this legislature that the sum collected by virtue of the one-half cent increase in the tax on motor fuel shall be apportioned and disbursed in accordance with the provisions of this act, notwithstanding the provisions of any act or acts, statutes or laws to the contrary. 

1949, No. 35. 1947, No. 25, § 1. P. L. § 1228. 1933, No. 28. 

1931, No. 14, § 1. 1929, No. 30, § 5. 108 Vt. 510. 

TITLE 15. MUNICIPAL CORPORATIONS. 

CHAPTER 163. TOWNS. 

Town Meetings. 

3499. Qualifications of voters.

A male or female citizen, twenty-one years of age, who has taken the freeman's oath and whose list, including poll, is taken in a town at the annual assessment preceding a town meeting, and whose poll and old age assistance taxes due such town, were paid prior to January 1 preceding such town meeting, or who has attained the age of twenty-one years subsequent to the last annual assessment and resided in the town since April 1 last preceding, or who is exempt from taxation for any cause, shall be a voter in town meeting while residing in such town. Such citizen, if a resident of an incorporated village within such town, shall not vote in town meeting for town road commissioner unless at least fifteen percent of the last highway tax of such incorporated village has been paid to the town treasurer to be expended upon the highways of the town outside

of such incorporated village. A person qualified to vote in town meeting under the provisions of this section shall be qualified to vote in all other municipal meetings.

Town Officers. 

3509. Qualification; election. At the annual meeting, a town shall choose from among the legally qualified voters thereof the following town officers, who shall serve until the next annual meeting and until others are chosen, unless otherwise provided by law or herein specified: 

IV. One selectman for a term of three years who shall be elected by ballot; 

V. One or two road commissioners who shall be elected by ballot, unless appointed by the selectmen as hereinafter provided; 

XVI. One or more patrolmen to patrol town highways under the direction of the selectmen, if the town so orders.

3513. Road commissioners. At the annual meeting, each town shall vote whether or not the selectmen shall be empowered and authorized to appoint one or two road commissioners, the number to be determined by the town. An article enabling a town so to empower and authorize the selectmen shall be inserted in the warning for such meeting.

3515. Money for town expenses to be voted sepcifically. A town shall vote such sums of money as it deems necessary for the interest of its inhabitants

and for the prosecution and defense of the common rights. It shall express in its vote the specific sum or rate per cent on a dollar of the grand list to be appropriated for laying out and repairing highways and for other necessary town expenses.

P. S. § 3531. 

3521. Incompatible offices. An auditor shall not be town clerk, town treasurer, selectman, first constable, collector of taxes, overseer of the poor, road commissioner or town district school director, nor shall any person assisting any of the foregoing officers in the discharge of their official duties be eligible to hold office as auditor. The husband or wife of such officials shall be ineligible to hold the office of auditor. A selectman shall not be first constable, collector of taxes, town treasurer, lister, auditor or town agent. A town treasurer shall not be constable or collector of taxes. And in towns whose population exceeds fifteen hundred, a town clerk shall not be first constable or collector of taxes.

3522. Same; exceptions. The preceding section shall not apply to towns having not more than twenty-five legal voters, but in such towns an auditor shall not audit his own accounts kept and rendered in some other official capacity nor shall the husband or wife of any town official audit such official accounts.

3524. Separate ballot for road commissioner, when. When a town has an official ballot, and has voters therein disqualified to vote for town road commissioner under section 3499, the names of candidates for town road commissioner shall be printed on a separate ballot and shall not appear on the ballot with other town officers. Such ballots for town road commissioner shall be deposited in a separate box to be furnished by the proper town officers. 

P. L. § 3447. G. L. § 3932. P. S. § 3432. 1904, No. 85, § 2. 

3526. Vacancy. When a town officer resigns his office, or has been removed therefrom, or dies, or becomes insane or removes from town, or when a town officer holding office for a term longer than one year ceases to be a legal voter at town meeting by reason of failure to pay his poll tax prior to January 1, such office shall become vacant. 

1949, No. 25. 1947, No. 202, § 3548. 1935, No. 60, § 1. P. L. § 3449. 1933, No. 157, § 3219. 

3527. Vacancies. A town at a special meeting may fill a vacancy in a town office.

P. L. § 3450. G. L. § 3935. P. S. § 3435. V. S. § 2987. R. L. § 2667. 1864, No. 17. 

G. S. 15, § 21. R. S. 13, § 20. R. 1797, p. 290, § 13. 1793, p. 48. R. 1787, p. 33. 

3528. Vacancies in town offices; filling. When a vacancy occurs in any town office, the selectmen forthwith by appointment in writing shall fill such vacancy until an election is had; except that in the event of more than one vacancy on the board of selectmen at the same time, such vacancies shall be filled by a special town meeting called for that purpose. Such appointment shall be filed by them in the office of the town clerk and duly recorded by him in the book of town records.

3533. Bonds; requirements. Before the school directors, constable, road commissioner, collector of taxes, treasurer, clerk and overseer of the poor enter upon the duties of their offices, the selectmen shall require each to give a bond conditioned for the faithful performance of his duties; the school directors, to the town school district; the other named officers, to the town. The treasurer and collector shall also be required to give a bond to the town school district for like purpose. 

All such bonds shall be in sufficient sums and with sufficient sureties as prescribed and approved by the selectmen. If the selectmen at any time consider a bond of any such officer to be insufficient, they may require, by written order, such officer to give an additional bond in such sum as they deem necessary. If an officer, required, neglects for ten days after such request to give such original or additional bond, his office shall be vacant. A bond furnished pursuant to the provisions of this section shall not be valid if signed by any other officer of the same municipality as surety thereon.

3558. Town or village reports. The clerk of a municipality shall supply annually each library in such municipality with two copies of the municipal report, upon its publication. He shall also mail to the state library two copies thereof, and one copy each to the secretary of state, commissioner of taxes, highway board, secretary of the state board of health, commissioner of social welfare, auditor of accounts and board of education. Officers making such reports shall supply the clerk of such municipality with the printed copies necessary for him to comply with the provisions of this and the following section.

1894, No. 39, § 4. 

3564. Orders for land damages or damages for injuries. The selectmen may draw orders on the town treasurer for damages for lands or property taken or improved for a highway or other public use, or for damages settled by agreement or awarded for injuries sustained from the insufficiency of a highway. 

P. L. § 3487. G. L. § 3972. P. S. § 3470. V. S. § 3019. R. L. § 2694. G .S. 15, § 51. 

R. S. 13, § 46. 

Town Accounts. 

3572. Tax levies; how kept. The town treasurer upon receiving from the selectmen a town tax bill under the provisions of section 839 or the receipt of the tax collector for such bill under the provisions of section 3562 shall credit the town highway department and the town school district, subject, however, to the provision of section 4464 as to school districts, each with the gross sum of the levy provided for such department and district. The balance of such levy shall be credited to the general fund. 

1947, No. 202, § 3594. P. L. § 3494. 1933, No. 43, § 2. 

CHAPTER 164. UNORGANIZED TOWNS AND GORES. 

Supervisors. 3664. Appointment. Biennially, on February 1, to hold office for two years therefrom, the governor shall appoint and commission one supervisor for the unorganized towns and gores in each county who shall give a bond to the state. Such supervisor shall not be an appraiser.

P. L. § 3579. 1933, No. 157, § 3334. G. L. §§ 859, 952. 

1912, No. 42, §§ 20, 21. 1910, No. 38, §§ 8, 10. P. S. §§ 1888, No. 7, § 1. R. L. §§ 290, 442. 1865, No. 21, § 1. 

1915, No. 32, § 5. 

522, 678. V. S. §§ 385, 539. 

1862, No. 18, §§ 1, 4. 

60 Vt. 79.

3667. Taxes expended; how. Upon allowance of the accounts of supervisors and appraisers for unorganized towns and gores, the auditor of accounts shall certify forthwith the amount thereof as allowed to the state treasurer and the balance, if any, of the moneys received from any supervisor, after deducting the amount of the county tax, if any. The amount of such supervisors' and appraisers' accounts, so certified, shall be used for the laying out, construction and maintenance of highways and bridges in the unorganized towns and gores for which such supervisor is appointed, to be expended by and under the direction of the state highway board, in the same manner as state highway appropriations. Such part of such money as remains unexpended for more than one year may be used for like purposes and expended in a like manner in towns adjoining such unorganized towns and gores.

P. L. § 3582. G. L. § 959. 1917, No. 48, § 5. 1912, No. 42, § 37. 

3669. Supervisor; general duties. Such supervisor shall act as selectman in matters of road encroachment, as overseer of the poor, as school director and truant officer, as constable, as collector of taxes and as town clerk in the matter of licensing dogs.

CHAPTER 166. TOWN MANAGERS. 

Definition. 

3675. Town construed; officers. The word "town" as used in this chapter may be construed to include a city or an incorporated village. Where powers are given to or duties imposed upon selectmen with reference to a town, the trustees of a village and mayor and board of aldermen of a city shall have the same powers and perform like duties in relation to their respective village or city. 

P. L. § 3590. 1931, No. 51, § 1. 1931, No. 52, § 1. G. L. § 4053. 1917, No. 104, § 1. 

111 Vt. 49. Manager. 

3676. Appointment; union of towns. The selectmen of towns adopting the provisions of this chapter shall forthwith appoint a general town manager, who may or may not be a resident of the town for which he is appointed. Two or more towns may vote to form a union to employ the same manager. 

1943, No. 39, § 1. P. L. § 3591. G. L. § 4054. 1917, No. 104, § 2. 

3680. Powers and duties in particular. The manager shall have authority and it shall be his duty:

P. L. § 3595. 1933, No. 157, § 3350. 1921, No. 103, § 1. G. L. § 4058. 1917, No. 104, § 4. 

100 Vt. 75.

II. 

To perform all duties now conferred by law upon the selectmen, except that he shall not prepare tax bills, sign orders on the general fund of the town, other than orders for poor relief, call special or annual town meetings, lay out highways, establish and lay out public parks, make assessments, award damages, act as member of the board of civil authority, nor make appointments to fill vacancies which the selectmen are now authorized by law to fill; but he shall, in all matters herein excepted, render the selectmen such assistance as they shall require; 

V. To perform all the duties now conferred by law upon the road commissioner of the town, including the signing of orders; provided, however, that when an in-

corporated village lies within the territorial limits of a town which is operating under a town manager, and such village fails to pay to such town for expenditure on the roads of the town outside the village, at least fifteen per cent of the last highway tax levied in such village, the legal voters residing in such town, outside such village, may elect one or two road commissioners who shall have and exercise all powers of road commissioner within that part of such town as lies outside such village.

CHAPTER 169. CITIES AND VILLAGES. 

General Powers of Villages. 

3737. By-laws. An incorporated village may enact such by-laws and regulations as are expedient, not inconsistent with law, particularly such as relate to streets, sidewalks, lanes, commons, shade and ornamental trees and lights thereon, slaughter-houses and nuisances, police protection, restraint of animals, erection and regulation of buildings and hay scales, the preservation of buildings, with the right of directing alterations in stoves, fireplaces, and causes from which danger from fire may be apprehended, fire engines and other apparatus necessary for the extinguishment of fire, establishment and regulation of fire companies and to the manufacture and safekeeping of ashes, explosives and combustibles.

CHAPTER 170. MISCELLANEOUS PROVISIONS COMMON TO TOWNS, CITIES AND INCORPORATED VILLAGES. 

Abatement of Public Nuisances. 

3765. Injunctions. The selectmen of a town in the name and behalf of such town or the town school district therein, as the case may be, and the trustees of an incorporated village, in the name and behalf of such village, may prefer a bill in equity for relief by injunction for the abatement of public nuisances. The court of chancery shall have jurisdiction of such actions. 

P. L. § 3668. G. L. § 4135. 1917, No. 254, § 4069. P. S. § 3607. 1898, No. 154, § 1. 

100 Vt. 37. 111 Vt. 49. 

Motor Vehicle Junk Yards. 

3766. Definition. "Motor vehicle junk business" or "motor vehicle junk yards" shall include any business and any place of storage or deposit, whether in connection with another business or not, where five or more unregistered, old or second-hand motor vehicles are displayed to the public view, for the purpose of resale of used parts of motor vehicles or old iron, metal, glass or other waste or discarded material which has been part of any motor vehicle, the sum of which parts or material shall be equal in bulk to five or more motor vehicles. 

P. L. § 3669. 1931, No. 88, § 1. 

113 Vt. 341. 

3767. Certificate of approval. A person shall not establish or enlarge a motor vehicle junk yard or business unless he shall first obtain a certificate of approval of the location to be used therefor from the legislative body wherein such location shall be situated.

P. L. § 3670. 1933, No. 157, § 3441. 1931, No. 88, § 2. 

113 Vt. 341. 

3768. Hearing; award; fees. Upon receipt of an application for such certificate of approval, the legislative body shall assign a hearing thereon to be held not less than two nor more than four weeks from the date thereof within the municipality in which such proposed location is situated. Notice of hearing shall be given such applicant and by publication once in a newspaper having a circulation within such municipality not less than seven days before the date of such hearing. A certificate of approval shall not be issued unless such legislative body shall find, after such hearing, that no unreasonable depreciation of surrounding property would ensue from the establishment or enlargement of such motor vehicle junk yard or business and that, in their judgment, the best interests of the community require the operation of such yard or business at the location designated. In awarding such certificate, the legislative body shall take into account the proximity of schools, churches or other places of public gatherings, the sufficiency in number of other similar places in the vicinity and the suitability of the applicant to receive such certificate of approval. Each applicant shall pay the costs of publication and expenses of such hearing to the treasurer of the municipality. 

P. L. § 3671. 1933, No. 157, § 3442. 1931, No. 88, § 3. 

113 Vt. 341. 

3769. Appeal. A person aggrieved by the action taken pursuant to the provisions of the preceding section may appeal to the county court of the county within which the municipality is located in the same manner as is provided for appeals from the decisions of selectmen in laying out and discontinuing highways. Upon such appeal, the court shall make such order in relation to the action appealed from as it may deem equitable. 

P. L. § 3672. 1933, No. 157, § 3443. 1931, No. 88, § 4. 

113 Vt. 341. 

3770. General prohibition. A motor vehicle junk yard shall not be located alongside or in plain view of a public traveled highway. Any junk yard so located is hereby declared to be a nuisance and the same may be abated on complaint of any prosecuting officer.

P. L. § 3673. 1933, No 157, § 3444. 1931, No. 88, § 5. 

113 Vt. 341.

3771. Penalty. A person who violates any provision of section 3767 shall be fined not more than $100.00 nor less than $10.00. 

P. L. § 3674. 1933, No. 157, § 3445. 1931, No. 88, § 6. 

Dumping Rubbish Adjacent to Highways. 

3772. Throwing, depositing and dumping refuse, penalty. A person shall not throw, dump or deposit or cause to be thrown, dumped or deposited bottles, glass, crockery, cans, scrap metal, junk, paper, garbage, old automobiles or parts thereof, refuse of whatever nature or any noxious thing on the public highway or within the limits of the highway right-of-way, or on lands or waters adjoining a public highway, without the consent of the owners of such land or waters; nor shal a person throw, dump or deposit or cause to be thrown, dumped or deposited such refuse or noxious things over the sides or approaches of any bridge or culvert into any stream or watercourse. A person who violates a provision of this section shall be fined not more than $50.00 nor less than $10.00. 

1951, No. 73. 1937, No. 110, § 1. 

Warning Signs. 

3776. At schoolhouses. There shall be erected by the selectmen of each town on all public highways near a schoolhouse used for school purposes warning signs about three feet in length by two feet in width, which shall bear the following notice: "Schoolhouse Zone." P. L. § 3681. G. L. § 4141. 1917, No. 137, § 2. 

3777. Duties of state highway board. The state highway board is hereby directed to procure a sufficient number of signs for the use of the several municipalities in carrying out the provisions of the preceding section. It shall furnish such signs to the municipalities upon such terms as may be agreed upon by it and such municipalities. The expense incurred by the board in carrying out the provisions of this section shall be paid from the highway appropriations. 

P. L. § 3682. 1933, No. 157, § 3456. G. L. § 4142. 1917, No. 137, § 3. 1915, No. 114, § 2.

3779. Tree warden's duties. The public shade trees, except those in public parks and places under the control of park commissioners, shall be under the care and control of the tree warden, who shall expend all money appropriated for planting and maintaining public shade trees and for exterminating insect pests. He shall enforce all regulations and 'aws relating to public shade trees. Shade trees within the limits of a public way or place shall be deemed public shade trees.

CHAPTER 175. FENCES AND FENCE VIEWERS. 

Maintenance of Fences. 

3890. Sufficient fences. Fences four and one-half feet high, in good repair and so constructed as to prevent the escape of sheep, and any natural barrier equivalent to such fence shall be deemed sufficient, except fences on the sides of highways which the owners of land are not bound to make and maintain. Occupied land bordering upon highways shall be deemed to be the enclosure of the owner or occupant. A person driving cattle, sheep, swine or other stock upon a highway, and exercising reasonable care, shall not be liable for damages by reason of the escape of such animals into an enclosure adjoinining the highway, unless the fence between the highway and the enclosure is a sufficient fence within the meaning of this section. 

1947, No. 202, § 3912. P. L. § 3765. G. L. § 4764. P. S. § 4134. 

V. S. § 3567. R. L. § 3178. G. S. 102, § 1. 1860, No. 30. 1853, No.

GENERAL CEMETERY ACT. 

4023. Public use of cemetery restricted. A public highway or railroad shall not be laid through such burial ground without the consent of the town, association or the general assembly, and no portion of such burial ground shall be taken for public use without special authority from the general assembly.

TITLE 17. STATE LIBRARY AND HISTORICAL SOCIETY. 

CHAPTER 186. HISTORIC SITES COMMISSION. 

4169. Commission created, members. A historic sites commission is hereby established composed of the director of the Vermont Historical Society, the director of the Vermont Development Commission, and a person appointed biennially by the governor. The commissioner of highways and the state forester, who shall serve as executive secretary, shall cooperate with and assist the commission, at its request and with the approval of the governor, in carrying out the duties of the commission. The governor shall designate the chairman of the commission. 

1947, No. 53, § 1. 

TITLE 20. DEPARTMENT OF HIGHWAYS. 

CHAPTER 233. HIGHWAY BOARD AND COMMISSIONER. 

4903. Definitions.

The highways of the state shall be known and classified as follows: 

I. "State Highways" are those highways exclusively in charge of the state highway board;

II. "State aid highways" are those highways controlled by the towns and selected as the most important highways in each town, and for the purpose of securing as far as possible trunk lines from town to town, and are eligible to receive under state supervision state aid funds for construction and maintenance; 

114 Vt. 41. 

III. "Town highways" are those highways exclusively controlled by the towns and over which the state highway board only supervises the expenditure of the state appropriations thereon.*

P. L. § 4643. 1933, No. 157, § 4363. 1927, No. 63, § 1. 

4904. State highway board; members, appointment, salary. The state highway board shall consist of three persons who shall be appointed by the governor with the advice and consent of the senate. The term of each member shall be six years. Biennially, in the month of February, with the advice and consent of the senate, the governor shall appoint a person as a member of such board for the term of six years, whose term of office shall commence March 1 of the year in which such appointment is made. The governor shall biennially designate a member of such board to be its chairman. Each member of the board shall receive $10.00 a day for service rendered at meetings of the board and as otherwise provided and in addition actual expenses when away from home. Whenever the total cost to the state for compensation received by the members of the board in any fiscal year shall have reached the sum of $3,000.00, no further cost or expense for such board shall be incurred during such fiscal year, except with the express approval of the governor. 

1943, No. 8, § 1. 1943, No. 72, § 1. P. L. § 4644. 1933, No. 157, § 4364. 1923, No. 76, § 1. 

1923, No. 7, § 2, Par. 4. 

4905. Commissioner of highways, appointment, duties. Annually, with the approval of the governor, the board shall appoint a commissioner of highways who shall be a person of special training and experience in the direction and administration of highway work, and who shall be the chief executive officer of the board. He shall hold his office for one year and until his successor is appointed and qualified. He shall prepare and make to the board reports at such times and on such subjects and in such detail as he may deem necessary in the proper discharge of his duties, or as the board may require, and from time to time shall recommend *But see 4913.

such action as in his judgment will best further the highway interests and work of the state.

P. L. § 4645. 1933, No. 157, § 4365. 1923, No. 76, § 2. 

4906. Meetings, quorum. The board shall hold in each year at least four regular meetings, and such special meetings as the performance of its duties may require, at times and places designated in notices therefor by the chairman, who shall call special meetings upon his own motion and upon written request of any member. Any two members of the board shall constitute a quorum and no services of the members of the board shall be considered rendered under the provisions of section 4904 unless a quorum is present; provided however, that services otherwise rendered by one or more members of the board at the direction of the board shall be compensated for under the provisions of section 4904. 

1943, No. 72, § 2. P. L. § 4646. 1933, No. 157, § 4366. 1921, No. 123, § 2. 

4907. Chief engineer, assistants. With the approval of the governor, the board shall employ a chief engineer and such scientific expert, technical, clerical and other assistants upon such terms and with such compensation, including traveling and other expenses while away from home on official business, as it shall deem necessary and proper to the efficient conduct of its work and discharge of its duties, and all such persons so employed by the board shall be subject to the state classification system for state employees. The board may contract, on such terms as it deems to be for the best interests of the state, for the construction, repair or maintenance of any bridge or highway; for the use of any machinery or equipment either with or without operators or drivers; for the operation, repair, maintenance or storage of any state-owned plants, machinery or equipment; for professional engineering services, inspection of work or materials, diving services, mapping services, photographic services including aerial photography or surveys, and any other service, with or without equipment, in connection with the planning, construction and maintenance of highways and bridges. Services rendered by any person incident to such contracts shall not constitute employment within the meaning of section 513 or within the provisions of any state retirement system. 

A highway or bridge contract of more than $25,000.00 shall be advertised and let to the lowest qualified bidder. 

1953, No. 187. P. L. § 4647. 1933, No. 157, § 4367. 1923, No. 76, § 3. 

4908. State engineer. The highway board by virtue of its office shall act as state engineer and carry out by its chief engineer such engineering work for state officials, boards, departments and commissions as the governor may direct. 

P. L. § 4648. 1933, No. 157, § 4368. 1923, No. 76, § 3. 1923, No. 7, § 32, Par. 2. 

G. L. §§ 399, 400. 1917, No. 128, §§ 1, 2. 

4909. General duties. The state highway board shall supervise and manage the public highway systems of the state, except where otherwise specifically provided by law; and, through its commissioner of highways, shall: 

I. Control and direct the use and expenditure of all moneys available for the use of the board, appropriated by the state or apportioned to towns or incorporated villages for constructive highway improvements, and prepare and use a budget for such expenditures;

II. Annually, or oftener as occasion arises, make and promulgate rules and regulations for the execution of the duties and powers by law imposed and conferred upon it, and all officers and employees under its supervision and control, and such rules and regulations, plans, specifications and advice as in its judgment will best aid the town road commissioners in the discharge of their duties and secure the best results from the expenditure of state funds in the construction and improvement of the highways of the state;

III. Control and direct the expenditure of all special appropriations of state funds to towns for the benefit of highways made by the legislature or by orders of court;* *But see 4913.

IV. Require any contractor or contractors, employed in any project of the highway board for constructive highway improvement, to file in the office of the commissioner of highways a good and sufficient surety bond to the state of Vermont, executed by a surety company duly authorized to transact business in this state in such sum as the board shall direct, conditioned for the compliance by such contractor or contractors and their agents and servants, with all matters and things set forth and specified to be by such principal kept, done and performed at the time and in the manner in the contract between the highway board and the contractor or contractors specified, and to pay over, make good and reimburse the state of Vermont, all loss or losses and damage or damages which the state of Vermont may sustain by reason of failure or default on the part of the contractor or contractors. 

The state highway board is authorized to require any other condition in such bond that from time to time be necessary; may

V. Require any contractor or contractors employed in any project of the highway board for constructive highway improvement, to file an additional surety bond to the commissioner of highways and his successor in office, for the benefit of labor and materialmen, executed by a surety company duly authorized to transact business in this state, in such sum as the board shall direct, conditioned for the payment, settlement, liquidation and discharge of the claims of all creditors of such contractor for material, merchandise, labor, rent, hire of vehicles, steam shovels, rollers, concrete mixers, tools, and other appliances, used or employed in carrying out the terms of the contract between such contractor and the state of Vermont and further conditioned for the payment of taxes, both state and municipal, and contributions to the Vermont unemployment compensation commission, accruing during the term of performance of such contract; provided however, that in order to obtain the benefit of such security, the claimant shall file with the commissioner of highways a sworn statement of his claim, within ninety days after the claimant ceases to perform labor or furnish labor, materials, appliances and equipment as aforesaid or within ninety days from the time such taxes or contributions to the Vermont unemployment compensation commission are due and payable and, within one year after the filing of such claim, shall bring a petition in the county court in the name of the commissioner of highways, with notice and summons to the principal, surety and the commissioner of highways, to enforce such claim, or intervene in a petition already filed. (a). Such petition shall be brought in the county where the creditor resides, and, if a nonresident, in the county of Washington, except that petitions to intervene shall be brought in any county wherein a petition under this section is already pending. (b). Such petition shall contain allegations of the nature and subject matter of the claim or contract or indebtedness relied upon, of the execution and delivery of the bond, and of the facts showing compliance by the claimant with the provisions of this section relative to the filing of such claim with the commissioner of highways. Subsequent pleadings may thereafter be filed by any party in interest for the purpose of formulating issues under the direction of the court. Any other person who has filed a claim as herein provided may intervene in a petition already instituted by applying to the court to which such first petition is returnable. In such case, before making final disposition of such petition, the court shall examine all claims which may arise under this section and which have been duly filed with the commissioner of highways in accordance herewith, and, after notice to all creditors who have filed claims as herein provided, determine the respective amounts due such claimants and their rights to participate in the security and apply the security to the claimants entitled thereto. Any claimant who has been granted permission to intervene shall have the right to appear at hearings on any petition and may object, except to, or appeal from any ruling or decision adversely affecting his claim. 

1947, No. 202, § 4854. 1939, No. 103. P. L. § 4649. 1921, No. 123, § 3. G. L. § 4583. 

P. S. § 4003. 1906, No. 111, § 2. 1898, No. 65, § 2.

4910. Biennial report. The state highway board shall make a report to the general assembly in detail of its acts and business for the biennial period ending on June 30 next preceding. Such report shall also show the progress and condition of all highway work begun during such period, the source, use made of, and amount of all money expended by or under its control or supervision and recommendations for such action as the experience and judgment of the board may suggest. 

P. L. § 4650. 1921, No. 123, § 7. 

4911. District engineers. The board shall divide the state into highway districts in such manner and in such number, not exceeding twelve, as it may determine, and shall employ a district engineer for each such highway district.

SECTION 1. Highway fund. All fees received by the commissioner of motor vehicles as head of the department of motor vehicles, all revenues derived from the tax on motor fuel, the revenue derived from town assessments under section 4967, the moneys received by the highway department for rental of machinery and equipment, for services rendered and from all other sources, and funds received from municipalities and public utilities cooperating with the highway board in any highway or grade crossing work shall be paid into the state treasury and shall be known as the highway fund. The state highway board shall, unless otherwise provided, and subject to the provisions of No. 35 of the Acts of 1949, control and direct the expenditure of the portion of this fund appropriated by the legislature for state highways, state aid highways, town highways, and the highway department. Funds received from municipalities and public utilities cooperating with the highway board in any highway or grade crossing work are hereby appropriated for the work or project for which such funds are contributed. 

SEC. 2. Town Highways. Construction and Maintenance. The appro priation for town highways shall be available to towns and incorporated villages for construction and maintenance on town highways and bridges, and so much thereof as may be necessary shall be apportioned by the highway board on the basis of $150.00 per mile for each mile of town highway to those towns and incorporated villages expending from their own funds on such highways, exclusive of winter maintenance, a sum aggregating not less than $25.00 per mile of such highways in the respective towns and villages as certified under oath by the selectmen or trustees thereof on forms furnished by the highway board and containing information in respect thereto as required by such board. The expenditure of each town's apportionment shall be laid out and expended under the charge of a committee consisting of the road commissioner, selectmen or trustees and the district engineer having jurisdiction in such town as a representative of the state highway board. 

Upon written application of the selectmen or trustees of a town or village and the recommendation of the district engineer, all or a portion of such town's or village's apportionment may be used for the improvement of state aid highways in such town or village. Not more than one-third the apportionment to a town or

village may be used for winter maintenance. Any balance of the town highway appropriation may be used by the highway board to assist towns and villages in the construction, repair and maintenance of town highways and bridges. 

No. 264, 1953.

SEC. 3. Wherever the words "maintenance fund" appear in the Vermont 

Statutes, Revision of 1947, and amendments thereto, the words "highway fund" shall be substituted therefor. 

SEC. 4. State aid highways; maintenance. The appropriation for state aid highway maintenance shall be used to assist towns and villages in the maintenance of their state aid highways, which shall include patrol, surface treatment, resurfacing, dust laying, winter maintenance, traffic control devices, replacement, repair and any other items of maintenance work deemed necessary by the highway board. The board shall make such rules and regulations for assisting towns and villages in the maintenance of state aid highways and on such terms as they shall deem for the best interests of the state. 

SEC. 6. Wherever in the revised statutes of 1947 or the amendments thereto and the acts of 1949, the words "district commissioners" or "district highway commissioners" occur they are hereby amended to read "district engineer". 

4915. Funds for state aid highways, how used. The moneys annually available for state aid highways and their supervision shall be expended for improvement of such highways in accordance with the provisions of this title.

Such moneys shall be expended under the supervision and direction of the highway board.

4918. Expenditure of money; state aid highways; duties of board. All moneys appropriated, unless otherwise provided, under the provisions of this chapter shall be expended for the purpose of permanently improving the main and most important highways in each town which shall be selected by the selectmen and town road commissioners, subject to the approval of the highway board. Such highways shall be known as state aid highways. As far as possible they shall be selected with the purpose of securing trunk lines of improved highways from town to town and to the important local railroad stations. Towns shall keep such state aid highways, together with all highways that have been improved at state expense, except those in the state highway system, in good repair at all seasons of the year. A town shall not be entitled to receive money from the state in any year until it has complied with the provisions of this chapter. All moneys appropriated or apportioned to towns by this chapter, unless otherwise provided, shall be laid out and expended under the direct supervision of such board. The location of each year's work, the plans of construction and the foreman to take charge of the work shall

be determined by such board, but the selectmen and road commissioner shall be consulted in regard to such plans and location of work. The provisions of this section in regard to the selection and care of highways and expenditure of state money shall apply to incorporated villages in expending their apportionment of state highway appropriations. 

1947, No. 202, § 4863. P. L. § 4657. 1933, No. 157, § 4375. G. L. § 4586. 1912, No. 145, § 1. 

1908, No. 97, § 2. P. S. § 4005. 1906, No. 111, § 4. 1902, No. 62, § 1. 1898, No. 65, § 3. 

V. S. §§ 3440, 3449. 1892, No. 56, §§ 9, 13. 106 Vt. 213. 

4919. Reports. Upon forms furnished by the board, a report of such work shall be made in detail as the board may require. Such report shall be subscribed and sworn to, shall be examined and approved by the district commissioner and forwarded to the board, which shall examine the same and issue a certificate on the auditor of accounts in favor of such municipality for the whole or such portion of its apportionment of state highway appropriations as it is satisfied has been expended in permanent work as provided by this title. 

1947, No. 202, § 4864. P. L. § 4658. G. L. § 4587. 1917, No. 254, § 4494. P. S. § 4006. 

1906, No. 111, § 5. 1902, No. 62, § 1. 1898, No. 65, § 3. V. S. § 3443. 1892, No. 56, § 12. 

4920. Highway tax; use for maintenance. At any annual meeting a town may vote any part of its highway tax to be used for the maintenance of such highways in such town as are not designated by the highway board for improvement in accordance with the provisions of this chapter. 

P. L. § 4660. 1919, No. 114, § 1. 

4921. Change of selection. The selectmen of each town with the approval of the highway board, as occasion arises, may make additions to or take from such state aid highways.

P. L. § 4661. 1927, No. 63, §2. 

4922. Measurement of highways, report. Annually, on or before March 

1st, the selectmen shall file with the town clerk a sworn statement of the description and measurements of all new highways built and all highways discontinued during the year previous thereto, not including pent roads and trails. Highways which are not passable for motor trucks during the summer season shall be treated as discontinued under this section. 

1951, No. 108, § 9. P. L. § 4662. 1931, No. 86, § 2. 

4923. Certificate, duties of town clerk. The certificate showing the highway mileage of towns and villages under the preceding section shall be forthwith. recorded in the office of the town clerk, who within five days shall forward a certified copy thereof to the state highway board. If the certified copy of the sworn statement for a town called for by this section fails to reach the state highway board on March 15th, then the board shall use the mileage shown on the last previous certificate properly filed. 

1951, No. 108, § 10. 1935, No. 105, § 2. P. L. § 4664. 1933, No. 157, § 4382.

Bridges on State Aid and Town Highways. 

4927. Bridge engineer, expense. When it becomes necessary to repair, build or rebuild a bridge or its abutments on a state aid or town highway, the selectmen of the town may apply to the highway board for an engineer to make

plans and give advice in regard to location and plan of construction for such repairs, building and rebuilding, and to supervise the work. On receipt of such application, the board shall furnish an engineer. The expense incurred for such engineering shall be paid on the certificate of the board, and the auditor of accounts shall issue his warrant therefor. 

1939, No. 105, § 1. 1937, No. 104, § 1. P. L. § 4668. G. L. § 4548. 1915, No. 129, § 1. 

4928. State aid. When the selectmen of a town repair, build or rebuild bridges wholly or partially within this state on a state aid highway of a span of not less than four feet, they may apply to the highway board for aid. If the repairs, building, or rebuilding of such bridge have been made according to the plans and specifications of such board, and upon the receipt of a sworn statement from the selectmen, on such forms as it may furnish, showing the cost of repairing, building or rebuilding such bridge and such other facts as it may require, such board shall issue a certificate in favor of the town in which such bridge has been repaired, built or rebuilt for a sum equal to four-fifths of the cost of the repairing, building or rebuilding of such bridge, unless the remaining one-fifth cost to be paid by such town shall exceed $1.00 on the grand list of such town in any one year, in which case the board shall include such excess in the certificate issued to the town. The highway board may apportion seventy-five per cent of the biennial appropriation for bridge work under this section during the first year of the biennial period, and shall not transfer any of such appropriation to other funds administered by the department until the selectmen of the towns having insufficient bridges on state aid highways have been notified of the intention to make such transfer and have failed, within a reasonable time thereafter, to apply for aid under this section. In all cases where the traffic demands it, the board may require that bridges be constructed to bear a load of not less than fifteen tons and to be not less than twenty feet in width, but, in case the circumstances warrant, these requirements may be varied as may be agreed upon between the board and selectmen. 

1941, No. 86. 

1931, No. 64.

G. L. § 4549. 

1937, No. 104, § 2. 1935, No. 107, § 1. P. L. § 4669. 1933, No. 73. 

1927, No. 64. 1925, No. 67. 1921, No. 122, § 1. 1919, No. 112, § 1. 

1917, No. 120, § 1. 1915, No. 129, § 2. 

4929. Biennial appropriations expended at one time. With the consent of the board, a town may expend in any first year of a biennial term the full amount of its apportionment of that year, together with an amount equal at least to its apportionment of the next ensuing year; but the apportionment shall be divided and paid by the state as though the work had been done in the two years under separate apportionments.

P. L. § 4670. G. L. § 4593. P. S. § 4010. 1906, No. 111, § 9. 

4930. Joint fund; report. When a town receives the apportionments provided for by section 4916, it shall expend the same under the rules provided by the board in accordance with the provisions of this chapter and shall report the work done with such fund on special forms furnished by the board. The board shall issue separate certificates to the auditor of accounts in favor of such town for such sums as are due it from such fund for the work actually done according to the provisions of this chapter. 

1951, No. 108, § 12. P. L. § 4671. 1933, No. 157, § 4392. G. L. § 4594. P. S. § 4011. 

1906, No. 111, § 10. 

4931. Contract work. When the selectmen of a town that receives the apportionments provided for by section 4916 request that the work be done by contract, the board may grant such request. The board in all such cases shall furnish plans and specifications upon which the contract shall be awarded by the selectmen and the board or its agent to the lowest responsible bidder. All such work shall be done to the satisfaction and in accordance with the requirements of the board. 

195,1 No. 108, § 13. P. L. § 4672. 1933, No. 157, § 4393. G. L. § 4595. P. S. § 4012. 

1906, No. 111, § 11.

4932. Road engineers for villages. Upon application from an incorporated village, the board may furnish the services of a competent highway engineer or expert for consultation and advice and the making of plans for the improvement of its streets and the supervision of work, under the following conditions: an incorporated village voting and expending at least $1,000.00 shall be entitled to aid in engineering service and advice under this section, but the amount of such service shall be determined by the board, and in no case shall it exceed $700.00 in any one year.

P. L. § 4673. 1927, No. 66. G. L. § 4596. 1917, No. 254, § 4503. 1908, No. 97, § 4. 

P. S. § 4013. 1906, No. 111, § 12. 

4933. Payment of apportionments. The auditor of accounts shall issue his warrants in payment of the certificates issued by the board in favor of towns or persons. In its discretion, the board may issue certificates in full after the work is completed or in part payment as the work progresses. Such warrants shall be paid from the respective funds provided for by this title, except as otherwise provided.

P. L. § 4674. G. L. § 4597. 1910, No. 127, § 3. 1908, No. 97, § 5. P. S. § 4015. 

1906, No. 111, § 14. 1898, No. 65, § 4. 

Unorganized Towns and Gores. 

4934. Duties of state highway board. The highway board shall have the same power in all matters pertaining to highways and bridges in unorganized towns and gores, and to lay out, cause to be worked and opened for public travel, alter and discontinue, build and repair highways and bridges therein, that selectmen and road commissioners have in organized towns. Landowners shall have the same right of appeal from the decision of the highway board in the exercise of such power, as in like proceedings by selectmen and road commissioners in organized towns.

P. L. § 4675. G. L. § 4455. 1912, No. 42, § 38. P. S. §§ 3875, 3876. V. S. §§ 3354, 3355. 

1892, No. 59, §§ 1, 2. 1888, No. 31, § 1. R. L. § 2971. G. S. 24, § 53. 1859, No. 23. 

68 Vt. 234.

Closing Highways. 

4935. Use of highways; regulations by board. The highway board shall make and promulgate such rules and regulations as from the then condition of any highway or portion thereof shall be necessary to prevent abuse of such highways or portion thereof during any period of any season of the year specified in such rules or regulations.

P. L. § 4676. 1933, No. 157, § 4397. 1921, No. 130, § 2. 

4936. Same; filing and posting notices. Such rules and regulations shall be filed with the selectmen of the towns in which the highways affected thereby are situated, and shall be posted in two public places in such towns, and conspicuous notices thereof shall be posted at each end of that part of the highway affected. 

P. L. § 4677. 1933, No. 157, § 4398. 1921, No. 130, § 2. 

4937. Penalty. A person who violates the rules and regulations specfied in the two preceding sections and thereby causes damage to a highway shall be fined not more than $100.00 for each offense, and shall be liable to the town in which the damage is done for all damages to such highway, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute.

P. L. § 4678. 1933, No. 157, § 4399. 1921, No. 130, § 2. 

Machinery; Federal Government 

4938. Acceptance, funds available. Upon such terms as the Federal government may prescribe, the highway board is authorized to accept from the Federal government machinery, trucks and equipment to be used in highway construction and maintenance, and for such purposes may use from the highway appropriations such funds as may be necessary. 

P. L. § 4679. 1923, No. 77, § 2. 1921, No. 127, § 1.

4939. Care of equipment. With the approval of the governor, the highway board may purchase, rent, lease or erect such suitable garages and such other buildings as may be necessary for the care of such equipment. Such garages and such other buildings shall be maintained and operated by the highway board. The board is authorized to employ necessary employees for such purposes and for the operation of trucks and machinery.

P. L. § 4680. 1933, No. 157, § 4401. 1921, No. 127, § 2. 

4940. Same; letting of equipment. The highway board may let trucks and other road machinery upon such terms as it sees fit. Moneys received from the rental of trucks or other machinery under the provisions of this section or from the sale thereof shall be covered into the state treasury. 

P. L. § 4681. 1933, No. 157, § 4402. 1921, No. 127, § 2. 

Winter Maintenance, Certain Highways. 

4941. State Aid; application. When a town or incorporated village desires to keep a part or all of its state aid highways open for motor vehicle traffic during the winter season, the selectmen of a town, or the trustees of an incorporated village, may apply to the highway board for aid in winter maintenance. The board may approve such applications.

P. L. § 4682. 1933, No. 157, § 4403. 1931, No. 65, § 1. 1927, No. 65, § 2. 

4942. Same; report. A report of the work done in accordance with such applications shall be made upon forms furnished by the board and in such detail as it may require and such reports shall be filed on or before May 15 annually. 

Such report shall be subscribed and sworn to and shall be approved by the district commissioner of the district and forwarded to the board. 

P. L. § 4683. 1933, No. 157, § 4404. 1931, No. 65, § 1. 1927, No. 65, § 2. 

4943. Same; payment. The board shall examine the report and issue a certificate on the auditor of accounts in favor of such town or incorporated village for such an amount as it finds due. The auditor shall issue his warrant in payment of such certificate. The amount shall not exceed one-half the cost of keeping such highway open for motor traffic nor shall it exceed an average of $50.00 per mile in any one town or village.

P. L. § 4684. 1933, No. 157, § 4405. 1931, No. 65, § 1. 1927, No. 65, § 2. 

4944. Winter maintenance on Town highways: appropriation. The sum of $250,000.00 is hereby appropriated annually from the highway fund to the state highway board to be expended for the benefit of towns in the winter maintenance of the town highways. This appropriation shall be expended by such board in a manner similar to the procedure provided by Sections 4941, 4942, and 4943 for winter maintenance of state aid highways provided the amount paid to any town shall not exceed the amount expended by such town nor shall it exceed an average of $25.00 per mile in any town. The expenditure of each town's proportionate share of such $250,000.00 shall be under the sole charge of a committee consisting of the selectmen of the town and the district engineer having jurisdiction in such town as a representative of the state highway board. 

1951, No. 108, § 4. 1945, No. 90, § 1. 

4945. Same; payment. The money appropriated under the provisions of the preceding section, except as hereinafter stated, shall be a direct grant to the towns to reimburse them for money paid out in winter maintenance of town highways. 

The preceding section shall not in any way be construed to mean that a town will have to match the money it receives. Any sums which a town would be entitled to that it does not use for such purposes, on or before the end of the fiscal year ending 

June 30, shall revert to the highway department. 

1947, No. 202, § 4890. 1945, No. 90, § 2. 

Guide Boards and Warnings. 

4946. Signs, erection by highway board. The highway board may erect and maintain guide boards, town line signs and danger signs on state and state aid

highways. The expense incurred in the erection and maintenance thereof shall be paid out of the appropriations available for the use of the department of highways. 

P. L. § 4685. 1925, No. 69, § 1. 1921, No. 126, § 1. 

4947. Marking of main routes and points of especial interest. The highway board is hereby authorized to mark by some distinguishing sign main routes of travel in the state and may mark points of especial interest. The expense thereof shall be paid out of the appropriations available for the use of the department of highways.

P. L. § 4686. 1925, No. 69, § 4. 1921, No. 116, § 4. 

4948. Guide boards; danger signs; permission for erection. A person shall not erect a guide board, danger, distance or routing sign on the state and state aid highways without first having obtained the consent of the highway board, or on other highways without the consent of the selectmen, trustees of a village or the city council in which the highway is located. The provisions of this section shall not apply to danger signs erected by railroads at railroad crossings, or to guide boards or signs erected by municipal officials.

P. L. § 4687. 1925, No. 69, § 2. 1921, No. 126, § 2. 

4949.

Penalty. A person violating a provision of the preceding section shall be fined not more than $25.00.

P. L. § 4688. 1925, No. 69, § 3. 1921, No. 126, § 3. 

Vermont Labor. 

4950. Preference to. A person, firm or corporation to whom has been awarded any contract for the construction, reconstruction, improvement and repair of a highway under the jurisdiction of the highway board shall give preference to Vermont labor and to trucks owned in Vermont and operated by residents of Vermont on all contracts for such construction, reconstruction, improvement and repair provided. this action is not contrary to the federal government rules and regulations.

P. L. § 4689. 1933, No. 75, § 1. 1931, No. 69, § 1. 

4951. Minimum wage. In making up specifications and advertising for bids on highway work and bridges the highway board shall fix, subject to local conditions, the minimum wage per hour for various classes of labor and the minimum to be paid per hour or per cubic yard for trucks or teams which the contractor shall be bound to pay.

P. L. § 4690. 1933, No. 76. 

Small Claims. 

4952. Small claims; hearing and award; payment. When a claim is made for personal injuries or property damage, or both, sustained as the result of negligence of any employee of the department of highways, the highway board shall forthwith hear all parties in interest and may award such damages as it deems just, not to exceed $300.00. The board shall certify its findings to the auditor of accounts who shall issue his warrant for the amount of the award and the same shall be payable from the appropriations available for the use of the department of highways.

P. L. § 4691. 1931, No. 67, § 1. 

CHAPTER 234. STATE HIGHWAY SYSTEM. 

4953. Definition. For the purposes of this chapter and unless otherwise required by the context, the word "highway" or "state highway" includes right of way, bridges, drainage structures, signs, guard rails, vegetation and structures. 

P. L. § 4692. 1931, No. 61, § 1.

4954. Highways taken over. At the expense of the state, the highway board shall construct, maintain and control the state aid highways now and hereafter included in the Federal Aid System, and such highways shall be known as state highways; provided however, that in towns having a population of fifteen hundred or over, such state highways may not include streets and highways where, for the space of one-half mile thereon, the houses average to stand one hundred feet or less apart.

P. L. § 4693. 1931, No. 61, § 2. 

4955. Additions to system; requirements; petition. When a state aid highway has been improved with a better than gravel surface, the highway board, upon the petition of the selectmen of the town in which such highway is situated, with the approval of the governor, may take over such highway as a part of the state highway system as authorized by this chapter, and such highway, when so taken over, shall be subject to all the provisions hereof. 

P. L. § 4694. 1933, No. 78, § 1.
