From route 14 in East Brookfield to the end of the state highway in Pond Village, otherwise known as Brookfield Village, such highway being about two miles in length. 

4957. Central route; authorization; financing. The state highway board, with the approval of the governor, is hereby authorized to make plans for, lay out, locate, construct, maintain, repair, widen, grade and improve as part of the state highway system a central route extending through the central portion of the state from the Massachusetts to the Canadian border. Such route shall utilize highways belonging to the state highway system, or eligible to be taken over under the provisions of section 4955, but the board with the approval of the governor and of the selectmen of the town in which such highway is located, may take over as part of the state highway system an unimproved highway forming a connecting link in such route and such highway when so taken over shall be subject to all the provisions of this chapter. The highway board shall use for such purposes such funds as are fairly allottable to the district through which such route shall pass on the equitable distribution of the funds available for the state highway system. 

1937, No. 108, § 1. 

4958. Same; picnic grounds and parks. In the name of the state, the state highway board may purchase or lease, or take in the manner provided in sections 4971 to 4978 inclusive, as part of the highway, any land adjacent to such central or any route which is needed for the preservation of its scenic character or for the purpose of providing picnic or camping grounds or parking places for the use of persons traveling thereon; provided however, that land taken for such purposes by condemnation proceedings shall not extend more than two hundred fifty feet from the center of the traveled portion of the highway. 

1937, No. 108, § 2. 

4959. Maps filed. Upon determination of the state highway system the board shall make maps showing all the highways taken over and file a copy thereof in its office in Montpelier. They shall also file a copy of the map of such highways located in any town in the office of the clerk of such town. 

When such maps are so filed, the transfer of control of such highways in the town shall be deemed to have taken place.

P. L. § 4695. 1931, No. 61, § 3. 

4960. Closing; detours. The board shall exercise exclusive jurisdiction over all state highways and may close any part, or all, of such highways to public travel during construction or repair, and shall mark and maintain a detour around the section closed.

P. L. § 4696. 1931, No. 61, § 4. 

4961. Parking places; camping grounds; regulations. The board may make regulations for the use of parking places and camping grounds within the limits of the state highway system, and, by regulation, may restrict the parking of a

vehicle on any part of the state highway system for commercial purposes. A copy of such regulation, duly certified to by the commissioner of highways, shall be competent evidence in any trial to prove such regulation. Conspicuous notice of such regulations shall be posted in each parking place and camping ground affected by such regulations. If the affected area is a highway, then such notices shall be posted at each end of the portion so affected. A person who violates any such regulation shall be fined not more than $100.00. 

1939, No. 107, § 1. P. L. § 4696. 1931, No. 61, § 4. 

4962. Transfer of duties and liabilities. When by existing statute a duty or liability is imposed upon or power granted to a town or village or any officer thereof with reference to highways, bridges or culverts or the laying out, construction or the discontinuance of the same, including the provisions of chapter 409 so far as the same are applicable, such duties, liabilities and powers so far as they apply to the highways, bridges and culverts taken over by the highway board, shall be imposed upon and granted to the state to be performed, assumed and exercised by such board.

P. L. § 4697. 1933, No. 157, § 4416. 1931, No. 61, § 5. 

105 Vt. 370. 

4963. Rights of action; notice. All rights of action on account of the insufficiency or want of repair of any bridge or culvert on the highways taken over by the state shall exist against the state and not against the town, provided that the notice required in sections 5229 and 5230 is first given in writing to the board and provided further that liability hereunder shall be the same as set forth in section 5227.

P. L. § 4698. 1933, No. 157, § 4417. 1931, No. 61, § 5. 

4964. Same; where brought and service. Such action against the state shall be brought against the state, returnable to the county court in the county where the damages were sustained. Service of the writ shall be made on the governor or the attorney general. 

P. L. § 4699. 1933, No. 157, § 4418. 1931, No. 61, § 5. 

4965. Same; settlement and payment. After receiving the notice aforesaid the highway board, with the approval of the attorney general, may adjust and settle with the person claiming damages and the auditor of accounts shall issue his warrant in payment of the certificate issued by the highway board and approved by the attorney general in favor of such person. All money expended under this section shall be charged to the annual appropriation for state highways. 

P. L. § 4700. 1933, No. 157, § 4419. 1931, No. 61, § 5. 

4966. Construction. This chapter shall not be construed to transfer the ownership of any real estate located within the limits of highways taken over by the board, or to affect penalties and liabilities heretofore incurred or proceedings pending at the time such highways were taken over. 

P. L. § 4701. 1931, No. 61, § 6. 

4967. Assessments of towns. Annually, on or before April 1, the board shall file with the state treasurer a certified list of the miles of highways taken over as state highways in each town. The state treasurer shall assess such towns an amount per mile on such state highways to be determined by dividing the total grand list of the town by the total highway mileage of that town, provided however, that in no case shall the annual assessment exceed $300.00 per mile or $5,000.00 or twenty cents on a dollar of the grand list to any one town. 

1935, No. 110, § 1. P. L. § 4702. 1931, No. 61, § 7. 

4968. Same; payment. On or before September 1 of each year the state treasurer shall make out and transmit to each town treasurer a notice of the amount so assessed and the same shall be paid into the state treasury on or before 

October 10 following and credited to the state highway appropriation. The town treasurer, upon receipt of such notice, shall transmit the same to the selectmen who

shall draw an order on the treasurer of the town for the amount of such assessment. 

Such treasurer shall pay the same into the state treasury out of any moneys in the town treasury. When the funds in the hands of the treasurer are not sufficient to pay such assessment, the selectmen shall borrow the necessary amount. 

P. L. § 4703. 1931, No. 61, § 8. 

4969. Boundaries. When the survey of a highway has not been properly recorded or the record preserved, or if terminations and boundaries cannot be ascertained, the board may use and control for highway purposes one and one-half rods each side of the center of the traveled portion thereof. 

P. L. § 4704. 1931, No. 61, § 9. 

4970. Purchase. The board is authorized to purchase or lease any land, taking conveyance in the name of the state, when such land is needed in connection with the layout, construction, repair and maintenance of any state highway, or the reconstruction of the same; and may acquire or construct buildings necessary for use in connection with such work. When any of the land or the buildings so acquired or the buildings constructed by it become no longer necessary for such purposes, the board may sell and convey the same, with the consent of the auditor of accounts. The avails from such sale shall be covered into the state treasury to the credit of the state highway appropriation.

P. L. § 4705. 1931, No. 61, § 11. 

4971. Condemnation. The board may lay out, relocate, alter, construct, reconstruct, maintain, repair, widen, grade and improve any state highway, when, in its judgment, the interest of the state shall so require. In the name of the state, it may take any land or rights therein that it deems necessary for such purposes, and the highway board and its agents in furtherance of such purposes may enter upon land adjacent to such highway or upon other lands for the purpose of examination and making necessary surveys doing as little damage as possible to the owners of such lands.

P. L. § 4706. 1931, No. 61, § 10. 

4972. Condemnation; survey. When the highway board takes or desires to take land or any rights therein for the purposes specified in the preceding section except as modified by section 4980 in regard to relocation of highways, it shall cause such land to be surveyed and furnish a copy thereof to the owner of such land, or his known agent.

P. L. § 4707. 1933, No. 157, § 4426. 1931, No. 61, § 12. 

4973. Same; notice; hearing. When such owner and the board are unable to agree as to the necessity for such taking, or as to the amount of compensation therefor, if the board desires to proceed with the taking thereof, it shall appoint a time and place, in the town where the land is situated, for examining the premises and hearing parties interested, giving at least twelve days' notice in writing thereof to the party owning such land, mortgagees, tenants, and all parties interested therein. At such time and place, the board shall consider whether the necessity or convenience of individuals or of the state require the taking of such land or rights therein and fix the compensation to be paid therefor. 

P. L. § 4708. 1933, No. 157, § 4427. 1931, No. 61, § 12. 

4974. Same; report. Within ten days thereafter, the board shall file in the office of the clerk of the town or city where such land is situated, a copy of the survey thereof and a report of its doings at such hearing to be recorded therein. When no appeal is taken from such action, the compensation for such land shall be paid within thirty days from the date such report is filed.

P. L. § 4709. 1933, No. 157, § 4428. 1931, No. 61, § 12. 

4975. Same; appeal. A person interested in such lands, who is dissatisfied with the decision of the board as to the necessity for the taking thereof, or as to the amount of damages awarded for the same, within twenty days after such report of

the highway board has been filed, may appeal from either the necessity of the taking, or the damages awarded, or both, by petition in writing to the county court in the county where the land is situated, and any number of persons aggrieved may join in such petition.

P. L. § 4710. 1933, No. 157, § 4429. 1931, No. 61, § 12. 

4976. Same; special term of court. Such petition, with a citation returnable in not more than twenty days from the date of appeal, shall be served on a member of the board at least twelve days before the same is returnable and shall be filed with the clerk of such court on or before the return date thereof. If such court is not in session, such clerk shall forthwith notify the superior judge who presided at the stated term of such court last adjourned, who shall thereupon reconvene such court and proceed to act upon such appeal. When such appeal is taken only upon the damages awarded, the board may proceed with the work for which such land is taken. When such appeal is upon the necessity of the taking of such land, all proceedings shall be stayed until final disposition of such appeal. 

1941, No. 88, § 1. P. L. § 4711. 1933, No. 157, § 4430. 1931, No. 61, § 12. 

4977. Same; commissioners. Within ten days from the return date of the petition, the court shall appoint three disinterested freeholders as commissioners to inquire into the matters contained in such appeal. Within ten days from their appointment, the commissioners shall give notice to the petitioners and to one or more of the highway board, when and where they will hear the parties. When they have completed the hearing, they shall make report to the court which shall act upon such report forthwith. 

1947, No. 202, § 4922. 1941, No. 88, § 2. P. L. § 4712. 1933, No. 157, § 4431. 

1931, No. 61, § 12. 

110 Vt. 44. 

4978. Same; procedure. The court may accept or reject the report in whole or in part and by its order may establish, alter, resurvey, widen, or change such highway, or make such other order as appears just and render judgment for the petitioners for such damages as they have severally sustained. When the sum allowed is greater than was offered by the board, the court may tax costs for the petitioners or for either party, as is just. The court shall fix the time for paying the damages awarded.

P. L. § 4713. 1933, No. 157, § 4432. 1931, No. 61, § 12. 

4979. Same; opening highway. When a highway is completed and opened for the use of the public under this chapter, the board shall make a certificate of that fact and cause the same to be recorded in the office of the town clerk where such highway is situated. The day on which such certificate is recorded shall be the time of the opening of such highway.

P. L. § 4714. 1933, No. 157, § 4433. 1931, No. 61, § 12. 

4980. Relocation; notice. When the state highway board desires to relocate a highway under this chapter, it shall set a time for hearing thereon and pub ish notice thereof in a newspaper having general circulation in the town where such relocation is to be made. Such publication shall be at least twelve days prior to such hearing. It shall also give a notice and furnish a survey to all persons interested in the lands to be taken for such highway, and also to one or more of the selectmen of the town, trustees of an incorporated village or mayor of a city where such highway is to be relocated. Such notices shall be given as provided in section 4973. 

P. L. § 4715. 1933, No. 77, § 1. 1931, No. 61, § 13. 

4981. Same; hearing. A person or municipal corporation affected by such relocation may appear and be heard in connection therewith. If after such hearing the board determines that the relocation of such highway is necessary for the convenience of individuals, or of the state, it shall then proceed to determine under what conditions the state highway board shall relinquish control to the town of that portion of the state highway system affected by such relocation or construc-

tion. It shall then proceed to make such certificate and award damages as is provided in section 4973.

P. L. § 4716. 1933, No. 77, § 1. 1931, No. 61, § 13. 

4982. Same; appeal. A person or municipal corporation aggrieved by such findings or report of the board may appeal therefrom in accordance with section 4975 and all proceedings in regard to the relocation of such highway shall be stayed until the final disposition of such appeal. 

P. L. § 4717. 1933, No. 77, § 1. 1931, No. 61., § 13. 

4983. Highway relinquished. When the state highway board relinquishes control as provided in this chapter, the maintenance and control of the portions of such highway or highways shall be vested in the town wherein located. 

P. L. § 4718. 1933, No. 77, § 1. 1931, No. 61, § 13. 

4984. Change of jurisdiction. Where a highway has been relocated under the provisions of the four preceding sections, the state highway board may relinquish control of the portion of the state highway system affected by such relocation or construction by filing a certificate, containing a description of such highway or portion thereof relinquished, in the town clerk's office. When such control is relinquished the maintenance and control of the portion of such highway shall be vested in the town wherein such highway is located. 

1947, No. 202, § 4929. P. L. § 4719. 1933, No. 77, § 2. 

4985. Grant of authority to erect buildings. The highway board is authorized to erect temporary or permanent buildings upon land owned or leased by the state and under the jurisdiction of the department of highways for purposes necessary to the construction and maintenance of state highways and bridges thereon.

P. L. § 4720. 1931, No. 61, § 14. 

Missisquoi Bay Bridge. 

4986. Missisquoi Bay bridge; transfer to state. The operation, management, control, maintenance and all powers incidental thereto granted to and vested in the Missisquoi Bay Bridge Commission are hereby transferred and vested in the state highway board. 

1941, No. 89, § 1. 

4987. Duties of state board. Upon receipt of a detailed financial statement audited by a certified public accountant duly signed and sworn to by the members of the Missisquoi Bay Bridge Commission, the state highway board, in behalf of the state, shall:

I. Control, operate, maintain and manage the Missisquoi Bay Bridge and approaches and appurtenances thereto belonging, free from toll; 

II. Assume all outstanding liabilities, including notes, bonds, contracts, leases, rights, easements and other interests heretofore created by the Missisquoi 

Bay Bridge Commission;

III. Pay out of the highway department funds the Missisquoi Bay Bridge 

Commission outstanding notes according to their tenor in the sum of $40,690.00 and interest accrued to date of payment, or pay such compromised amounts in settlement thereof as approved by the governor; 

IV. Accept and receipt for all cash turned over to such board by the Missisquoi Bay Bridge Commission. 

1947, No. 202, § 4932. 1945, No. 92, § 1. 1941, No. 89, § 2. 

4988. Appropriation. The moneys necessary to retire the obligations heretofore contracted in the construction of the Missisquoi Bay Bridge are hereby appropriated from highway funds at the time such obligations accrue. 

1945, No. 92, § 2.

Sandbar Bridge. 

4989. Sandbar bridge and highway southerly therefrom; maintenance.

The board shall maintain, toll free, at the expense of the state, the sandbar bridge and the highway southerly therefrom to the southerly end of the lower iron bridge over the Lamoille river, and no town shall be liable by reason of the insufficiency of any bridge or culvert therein included. The board shall issue its certificates to the auditor of accounts for expenses incurred under the provisions of this section. The auditor shall issue his warrants in payment of such certificates, and such warrants shall be paid out of the maintenance fund. 

P. L. § 4721. 1933, No. 157, § 4438. 1925, No. 68. G. L. § 4584. 1915, No. 139. 

1910, No. 272. 1906, No. 253, § 3. 

Drawbridges. 

4990. When maintained by state. When a town or corporation, owning or controlling a drawbridge and the abutments thereto in this state, conveys to this state all its rights, title and interest in such drawbridge and the abutments thereto not exceeding thirty feet on each side of the draw in such bridge, the same shall be maintained and operated by the state, tolls free, under the supervision of the highway board. The board shall issue its certificates to the auditor of accounts for expenses incurred under the provisions of this section. The auditor shall issue his warrants in payment of such certificates, and such warrants shall be paid out of the highway appropriations.

P. L. § 4722. 1933, No. 157, § 4439. G. L. § 4518. 1912, No. 263. 1910, No. 142. 

Peru Turnpike. 

4991. Maintenance; appropriation. The highway board shall maintain, toll free, at the expense of the state that section of the Peru Turnpike, so-called, extending from the Peru town line to the Manchester town line. The town of 

Winhall shall not be liable by reason of the insufficiency of any bridge or culvert thereon. The board shall issue its certificates to the auditor of accounts for expenses incurred under the provisions of this section. The auditor shall issue his warrants in payment of such certificates, and such warrants shall be paid out of the highway appropriations.

P. L. § 4723. 1933, No. 157, § 4440. 1929, No. 156, § 1. 

Through Ways. 

4992. Through way designation. The highway board from time to time may designate any highway or part thereof as a "through way," and after notice may revoke such designation. A city or incorporated village may designate any highway, street or part thereof within the control of such city or incorporated village as a "through way" and may revoke such designation. 

P. L. § 4724. 1933, No. 157, § 4441. 1931, No. 68, § 1. 

108 Vt. 182. 

4993. Same; warning signs. Such designation of a "through way" shall not become effective as to regulation of traffic at any point of intersection with another "way" until the highway board or the officer having control of streets in a city or incorporated village, as the case may be, shall have caused suitable warning signs and signals to be erected at or near such point. 

P. L. § 4725. 1933, No. 157, § 4442. 1931, No. 68, § 1. 

108 Vt. 182. 

4994. Same; intersecting ways. A way joining a "through way" at an angle, whether or not it crosses the same, shall be deemed to intersect it, and the word "way", unless the context otherwise requires, shall include a through or other way.

P. L. § 4726. 1933, No. 157, § 4443. 1931, No. 68, § 1. 

108 Vt. 182. 

4995. Same; penalty. A person who violates a provision of the three preceding sections shall be fined not more than $100.00 or imprisoned not more than thirty days, or both. 

1947, No. 202, § 4939. P. L. § 4727. 1931, No. 68, § 2.

Roadside Improvement. 

4996. Planting. Subject to the consent of abutting landowners, the highway board may cause or allow grasses, shrubs, vines and trees to be planted and maintained along state highways and state aid highways, to be paid for as part of the cost of construction or maintenance of such highways. 

P. L. § 4728. 1931, No. 71, § 1. 

4997. Same; removal. A person shall not cut, trim, remove or otherwise damage any grasses, shrubs, vines, or trees growing within the limits of a state or state aid highway, without first having obtained the consent in writing of the board. 

1947, No. 202, § 4941. P. L. § 4729. 1933, No. 157, § 4446. 1931, No. 71, § 2. 

4998. Same; penalty. A person who wilfully or maliciously cuts, trims, removes or otherwise damages such grasses, shrubs, vines or trees without first having obtained the consent in writing of the board, shall be fined not more than $100.00 nor less than $10.00, for each offense. Nothing in the two preceding sections shall be construed to limit the present rights of abutting property owners. 

P. L. § 4730. 1933, No. 157, § 4447. 1931, No. 71, § 2. 

4999. Same; contracts; appropriations. The board may enter into agreements with individuals or organizations who wish to plant grasses, shrubs, vines, trees or flowers; or to make gifts or appropriations to carry out the provisions of sections 4996 and 4997.

P. L. § 4731, 1931, No. 71, § 3. 

5000. Trees and brush; removal; regulations. Annually, during the months of August and September, or earlier if deemed necessary, the road commissioner of a town shall cause to be cut and burned or removed from within the limits of the highways under his care, all trees and bushes that obstruct the view of the road ahead or that cause damage to the highway or that are objectionable from a material or scenic standpoint. Shade and fruit trees that have been set out or marked by the abutting landowners shall be preserved if the usefulness or safety of the highway is not impaired thereby. Young trees standing at a proper distance from the road bed and from each other, and banks and hedges of bushes that serve as a protection to the highway or add beauty to the roadside, shall be preserved. 

On all state roads, the work to be done under the provisions of this section shall be under the supervision and control of the state highway commissioner, who shall annually make such rules and regulations for the purpose of carrying out such work as he deems to be for the best interest of the town and state. The road commissioner shall draw his orders to pay for the work herein provided for. 

P. L. § 4732. 1933, No. 79. G. L. § 4562. 1912, No. 141. 1908, No. 96 P. S. § 3977. 

1906, No. 110, § 1. V. S. § 3464. 1884, No. 17. 

5001. Slash and brush disposal; regulations. A person, firm or corporation who cuts, or who causes or permits to be cut, any forest growth on lands which are within or border upon the right of way of any public highway, or which border upon the woodland of another, or which border upon land having buildings standing thereon within one hundred feet of such border, shall dispose of the slash and debris caused by such cutting in such a manner that no inflammable material shall remain on the ground within fifty feet of any public highway or of any such lands above specified. 

1945, No. 84, § 1. 1941, No. 82, § 1. 

5002. Same; exception. If in the opinion of the town forest fire warden. there is no fire hazard as a result of a cutting he shall issue, upon request, a statement relieving the operator of the conditions required in section 5001. 

1941, No. 82, § 2. 

5003. Same; penalty. Upon complaint of a fire warden, a person who violates the provisions of sections 5001 and 5002 shall be fined not more than $50.00 for each offense. 

1941, No. 82, § 3.

CHAPTER 235. FEDERAL AID. 

5004. Federal aid road act; approval by state; duties of highway board; flight strips. The state of Vermont assents to, approves and pledges its good faith to meet the terms of an act of Congress approved July 11, 1916, entitled "An act to provide that the United States shall aid the states in the construction of rural post roads, and for other purposes," and amendments thereto, including flight strips, and the highway board is authorized to take such action as may be necessary to secure to the state the aid and benefits provided thereby. 

1943, No. 74. P. L. § 4733. G. L. § 4599. 1917, No. 123, §§ 1, 2. 

5005. Same; cooperation with federal government and use of highway funds. To effect the purposes of the preceding section, the highway board may comply with the rules and regulations prescribed by the commissioner of public roads and may use so much of the funds available for highway purposes as shall be necessary to secure aid from the federal government under the federal act specified in the preceding section; and in addition thereto may use such further sum as may be necessary for surveys, plans, specifications, estimates and assistance necessary to carry out the provisions of this chapter. 

1947, No. 202 § 4949. P. L. § 4734. G. L. § 4600. 1917, No. 123, §§ 2, 3. 

5006. Same; designation of highways; approval of contracts. The highway board may designate the highways to be improved, constructed or reconstructed under the provisions of this chapter. Subject to the limitations of the federal aid road act, and the rules and regulations of the commissioner of public roads, pertaining thereto, the board may contract in the name of the state for the construction or reconstruction of any highway and the building of any bridge under this chapter and the location of the work, the kind of construction, and whether the same shall be by force account or by contract. 

1947, No. 202, § 4950. P. L. § 4735. 1923, No. 77, § 1. 1919, No. 116, § 1. G. L. § 4601. 

1917, No. 123, § 4. 

114 Vt. 41. 

5007. Same; cooperation by municipalities. A municipality may cooperate with the highway board in any way necessary to carry out the provisions of this chapter, and to that end may contribute financial or other assistance. 

P. L. § 4736. G. L. § 4602. 1917, No. 123, § 5. 

114 Vt. 41. 

Construction; Bridges; Federal Aid Road Act. 

5008. Federal bridges; construction. In the construction of highways under the provisions of the federal aid road act the highway board by agreement with the selectmen of the town in which a bridge is located may repair, build or rebuild the bridge. The cost of such bridge work, in the discretion of the highway board, may be paid out of the appropriation to aid towns as to bridges. 

P. L. § 4737. 1919, No. 115, § 1. 

114 Vt. 41. 

5009. Same; maintenance; payment. When it deems necessary, the highway board may maintain any bridge built or repaired under the provisions of the preceding section and may advance the cost thereof. Upon demand of the highway board, the municipality shall thereafter reimburse the state for the expenditures so made, and the amount repaid shall be covered into the state treasury. 

P. L. § 4738. 1919, No. 115, § 3. 

5010. Same; moneys received. All moneys received from the federal government, from municipalities and from other sources on account of the construction of bridges under this chapter shall be placed to the credit of the federal aid fund to be used for the purposes provided by law for such fund. 

P. L. § 4739. 1927, No. 67, § 1. 1919, No. 115, § 2.

CHAPTER 236. LAYING OUT AND DISCONTINUING HIGHWAYS BY LOCAL AUTHORITIES. 

General Provisions. 

5011. Duties of towns. Highways shall be laid out, built and repaired, and damages to landowners paid by the town in which such highways are situated, except as otherwise provided by law. 

P. L. § 4740. 

R. S. 20, § 4. 

83, Vt. 421.

G. L. § 4387. P. S. § 3807. V. S. § 3287. R. L. § 2913. G. S. 24, § 14. 

38 Vt. 350. 41 Vt. 681. 68 Vt. 66. 70 Vt. 381. 70 Vt. 391. 72 Vt. 242. 

106 Vt. 213. 

5012. Duties of selectmen; pent roads; trails. As the convenience of the inhabitants and the public good require, selectmen shall lay out, alter and discontinue highways. Pent roads and trails shall be deemed highways. 

§ 3288. R. L. § 2914. 

1813, p. 165. 

172. 114 Vt. 1.

P. L. S 4741. 1921, No. 121, § 1. G. L. § 4388. P. S. § 3808. V. S. 

G. S. 24, §§ 1, 15. R. S. 20, §§ 1, 5. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 9. 

22 Vt. 44. 27 Vt. 713. 28 Vt. 587. 47 Vt. 20. 54 Vt. 306. 57 Vt. 

5013. Trails; non-liability of towns. A town shall not be liable for the construction, maintenance or repair of a trail laid out under the provisions of the preceding section.

P. L. § 4742. 1921, No. 121, § 4. 

5014. Width of highways; trails. Highways shall not be less than three rods wide; but the provisions hereof shall not apply to trails, crossroads, lanes used as pent roads and highways lying within an incorporated village or city to connect existing highways, and roads only one end of which connects with existing highways.

P. L. § 4743. 

1884, No. 16. 

1822, p. 18. 

1921, No. 121,

R. L. § 2915.

§ 2. G. L. § 4389. P. S. § 3809. 1896, No. 68, § 1. V. S. § 3289. 

R. S. 20, § 2. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 23. 

67 Vt. 132.

G. S. 24, § 2. 

58 Vt. 567. 

5015. Widening of highways. When highways have been laid out of less width than the law or the public convenience requires, the selectmen may lay out, widen and open the same. Persons whose lands are taken, or who are damaged thereby, shall be entitled to compensation.

P. L. § 4744. G. L. § 4390. P. S. § 3810. V. S. § 3290. R. L. § 2916. G. S. 24, § 16. 

R. S. 20, § 7. 1806, p. 88. 

54 Vt. 306. 

5016. Survey and boundaries. When selectmen or commissioners lay out or alter a highway, as provided in this chapter, they shall cause a survey thereof to be made, mark each termination of the survey by a permanent monument or boundary, or refer the same, by course and distance, to some neighboring permanent monument, and shall establish other boundaries in a similar way. The survey shall describe the highway by courses, distances and width and describe the monuments and boundaries. 

P. L. § 4745. G. L. § 4392. P. S. § 3812. V. S. § 3292. R. L. § 2918. G. S. 24, § 18. 

R. S. 20, § 9. R. 1797, p. 347, § 1. 33 Vt. 653. 54 Vt. 306. 

5017. Order of court and survey; record. A copy of the order of a court, establishing, altering or discontinuing a highway, with a survey of so much of the road as is in any one town, certified by the clerk of the court, shall be recorded in the office of the clerk of such town.

P. L. § 4746. G. L. § 4393. P. S. § 3813. V. S. § 3293. R. L. § 2919. G. S. 24, § 23. 

R. S. 20, § 14. 

5018. Resurvey. If the survey of a highway has not been properly recorded, or the record preserved, or if each termination and its boundaries cannot be ascertained, the highway board for state highways and the selectmen for state aid and town highways may resurvey the same, and make a record thereof in the office of the town clerk in each town in which the resurveyed highway is situate. In the event the boundaries cannot be ascertained, notice of such resurvey shall be given as provided for proceedings held pursuant to chapters 234 and 236 respectively.

When a person owning or interested in lands through or along which a highway is resurveyed by the state highway board is dissatisfied with such resurvey or damages awarded such person may appeal under the provisions of section 4975. On motion of such person or the state highway board or upon order of the court, other persons owning or interested in lands which might be affected by the decision in said appeal may be cited as parties thereto at any time before final determination upon such terms as the court shall prescribe. 

1953, No. 238. P. L. § 4747. G. L. § 4394. P. S. § 3814. 

1906, No. 107, § 1. V. S. § 3294. R. L. § 2920. G. S. 24, § 17. 

55 Vt. 412. 57 Vt. 538. 62 Vt. 198. 66 Vt. 200. 

75 Vt. 385. 92 Vt. 440. 

27 Vt. 728. 

73 Vt. 258. 

R. S. 20, § 8. 

67 Vt. 163. 

5019. Entrance upon lands for survey. In a proceeding to lay out, alter or discontinue a highway, or to determine which of the owners of lands adjacent to a highway have encroached upon the same, and in a proceeding touching public improvements, a surveyor, with the necessary assistants, employed by persons interested in such proceedings, may enter upon lands adjacent to such highway, or upon other lands, for the purpose of making the necessary surveys, doing as little damage as possible to the owners of such lands. 

1947, No. 202, § 4963. P. L. § 4748. G. L. § 4395. P. S. § 3815. 1902, No. 160, § 1. 

Laying out Highways by Petition to Selectmen. 

5020. Petition. Three or more freeholders in a town, desiring to have a highway laid out, altered or discontinued, may apply by petition in writing to the selectmen for that purpose.

P. L. § 4749. G. L. § 4396. P. S. § 3816. V. S. § 3295. R. L. § 2921. G. S. 24, § 19. 

R. S. 20, § 10 1831 No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 9. R. 1797, p. 347, § 1. R. 1787, p. 78. 

57 Vt. 172. 66 Vt. 200. 

5021. Notice, hearing. The selectmen shall appoint forthwith a time for examining the premises and hearing parties interested, and give twelve days' notice thereof to one or more of the petitioners. They shall also give like notice to persons owning or interested in lands through which the highway to be laid out, altered or discontinued may pass, and when a highway is to be altered or discontinued, a like notice shall be given to persons owning or interested in land abutting such highway, of such time and of the time when they will consider claims for damages. 

1935, No. 111. P. L. § 4750. G. L. § 4397. P. S. § 3817. R. 1906, § 3683. V. S. § 3296. 

R. L. § 2922. G. S. 24, § 19. R. S. 20, § 10. 29 Vt. 157. 41 Vt. 579. 47 Vt. 300. 

56 Vt. 28. 57 Vt. 172. 66 Vt. 110. 66 Vt. 200. 66 Vt. 570. 

5022. Survey; discontinuance. When the selectmen, after examining the premises and hearing parties, judge that the public good or the necessity or convenience of individuals requires such highway to be laid out or altered as claimed in the petition, they shall cause the same to be surveyed and shall place suitable stone or iron monuments to properly mark the bounds of their survey. If they decide to discontinue a highway, such discontinuance shall be in writing setting forth definitely such highway.

P. L. § 4751. G. L. § 4398. P. S. § 3818. 1906, No. 108, § 1. V. S. § 3297. R. L. § 2923. 

G. S. 24, §§ 20-22. R. S. 20, §§ 11-13. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 9. R. 1797, p. 347, § 1. 

R. 1787, p. 78. 57 Vt. 538. 66 Vt. 451. 

5023. Selectmen's return; record. Within sixty days after such examination and hearing, the selectmen shall return the original petition with a report of their doings thereon and of the manner of notifying the parties, with such survey or discontinuance, to the town clerk's office. Their order laying out, altering or discontinuing such highway, with the survey, shall be recorded by the clerk. 

P. L. § 4752. G. L. § 4399. P. S. § 3819. R. 1906, § 3685. V. S. § 3298. R. L. § 2924. 

G. S. 24, §§ 20, 22, 23. R. S. 20, §§ 11, 13, 14. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 9. 

R. 1797, p. 347, § 1. 66 Vt. 200. 87 Vt. 46. 

5024. Damages paid or tendered. When the selectmen judge that a person, through whose land such highway is laid or altered, is entitled to damages, they

shall pay or tender to him such damages as they deem reasonable before the highway is opened.

P. L. § 4753. G. L. § 4400. 

R. S. 20, § 12. 

64 Vt. 540. 

1831, No. 4.

P. S. § 3820. V. S. § 3299. R. L. § 2925. G. S. 24, § 21. 

1830, No. 12, §§ 9, 10. R. 1797, p. 347, § 1. R. 1787, p. 78. 

5025. Time for vacating land; notice. When the selectmen lay out or alter a highway, they shall fix in their order the time within which the owner of the lands taken shall remove his buildings, fences, timber, wood or trees. Without the consent of the owner, such time shall not be less than two months, but if the lands taken have buildings thereon, such time shall not be less than six months, nor, in either case, until compensation for damages is paid, if the sum fixed by the selectmen is accepted or damages are awarded by referees. Notice of the time when such removal shall be made shall be given to the owner. 

P. L. § 4754. G. L. § 4401. P. S. § 3821. 1900, No. 49, § 1. V. S. § 3300. R. L. § 2926. 

G. S. 24, § 26. R. S. 20, § 24. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 10. 21 Vt. 108. 57 Vt.538. 

66 Vt. 110. 66 Vt. 200. 

5026. Possession after time expires. When a highway is laid out or altered, the selectmen may take possession of the land within the surveyed limits thereof, at any time after the expiration of the time fixed by the selectmen or county court for landowners to remove their buildings, fences, trees, timber or wood. They may remove obstructions therefrom, and lay such lands open for working and travel, if they have previously paid or tendered to the landowners the damages awarded by them or by the commissioners laying out or altering the same. 

P. L. § 4755. G. L. § 4402. P. S. § 3822. V. S. § 3301. R. L. § 2927. 1867, No. 30. 

5027. Walls and fences; removal. When selectmen lay out or widen a highway, and a considerable portion of the damage to owners of lands consists in the removal of walls or fences, and persons desiring such change in the highway offer to remove such walls or fences, and reconstruct them upon the line of the proposed highway, the selectmen, or the commissioners, shall award only reasonable damages to landowners, exclusive of the expense of removing such walls or fences.

P. L. § 4756. G. L. § 4403. P. S. § 3823. V. S. § 3302. R. L. § 2928. G. S. 24, §§ 27, 29. 

1851, No. 26. 

64 Vt. 540. 

5028. Certificate of completion; record. When such highway is completed and opened for the use of the public, the selectmen shall make a certificate of that fact, which shall be recorded in the office of the town clerk. The day on which such certificate is recorded, shall be the time of opening such highway. 

P. L. § 4757. G. L. § 4404. P. S. § 3824. V. S. § 3303. R. L. § 2929. G. S. 24, § 30. 

R. S. 20, § 25. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, §§ 9, 10. 1820, p. 22. 3 Vt. 457. 3 Vt. 590.

87 Vt. 46. 

5029. Copy to owner or occupant of land. Within six days from the day of its record, a copy of such certificate shall be delivered by the selectmen to each person whose lands are taken by such highway, if such person is known, or if unknown, to the occupant, if any, of such lands. 

P. L. § 4758. G. L. § 4405. P. S. § 3825. V. S. § 3304. R. L. § 2930. G. S. 24, § 32. 

1845, No. 24, § 1. 87 Vt. 46. 

Reference and Proceedings Before a Justice as to Damages. 

5030. Reference and award. When a person interested in lands through which a highway is laid or altered by selectmen does not accept the damages awarded by them, the selectmen, in behalf of the town, may agree to refer the question of damages to one or more disinterested persons, whose award shall be final. Such reference and award, and the proceedings in settling the damages, shall be stated in the doings of the selectmen returned to the town clerk. 

P. L. § 4759. G. L. § 4406. P. S. § 3826. V. S. § 3305. R. L. § 2931. G. S. 24, § 31. 

R. S. 20, § 15. 19 Vt. 453. 26 Vt. 345.

5031. Petition to whom. When a person interested in lands through which selectmen have laid or altered a highway is not satisfied with the damages offered by the selectmen, or when damages are not offered and a reference thereof is not agreed upon, by petition in writing, he may apply to a municipal judge or to a justice of the county residing in an adjoining town, for the appointment of commissioners to appraise such damages. Any persons claiming damages on account of such highway may join in the petition, but such petition shall not delay the opening of the highway.

P. L. § 4760. G. L. § 4407. 1908, No. 62. P. S. § 3827. V. S. § 3306. R. L. § 2932. 

G. S. 24, § 32. R. S. 20, § lu. 1820, p. 20. R. 1797, p. 347, § 1. R. 1787, p. 78. 19 Vt. 453.

5032. Same; when brought. Such application shall be made within sixty days after the highway is opened for travel, in case of persons notified of such opening as provided in section 5029. In case of persons not so notified, it may be made within one year from such opening. 

P. L. § 4761. G. L. § 4408. P. S. § 3828. V. S. §3307. R. L. § 2933. G. S. 24, § 32.

5033. Same; citation; service. On the receipt of the petition, the magistrate shall issue a citation, stating the time and place of hearing, which, with the petition, shall be served on one or more of the selectmen at least six days before such time.

P. L. § 4762. G. L. § 4409. P. S. § 3829. V. S. § 3308. R. L. § 2934. G. S. 24, § 33.

5034. Commissioners. When the parties do not agree upon any other mode of appointing such commissioners, the magistrate may make a list of eighteen disinterested freeholders of the town in which he resides. Each party may object to six on the list, and out of the number not objected to, the magistrate shall, by lot, select three for commissioners.

P. L. § 4763. G. L. § 4410. P. S. § 3830. V. S. § 3309. R. L. § 2935.

72 Vt. 419. 

5035. Same; commission. The magistrate shall annex a commission to the petition and citation and issue the same to such commissioners, directing them to appraise impartially the damages sustained by the petitioner as set forth in the petition, and make return of their doings on oath to him by a day fixed in such commission, if the damages appraised to a petitioner do not exceed $40.00, and to the county court in the same county, if the damages appraised to a petitioner exceed that sum. 

1947, No. 202, § 4979. P. L. § 4764. G. L. § 4411. P. S. § 3831. V. S. § 3310. R. L. § 2936. 

G. S. 24, § 34. R. S. 20, § 18. 1820, p. 20. 72 Vt. 419. 

5036. Commissioners' duties; oath. The commissioners shall be sworn to make an impartial appraisal, shall give at least six days' notice of the time of making the same to one or more of the selectmen, and, at the time appointed, shall view the premises, make the appraisal, and make return as directed in their commission, with a statement of their time and expenses. 

P. L. § 4765. G. L. § 4412. P. S. § 3832. V. S. § 3311. R. L. § 2937. G. S. 24, § 35. 

R. S. 20, § 19. 1820, p. 21. R. 1797, p. 347, § 1. R. 1787, p. 78. 22 Vt. 131. 72 Vt. 419. 

5037. Same; report. The magistrate or the county court to which the return is made, shall establish the appraisal unless cause is shown to the contrary, and fix the time within which it shall be paid; but the county court may accept or reject the report, in whole or in part, or recommit it to the same or new commissioners, with such instructions as justice requires. 

P. L. § 4766. G. L. § 4413. P. S. § 3833. V. S. § 3312. R. L. § 2938. 1866, No. 26.

5038. Costs. When the appraisal by commissioners is more than was offered by the selectmen, the petitioner may recover costs; when less, costs shall be

allowed to the town, and the magistrate or court may award execution to carry into effect any judgment rendered upon such report.

5039. Petition. When a person owning or interested in lands through which a highway is laid out, altered or resurveyed by selectmen, is dissatisfied with the laying out, altering or resurveying of such highway, or with the compensation for damages, he may apply by petition in writing to the county court in the same county. Any number of persons aggrieved may join in the petition. Such petition shall be brought within one year after order of the selectmen on such highway is recorded.

5040. Service appointment of commissioners. The petition with a citation shall be served on one or more of the selectmen within twenty-one days from the date thereof. The court shall appoint three disinterested freeholders as commissioners, to inquire into the convenience and necessity of such highway and the manner in which it has been laid out, altered or resurveyed and as to the damages sustained by the petitioner.

5041. Commissioners' report; judgment on report. The commissioners shall give notice to one or more of the selectmen and to the petitioners of the time when and place where they will make such inquiry, and hear the parties. When they have completed their inquiries, they shall make report to the court. Upon hearing, the court may accept or reject the report in whole or in part, and by its order or decree may establish, alter, resurvey or discontinue the highway, as appears just, and may render judgment for the petitioners for such damages as they have severally sustained. When the sum so allowed is greater than was offered by the selectmen, the court may tax costs for the petitioners, or for either party, as is just.

5042. Opening of highway stayed; damages. When such application is made to the county court, the opening of the highway shall be stayed until the decision of the court. The court may fix the time when it shall be opened, as provided in this chapter, and for paying damages awarded. When the damages are not paid within the time limited, the court may award execution therefor.

Proceedings in County Court on Refusal of Selectmen to Act. 

5043. Petition. When, upon application as provided in this chapter, the selectmen do not lay out, alter or discontinue a highway, or when a highway is laid out by selectmen and is not built and opened for use within three years, or if discontinued by the selectmen before it is built and opened, but in no other case, three or more freeholders of the town or vicinity may apply by petition in writing to the county court for any of such purposes.

5045. Court may refuse to appoint commissioners. When, within five years from the date of such petition, two prior petitions have been brought asking for the laying of a highway over substantially the same location, or a portion thereof, and commissioners appointed thereon whose report in each case, adverse to the laying of such highway, had been accepted, the court may, in its discretion, refuse to appoint commissioners and dismiss the petition.

5046. Hearing by commissioners. The commissioners shall appoint a time and place for examination and hearing, give notice thereof to one or more of the selectmen, and to persons owning or interested in the lands through which the highway may be laid, examine the premises, and inquire into the convenience and necessity of such highway.

The expenses of making such survey shall be included in the commissioners' accounts and allowed. by the court.

5048. Petitioners especially benefited; expense apportioned. When the commissioners are of opinion that the petitioner or some of them will be especially benefited, they may make their decision to lay out such highway conditional upon the payment by the petitioners so benefited of such sums as the commissioners may deem just towards the expense of laying out and building such highway, and shall include a statement of the names of the parties benefited and the amount apportioned to each, in their report to the county court.

5051. Who may defend.

The town or any person interested may appear and resist the acceptance of the report by showing any facts relevant.

5052. Orders on report; costs. For sufficient reasons, the court may reject or accept the report, in whole or in part, and by its order or decree establish, alter or discontinue the highway, in whole or in part, and render judgment against the town in favor of the persons interested in the lands, for the damages they have severally sustained. If the prayer of the petition is wholly granted, or wholly rejected, such court shall tax costs accordingly, or if otherwise, may tax costs as appears just, and may issue execution for such damages and costs.

Highway or Bridge on Line Between Two Towns. 

62 Vt. 106. 

5054. By agreement. The selectmen of two adjoining towns may, by agreement, lay out a highway on the line between such towns, or erect a bridge over a stream between such towns, if a majority of the selectmen of each town assent thereto.

P. L. § 4783. G. L. § 4430. P. S. § 3850. V. S. § 3329. R. L. § 2952. G. S. 24, § 36. 

R. S. 20, § 20. 1824, p. 30. R. 1797, p. 355, § 14, 70 Vt. 278. 

5055. Expense; apportionment. When such highway is or has been previously laid out on the line between the towns, they may agree as to what part of the highway shall be built and repaired, and what share of the damages paid by each town. When a bridge is so erected, they may agree upon the proportion which each town shall pay towards making and keeping the same in repair. 

P. L. § 4784. G. L. § 4431. P. S. § 3851. V. S. § 3330. R. L. § 2953. G. S. 24, § 36. 

R. S. 20, § 20. 1824, p. 29. R. 1797, p. 355, § 14. 

70 Vt. 278. 

5056. Petition. When the public good or convenience of the inhabitants requires a highway to be laid out, altered or discontinued on, or intersecting the line between two towns, or a bridge across a stream between two towns to be built, seven or more freeholders of the towns or vicinity in case of a highway, or twenty or more such freeholders in case of a bridge, may petition, in writing, the selectmen of such towns to lay out, alter or discontinue such highway, or build such bridge. 

1939, No. 108, § 1. P. L. § 4785. 

G. S. 24, § 37. R. S. 20, § 21.

4 Vt. 224. 24 Vt. 176. 65 Vt. 116. 70 Vt. 278. 

5057. Hearing; notice. The selectmen to whom such petition is first presented, shall notify the selectmen of the other town, and the selectmen of such towns shall appoint a time of hearing and give notice thereof. 

P. L. § 4786. G. L. § 4433. P. S. § 3853. V. S. § 3332. R. L. § 2955. G. S. 24, § 38. 

R. S. 20, § 22. 

70 Vt. 278. 

5058. Proceedings by selectmen. The selectmen shall proceed in the same manner as the selectmen of one town in laying out highways or building bridges. 

Their doings, or a duplicate thereof, shall be returned to the clerk of each town within six months from the time of final hearing on such application, and their order and surveys shall be recorded in each town clerk's office, and their doings shall be binding on their respective towns. 

P. L. § 4787. G. L. § 4434. P. S. § 3854. V. S. § 3333. R. L. § 2956. G. S. 24, §§ 39, 56. 

1857, No. 27. R. S. 20, § 23. 70 Vt. 278. 

5059. Rights and liabilities of each town. Each town shall have the rights, and be subject to the liabilities, in relation to the part of such highway or bridge located in such town, as though the same were located wholly therein. 

P. L. § 4788. G. L. § 4435. P. S. § 3855. V. S. § 3334. R. L. § 2957. G. S. 24, § 39. 

R. S. 20, § 23. 1824, p. 30. 70 Vt. 278.

5060. Highway near but not on line between towns; proceedings.

When the public good or the convenience of the inhabitants requires a highway to be laid out or altered, so that it shall be near the line between two towns, instead of on such line, on account of the position of the land or nature of the soil over which it is laid, and, when both towns are benefited in a similar manner as though the highway were on such line, the highway may be laid out or altered by the selectmen or the county court, on the report of commissioners. The damages and expenses of making and repairing such highway may be apportioned, and the same proceedings had as provided in case of laying out and altering highways on the line between two towns.

P. L. 4789. G. L. § 4436. P. S. § 3856. V. S. § 3335. R. L. § 2958. G. S. 24, § 47. 

1845, No. 21. 64 Vt. 28. 70 Vt. 278. 

5061. Refusal of selectmen to act; petition, procedure. When, upon application as provided in this chapter, the selectmen of such towns do not lay out, alter or discontinue a highway on or near a line between two towns, seven or more freeholders of such towns or vicinity may apply to the county court and the court, by commissioners, may make inquiry and render judgment and the same proceedings shall be had as in case of a highway extending into or through two or more towns. By its order, the court shall direct which part of such highway each town shall make and repair, and what damages shall be paid by each, if a highway is made or altered.

P. L. & 4790. G. L. § 4437. P. S. § 3857. V. S. § 3336. R. L. § 2959. G. S. 24, § 54. 

R. S. 20, § 35. 1824, p. 30. 24 Vt. 176. 59 Vt. 632. 70 Vt. 278. 

5062. Town not assessed for highway in another town; exception. A town shall not be assessed towards the expense of building or maintaining any highway in another town, except as provided in the two preceding sections. 

P. L. § 4791. G. L. § 4438. P. S. § 3858. V. S. § 3337. 1884, No. 18, § 1. 64 Vt. 28. 

70 Vt. 278. 

5063. Petition on failure to report. When application is made to selectmen to erect a bridge over a stream between two towns, and they do not report within the time herein before provided, or deny the petition, twenty or more freeholders of the vicinity may apply by petition to the county court. 

1945, No. 29, § 49. P. L. § 4792. G. L. § 4439. P. S. § 3859. V. S. § 3338. R. L. § 2960. 

G. S. 24, § 56. 1857, No. 27. R. S. 20, § 36. 1834, No. 16. 

4 Vt. 224. 64 Vt.28. 

5064. Same; service; commissioners. Such petition with a citation shall be served on one or more of the selectmen of each town within twenty-one days from the date thereof. On the return of the petition, the court shall appoint as commissioners three disinterested freeholders of neighboring towns. 

1945, No. 29, § 50. P. L. § 4793. G. L. § 4440. P. S. § 3860. V. S. § 3339. R. L. § 2961. 

G. S. 24, §§ 56, 57. R. S. 20, §§ 36, 37. 1834, No. 16. 

5065. Hearing; notice. The commissioners shall give notice to one or more of the selectmen of each town of the time when and place where they will hear parties interested. They shall inquire into the convenience and necessity of such bridge, its location, the need of laying out or altering a highway in connection therewith, and the proportion of the expense of building and repairing the bridge to be borne by each town.

P. L. § 4794. G. L. § 4441. P. S. § 3861. V. S. § 3340. R. L. § 2962. G. S. 24, § 57. 

R. S. 20, § 37. 1834, No. 16. 

5066. Survey; assessment of damages. When the commissioners decide that the bridge shall be built and that a highway on either side is required, they shall cause such highway to be surveyed, and may assess damages to the owners of land as in other cases of laying out highways. 

P. L. § 4795. G. L. 4442. P. S. § 3862. V. S. § 3341. R. L. § 2963. G. S. 24, § 57. 

R. S. 20, § 37. 

5067. Report, filing. When the commissioners complete their inquiries, they shall report their doings and decision, with a survey of the highway they find nec-

essary, and their appraisal of damages. They shall cause their report to be filed in the office of the clerk of the court within thirty days thereafter. 

1945, No. 29, § 51. P. L. § 4796. G. L. § 4443. P. S. § 3863. V. S. § 3342. R. L. § 2965. 

G. S. 24, § 59. R. S. 20, § 39. 1834, No. 16. 

5068. Same; procedure. The towns assessed towards the expense of building or repairing such bridge may appear and resist the acceptance of the report by showing any facts relevant. The court may reject the report, or accept it, in whole or in part, and may establish and order the building of such bridge, and such highway leading to it as may be necessary, and settle the proportion which each town shall contribute to the expense of building or repairing such bridge, and render judgment for the damages sustained by persons interested in lands through which such highway is laid, as in other cases, and for cost as is just. 

P. L. § 4797. G. L. § 4444. P. S. § 3864. V. S. § 3343. R. L. § 2966. G. S. 24, §§ 59, 60. 

R. S. 20, §§ 39, 40. 1834, No. 16. 1826, No. 21. 4 Vt. 224. 31 Vt. 153. 

5069. Reapportionment. A town dissatisfied with or deeming itself oppressed by the order apportioning the expense of building or repairing a bridge between two towns, after five years from the making of the order, and once in every five years thereafter, may apply to the court making the order for a new apportionment, and the same notice shall be given and proceedings had as in the original apportionment. Upon the report of the commissioners the court shall make such order as it deems just. 

P. L. § 4798. G. L. § 4445. P. S. § 3865. V. S. § 3344. 1884, No. 18, § 5. R. L. § 2978. 

G. S. 24, §§ 62, 65. 1848, No. 39. 1847, No. 27. R. S. 20, § 42. 1816, p. 84. 58 Vt. 212. 

64 Vt. 28. 

5070. Commissioner for repairing bridge; duties. In its discretion, the court may appoint a commissioner for a time, not exceeding five years, who shall have charge of building or repairing such bridge and under whose direction the moneys appropriated therefor, under the two preceding sections, shall be expended. 

The court shall order the towns to pay the commissioner the sums of money assessed against them, for building or repairing such bridge. 

P. L. § 4799.

V. S. § 3345. 

G. L. § 4446. 1917, No. 254, § 4353. 1915, No. 32, § 8. P. S. § 3866. 

R. L. §§ 2980, 2981. 1874, No. 52, §§ 1, 2. 1868, No. 9, §§ 1, 2. 

5071. Same; accounts. The commissioner shall render annually to the court an account of his doings and expenditures in the matter. Such account shall be audited and adjusted by the court upon notice to the several towns interested therein. If, upon such accounting, moneys or property is found in his hands, he shall refund the same to the several towns according to their respective interests. 

P. L. § 4800. G. L. § 4447. 1917, No. 254, § 4354. 1915, No. 32, § 9. P. S. § 3867. 

V. S. 3346. R. L. § 2982. 1874, No. 52, § 3. 1868, No. 9, § 3. 

5072. Same; removal. The court may remove at any time the commissioner and appoint another.

P. L. § 4801. G. L. § 4448. P. S. § 3868. V. S. § 3347. R. L. § 2983. 1874, No. 52, § 4. 

5073. Same; bond. The commissioner shall give a bond in such sum and with such sureties as the court requires, to each of the towns interested as aforesaid, conditioned for the faithful performance of his duties, and for the repayment of such moneys or property as upon such accounting are found in his hands. 

P. L. § 4802. G. L. § 4449. P. S. § 3869. V. S. § 3348. R. L. § 2984. 1874, No. 52, § 5. 

1868, No. 9, § 4. 

5074. Petition by selectmen. When a bridge is required between two towns and the selectmen of the towns do not agree to build the same, application by petition may be made to the county court by the selectmen of either town. The petition and citation shall be served on one or more of the selectmen of the other town, and the same proceedings shall be had as though application had been made by twenty or more freeholders of the vicinity. 

P. L. & 4803. 

R. S. 20, § 41.

G. L. § 4451. P. S. § 3871. V. S. § 3350. R. L. § 2967. G. S. 24, § 61. 

R. 1797, p. 355, § 14.

Petition to Supreme Court for Highways and Bridges. 

5075. When towns in different counties; proceedings. An application to lay out, alter or discontinue a highway extending into or through two or more towns, or on the line between two towns, or for building a bridge between two towns, where such towns lie in different counties, shall be made to the supreme court, which shall have the same power, and shall proceed in the same manner as the county court, as when the towns lie in the same county. 

P. L. § 4804. 

R. S. 20, § 43. 

G. L. § 4452. P. S. § 3872. V. S. § 3351. R. L. § 2968. G. S. 24, § 64. 

1820, p. 22. 45 Vt. 33. 67 Vt. 114. 68 Vt. 168. 93 Vt. 102. 

Highways Through Two or More Towns. 

5076. Application; proceedings. When the public good requires a highway to be laid out, altered or discontinued, extending into or through two or more towns in the same county, seven or more freeholders of such towns, or the vicinity, may apply to the county court. Such court, by commissioners appointed for that purpose, may make inquiry and render judgment, as when the highway is located in only one town. The same proceedings shall be had, except that the notices shall be given to the selectmen of each town. 

P. L. § 4805. G. L. § 4453. P. S. § 3873. V. S. 

R. S. 20, § 34. 1825, No. 11, § 4. 1820, p. 22. 

55 Vt. 490. 56 Vt. 53. 57 Vt. 569. 59 Vt. 632. 

§ 3352. R. L. § 2969. G. S. 24, § 52. 

16 Vt. 510. 42 Vt. 651. 46 Vt. 504. 

68 Vt. 234. 93 Vt. 102. 

5077. Separate judgment against towns; costs. When judgment is rendered against the towns for damages or costs, it shall be against them separately for such damages and costs as each is made liable to pay. When the road prayed for is not laid out in any one of the towns, such town shall recover its costs. The petitioners shall not be entitled to recover in their bill of costs the expenses of examining such part as is not laid out. 

P. L. § 4806. G. L. § 4454. P. S. § 3874. V. S. § 3353. R. L. § 2970. G. S. 24, § 52. 

R. S. 20, § 34. 68 Vt. 234. 

Grading and Graveling Highways. 

5078. Powers of selectmen. Selectmen shall have the same power to order hills graded, surfaces graveled, treated with a dust layer, or surface treated with bituminous material, upon any highway either laid out by them or already existing, that they have by this chapter to lay out, alter or discontinue highways. 

1931, No. 62, § 1. G. L. § 4456. P. S. § 3877. V. S. § 3356. R. L. § 2972.. 

68 Vt. 234.

P. L. § 4807. 

1869, No. 19. 

5079. Change of grade; notice. The selectmen or road commissioner shall not alter a highway, by cutting down or raising the roadbed in front of a dwelling house or other building standing upon the line of such highway more than three feet without first giving notice to the owner thereof, of a time when they will examine the premises, and hear such owner upon the question of making such alteration and the damages by reason thereof. 

P. L. § 4808. G. L. § 4457. P. S. § 3878. V. S. § 3357. 1884, No. 15, § 1. 68 Vt. 168. 

75 Vt. 221. 87 Vt. 479. 91 Vt. 343. 93 Vt. 8. 114 Vt. 41. 

5080. Damages. When the selectmen are of opinion that the public good or the necessity or convenience of individuals requires that such roadbed be altered by lowering or raising the same more than three feet, they may order such alteration to be made. If they are of opinion that the owners will sustain damage by reason of such alteration, they shall determine and award the amount thereof to the owners, respectively, taking into account, by way of set-off thereto, such special benefit, if any, as shall accrue to them by reason of such alteration. 

P. L. § 4809. G. L. § 4458. P. S. § 3879. V. S. § 3358. 1884, No. 15, § 2. 75 Vt. 221. 

114 Vt. 14. 

5081. Order of selectmen; record. The selectmen shall make their order and award for damages, or refusal to award damages to such owner, in writing,

and shall cause the same to be recorded in the office of the clerk of the town in which such highway is situated within ten days from the time of making the same. 

P. L. § 4810. G. L. § 4459. P. S. § 3880. V. S. § 3359. 1884, No. 15, § 3. 

114 Vt. 41. 

5082. Same; petition; commissioners. When an owner is dissatisfied with the amount of damages awarded, or with the refusal to award damages, he may petition, in writing, a municipal judge or a justice of the county residing in an adjoining town, for the appointment of commissioners to appraise such damages, and any number of such owners thus dissatisfied may join in such petition. Such petition shall be made to such magistrate within sixty days after the selectmen's award or refusal to award damages has been recorded in such town clerk's office, but such petition shall not delay the making of such alteration in such roadbed. 

P. L. § 4811. G. L. § 4460. 1908, No. 62. P. S. § 3881. V. S. § 3360. 1884, No. 15, § 4. 

72 Vt. 419. 73 Vt. 124. 114 Vt. 41. 

5083. Same; proceedings. When such petition has been filed with such magistrate, the same proceedings shall be had, and the petitioners and town shall be entitled to the same rights and subject to the same liabilities as provided in this chapter in proceedings before a municipal judge or justice of the county to assess damages caused by laying out a highway. 

P. L. § 4812. G. L. § 4461. 1908, No. 62. P. S. § 3882. V. S. § 3361. 1884, No. 15, § 5. 

72 Vt. 419. 114 Vt. 41. 

5084. Duties of commissioners appointed by the supreme or county court. Commissioners appointed by the supreme or county court to build or repair a bridge, or lay out, alter or discontinue a highway, may order the town or towns, in or through which they lay out or alter a highway, to grade the hills thereon to such inclination as they deem proper, and to gravel such portions thereof as they deem necessary. The commissioners shall estimate the expense thereof and report to the court the order so made and the estimates. The court may accept or reject the report in whole or in part and order such town or towns to gravel such portions of such highway and to grade the hills in such manner and to such extent as it deems proper. 

P. L. § 4813. G. L. § 4462. P. S. § 3883. V. S. § 3362. R. L. § 2973. 1867, No. 28, § 1. 

G. S. 24, § 66. 1848, No. 32, § 1. 68 Vt. 234. 

5085. Powers of supreme and county courts. Upon petition for that purpose as in case of laying out, altering or discontinuing a highway, and by similar proceedings, the supreme or county court may order hills to be graded and surfaces to be graveled on existing highways, as provided in the preceding section in case of new highways.

P. L. § 4814. G. L. § 4463. P. S. § 3884. V. S. § 3363. R. L. § 2974. 1869, No. 19. 

68 Vt. 234.

Towns Assessed in Certain Cases May Petition for Relief. 

5086. Petition; proceedings. A town assessed towards the expense of maintaining or repairing a bridge or highway in another town, under number 16 of the acts of 1882, may petition the court which ordered the assessment. The petition shall be served on the town to which the assessment is payable like a writ of summons and the court shall thereupon vacate the assessment. Whenever any such petition is brought, the court shall, upon application, order a stay of proceedings to enforce the collection or payment of any such assessment. 

P. L. § 4815. G. L. § 4471. P. S. § 3892. V. S. § 3371. 1894, No. 81, § 1. 

1886, No. 16. 

1884, No. 11. 1884, No. 18. 1882, No. 16. 61 Vt. 126. 64 Vt. 28. 

5087. Former judgment not a bar. A former judgment on proceedings for relief under section 6 of number 18 of the acts of 1884 shall not bar the relief provided for by the preceding section. 

P. L. § 4816. G. L. § 4472. P. S. § 3893. V. S. § 3372. 1894, No. 81, § 2. 

64 Vt. 28. 

61 Vt. 126.

Time for Opening of Road and Payment of Damages. 

5088. Limitations. When the supreme or county court orders a highway to be laid out or altered, it shall fix the time within which owners of lands taken shall remove their buildings, fences, timber, wood and trees, and open such land for working, and the time when it shall be opened to be occupied; which time, without consent of the owner, shall not be less than two months, nor less than six months if the lands taken have buildings thereon. The court may order sections of a highway to be completed in different times as circumstances may require, but the whole period shall not be less than one year. When the court orders the building of a bridge, it shall fix the time within which it shall be completed, which shall not be less than six months.

P. L. § 4817. G. L. § 4473. P. S. § 3894. 1902, No. 59, § 1. 1900, No. 49, § 2. V. S. § 3373. 

R. L. § 2985. G. S. 24, § 68. R. S. 20, § 44. 1832, No. 8. 

5089. Extension of time. The selectmen of a town in which a highway or bridge is ordered to be built may apply by petition to the court making the order, to extend the time for completing the same. Notice thereof shall be given to one or more of the original petitioners for the highway or bridge, and one or more of the selectmen of any other town, in which the highway or bridge is partly located, by serving on them the petition, with a citation for that purpose, at least twelve days before the term of the court to which the petition is returnable, and the court may extend the time as the circumstances of the case require. 

P. L. § 4818. G. L. § 4474. P. S. § 3895. V. S. § 3374. R. L. § 2986. G. S. 24, § 69. 

R. S. 20, § 45. 1835, No. 17. 

5090. Damages; time of payment. When a court orders damages to be paid to a person for injury done to his lands by laying out or altering a highway, it may allow the town a reasonable time to pay the damages, not exceeding the time allowed for opening the highway. If the sum allowed is not paid within the time limited, the clerk of the court shall issue execution for the same. 

P. L. § 4819. G. L. § 4475. P. S. § 3896. V. S. § 3375. R. L. § 2987. G. S. 24, § 70. 

R. S. 20, § 46. 42 Vt. 651. 

New Trial on the Question of Damages. 

5091. New trial; application. When a person owning or interested in lands through which a highway is laid out is dissatisfied with the sum allowed as damages by the court, and if he had been prevented by absence or want of notice, or otherwise, from having a fair trial before the commissioners or before the court, or if new facts have appeared to change the amount of injury, he may apply by petition to the county court, and any number of persons may join in the petition. 

P. L. § 4820. G. L. § 4476. P. S. § 3897. V. S. § 2988. G. S. 24, § 71. R. S. 20, § 47. 

1835, No. 18, § 1. 16 Vt. 415. 73 Vt. 124. 

5092. Same; notice; commissioners. Notice shall be given to one or more of the selectmen of the town in which the lands are located by service of the petition and a citation at any time previous to the expiration of sixty days after the highway is laid open to be worked. The court may appoint commissioners, as in case of an application for laying out a highway. 

1945, No. 29, § 52. P. L. § 4821. G. L. § 4477. P. S. § 3898. V. S. § 3377. R. L. § 2989. 

G. S. 24, § 71. R. S. 20, § 47. 1835, No. 18, § 1. 

16 Vt. 415. 

5093. Proceedings. The commissioners shall notify the petitioners and one or more of the selectmen of each town in interest of the time and place of hearing, and shall examine the premises and the circumstances of the case, and make a new appraisal. On their report, the court may set aside the former assessment of damages and render judgment for such sums as appear just. If the sum is greater than the sum before allowed, the court may tax costs for the petitioner, otherwise the towns shall recover their costs.

P. L. § 4822. G. L. § 4478. P. S. § 3899. V. S. § 3378. R. L. § 2990. G. S. 24, § 72. 

R. S. 20, § 48. 

1835, No. 18, § 2.

Discontinuance of Highways. 

5094. Procedure, notice. The selectmen may discontinue a highway or bridge wholly within the town unless it shall be made to appear that the same was originally laid out under authority of the county or supreme court. The selectmen, before discontinuing a highway or bridge, shall appoint a time for examining the premises and hearing parties interested. They shall give notice thereof by posting notice in two or more public places in the town of the time and place of such hearing and shall give at least twelve days' notice in writing of the time and place of such hearing to all persons owning or interested in land abutting such highway or bridge, such notice to be by registered mail to the last known address of such person. A county court, by commissioners, may discontinue a highway or bridge wholly in the county, except one laid out by order of the supreme court. The supreme court may discontinue a highway or bridge laid out under the order of such court or which extends into or through two or more counties; and such highways or bridges may be discontinued whether they have been built or not. The commissioners appointed to discontinue a highway or bridge shall be disinterested freeholders, and other than those appointed to lay out the highway or bridge. A decree or order made under the provisions of this section may be reviewed by the county or supreme court under the same conditions and the same proceedings shall be had thereon as are provided for the laying out of highways. 

1935, No. 111, § 2. P. L. § 4823. 1931, No. 63, § 1. G. L. § 4479. P. S. § 3900. 

1896, No. 69, § 1. V. S. § 3379. R. L. § 2991. G. S. 24, § 73. 1845, No. 22. 

R. S. 20, § 49. 1832, No. 9. 1825, No. 11, §§ 3, 4, 5. 1813, p. 165. 

52 Vt. 77. 101 Vt. 181. 

8 Vt. 223. 

5095. Notice; costs. When application by petition is made to the county or supreme court to discontinue a highway laid by commissioners appointed by either of such courts, which has not been built agreeably to the orders of such court, the petition shall be served on one or more of the original petitioners for the laying of such highway, as well as on one or more of the selectmen of the town or towns through which the highway is laid, or the same, on motion, shall be dismissed. If commissioners are appointed who report adversely to the prayer of the petition, the original petitioners shall, in the discretion of the court, be entitled to costs. 

P. L. § 4824. G. L. § 4480. P. S. § 3901. V. S. § 3380. R. L. § 2992. 1868, No. 10. 

5096. County highways. The selectmen may discontinue such portions of the old county highways laid out by county or county court commissioners as the public good requires, when such highways have not been in use for one year or have been abandoned by reason of new highways.

P. L. § 4825. G. L. § 4481. P. S. § 3902. V. S. § 3381. R. L. § 2993. 1870, No. 53. 

5097. Highways ordered by general assembly. Selectmen may alter or discontinue a highway laid out by a committee appointed by the general assembly; but if the highway is laid through two or more towns, the same proceedings shall be had as in laying, altering or discontinuing highways through two or more towns. 

P. L. § 4826. G. L. § 4482. P. S. § 3903. V. S. § 3382. R. L. § 2994. G. S. 24, § 74. 

1842, No. 10. 

5098. Title to discontinued highway. When a highway is discontinued, it shall be set and belong to the owners of the adjoining lands; if it is located between the lands of two different owners, it shall be set to the lots to which it originally belonged, if they can be ascertained; if not, it shall be equally divided between the owners of the lands on each side. 

P. L. § 4827. G. L. § 4483. P. S. § 3904. V. S. § 3383. R. L. § 2995. G. S. 24, § 75. 

R. S. 20, § 50. 1813, p. 165. 

7 Vt. 514. 

5099. Assessment of damages, when void. When a public highway is laid out, and the damages are assessed to the owners of the land, and the highway is

legally discontinued before being worked or opened, the assessment of damages shall become void, and an action shall not be maintained thereon. 

P. L. § 4828. G. L. § 4484. P. S. § 3905. V. S. § 3384. R. L. § 2996. G. S. 24, § 82. 

42 Vt. 651. 

5100. Recovery of actual damages. The owner of the land over which such discontinued highway was laid may recover of the town the actual damages sustained by him in consequence of laying out the highway, in an action of tort on this statute. Such damages shall be fixed and allowed by the board discontinuing the road, at the time of discontinuance, and the same proceedings may be had in fixing the damages as in the assessment of land damages upon the laying out of a highway. 

P. L. § 4829. G. L. § 4485. P. S. § 3906. V. S. § 3385. R. L. § 2997. G. S. 24, § 82. 

5101. Recovery of assessments. When such land damages are paid before the highway is discontinued, the town may recover of the landowner in an action of contract on this statute, the amount so paid, deducting therefrom the damages which have actually accrued to him in consequence of laying out the highway, to be fixed and assessed as provided in the preceding section. 

P. L. § 4830. G. L. § 4486. P. S. § 3907. V. S. § 3386. R. L. § 2998. G. S. 24, § 83. 

42 Vt. 651. 

Neglect of Town to Build Highways or Bridges. 

5102. Fine; expenditure. When a town does not perform the order of the supreme or county court as to building a highway or building a bridge, such court may, on application, fine the town in a sum sufficient to build and complete the highway or bridge. The fine shall be expended in building the highway or bridge under the direction of a commissioner to be appointed by the court. 

P. L. § 4831. G. L. § 4487. P. S. § 3908. V. S. § 3387. R. L. § 2999. G. S. 25, § 49. 

1856, No. 32, § 1. 1850, No. 32. R. S. 21, § 28. 1821, p. 82. 1818, p. 100. 1806, p. 87. 

2 Vt. 548. 12 Vt. 422. 40 Vt. 121. 81 Vt. 549, 

5103. Application; service of citation. Such application may be made by one or more of the original petitioners for the highway or bridge, within three years from the expiration of the time prescribed by such order for the performance thereof, and, with a citation, shall be served on one of the selectmen of the town to be proceeded against, within twenty-one days from the date thereof, notifying the town to appear and show cause why the prayer of the application should not be granted. 

1945, No. 29, § 53. P. L. § 4832. G. L. § 4488. 1908, No. 62. P. S. § 3909. V. S. § 3388. 

R. L. § 3000. G. S. 25, § 50. 1856, No. 32, § 2. 12 Vt. 422. 40 Vt. 121. 

5104. Trial by jury; costs. Such town may have a trial by jury as to its neglect to perform the order of the court, and the court may award costs to either party in its discretion.

P. L. 4833. G. L. § 4489. P. S. § 3910. V. S. § 3389. R. L. § 3001. G. S. 25, §§ 51, 52. 

1856, No. 32, §§ 3, 4. 

5105. Fine for not building bridge; expenditure. When a fine is assessed for failure to build a bridge between two towns such fine and costs shall be paid by the several towns in the proportion in which they were assessed for the building of the bridge; but, in making the apportionment, the court may take into consideration what has already been paid by any town. The court may issue execution against each town for its proportion only; and the fine shall be expended under the direction of a commissioner appointed for the purpose of building or completing the bridge.

P. L. § 4834. G. L. § 4490. P. S. § 3911. V. S. § 3390. R. L. § 3002. G. S. 25, § 48. 

R. S. 21, § 29. 1834, No. 11. 

5106. Discontinuance; suspension of fine. When a highway or bridge established by a court is discontinued, in whole or in part, before the fine assessed
