upon the town is expended according to the order of the court, on application for that purpose by the town fined, the court may order the same, in whole or in part, to be expended in building any highway or bridge, which such town is liable to make or build and may suspend the collection of the fine, in whole or in part, in its discretion.

P. L. 4835. G. L. § 4491. P. S. § 3912. V. S. § 3391. R. L. § 3003. G. S. 25, § 47. 

1850, No. 32.

Miscellaneous Provisions. 

5107. Enclosure and occupation; gates and bars. The selectmen may allow pent roads and trails to be enclosed and occupied by the owner of the land during any part of the year, and stiles, gates and bars in such places as they designate, to be built thereon, and such permission and alterations therein shall be in writing and recorded in the office of the town clerk. 

P. L. § 4836. 1921, No. 121, § 3. G. L. § 4492. P. S. § 3913. V. S. § 3392. 

G. S. 24. §§3, 4. 1854, No. 39. R. S. 20, § 3. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 9. 

40 Vt. 40. 58 Vt. 567. 67 Vt. 102. 67 Vt. 132. 114 Vt. 1. 

R. L. § 3004. 

1813, p. 165. 

5108. Hitching posts. At the expense of the town, the selectmen shall erect hitching posts on each side of the gates or bars on a pent road. 

G. L. § 4494. 

1917, No. 254, § 4401. P. S. § 3915. V. S. § 3394. 

1858, No. 23, § 1. 

P. L. § 4837. 

R. L. § 3006. G. S. 24, § 6. 

5109. Gates and bars; penalty for removal. A person who wilfully removes any such gate or bars, thereby exposing the land or crops of any person to damage, shall forfeit not more than $5.00, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, by any person interested in the lands, and shall be also liable to the owner of the lands or crops for such damage. 

P. L. § 4838. G. L. § 4493. P. S. § 3914. 

1854, No. 39. 51 Vt. 544. 53 Vt. 364. 

V. S. § 3393. 

67 Vt. 102.

R. L. § 3005. G. S. 24, § 7. 

114 Vt. 1. 

5110. Removal of obstructions. When they lay out a pent road, in their order, the selectmen shall require the owner of the land, within a reasonable time, to remove his fences, timber, wood or trees; but the order shall not take ffect until compensation for damages to the land is paid, as provided in this chapter. 

P. L. § 4839. G. L. § 4495. P. S. § 3916. V. S. § 3395. R. L. § 3007. G. S. 24, § 5. 

1851, No. 25. 

Winter Roads and Roads for the Removal of Lumber. 

5111. Opening. When a public highway used for travel is so obstructed by snow that travel thereon is impracticable, the selectmen of the town, in their discretion, immediately may lay out, prepare and open for travel, a winter road in place of such highway. Such highway shall be continued only during the winter in which it is laid out. 

P. L. § 4840. G. L. §§ 4496, 4498. P. S. §§ 3917, 3919. V. S. §§ 3396, 3398. 

R. L. §§ 3008, 3010, 3011. G. S. 24, §§ 8, 10, 11. 1856, No. 35, §§ 1, 3, 4. 

5112. Damages. On opening a winter highway the selectmen shall forthwith give notice to the owners of the land through which it passes, and, upon notice and hearing, shall award to such owners reasonable damages occasioned by the highway. When the damages are accepted, the selectmen shall draw orders therefor. 

The owners may appeal from the award as in other cases of laying out highways by selectmen.

P. L. § 4841. G. L. § 4497. P. S. § 3918. V. S. § 3397. R. L. § 3009. G. S. 24, §§ 9, 12. 

1856, No. 35, §§ 2, 5. 

5113. Record of proceedings; continuance of road. The selectmen forthwith shall cause their doings in laying out and awarding damages for a winter highway, certified by them, to be recorded in the office of the town clerk. 

P. L. § 4842. G. L. § 4498. P. S. § 3919. V. S. § 3398. R. L. §§ 3010, 3011. 

G. S. 24, §§ 10, 11. 1856, No. 35, §§ 3, 4.

5114. Preparation for winter highway. When the selectmen are of the opinion that a winter highway is likely to be needed, they may cause preparation to be made, and, upon notice to and hearing of each landowner affected thereby, shall award him reasonable damages. When the landowner is dissatisfied with the award, he may appeal as in case of appraisal of damages by selectmen in laying out highways. 

P. L. § 4843. G. L. § 4499. P. S. § 3920. V. S. § 3399. R. L. § 3012. G. S. 24, § 13. 

1857, No. 26. 

5115. For the removal of lumber; damages. When it becomes necessary for the convenient removal of lumber, wood or other material, to pass through the lands of a person other than the owner of the land from which such lumber, wood or other material is to be removed, the selectmen, in their discretion, may lay out a right of way through the land of any person for the purposes aforesaid, and, upon notice to and hearing of the owner of the lands, shall determine the necessity for and assess the damages occasioned by the laying out of such right of way, and such damages shall be paid by the person applying for such right of way before the same shall be open for use. A party aggrieved by the action of the selectmen may appeal by way of petition to the county court as provided in case of the refusal of selectmen to lay out, alter or discontinue highways. 

P. L. § 4844. G. L. § 4500. 

V. S. 3400. R. L. § 3013. 

1917, No. 117, § 1. P. S. § 3921. 1904, No. 82, § 1. 

1880, No. 65, § 1. 

5116. Use; discontinuance. The selectmen may fix the length of time such right of way may be used and may order the same closed or discontinued when in their judgment it is necessary.

P. L. § 4845. G. L. § 4502. P. S. § 3923. 1904, No. 82, § 2. V. S. § 3402. R. L. § 3015. 

1880, No. 65, § 3. 

Removing or Building Fences to Prevent Snowdrifts. 

5117. Fences laid down. When a road commissioner judges that a highway is liable to be obstructed by snowdrifts, and the fences adjoining the highway can be conveniently laid down, he may cause any portion thereof to be laid down during the winter.

P. L. § 4846. G. L. § 4503. P. S. § 3924. V. S. § 3403. R. L. § 3017. 1868, No. 11, § 1. 

1867, No. 29. 

5118. Same; damages. The selectmen of the town where a fence is so laid down, if requested by parties interested, shall give reasonable notice to all such parties of a time and place of hearing, and assess reasonable damages occasioned to a person by taking down and putting up the fence, and draw orders on the town treasurer therefor.

P. L. § 4847. G. L. § 4504. P. S. § 3925. V. S. § 3404. R. L. § 3018. 1868, No. 11, § 2. 

5119. Contract with fence owner. When the selectmen judge that a highway is liable to be obstructed by snowdrifts caused by the fences adjoining such highway, they may contract with the owner of the fences to remove any portion of the same, or to build wire fences in their places, and may allow him a reasonable compensation for removing such fences, or building such wire fences. 

P. L. § 4848. G. L. § 4505. P. S. § 3926. V. S. § 3405. R. L. § 3019. 1880, No. 68, § 1. 

5120. Same; record; effect. The contract made by the selectmen with the owner of such fences and the sum allowed as compensation shall be recorded in the office of the clerk of the town in which the fences are situated. A contract made under the preceding section shall bind such owner or owners and their assigns not to replace such wire fences with other than wire fences or to rebuild a fence so removed. 

P. L. § 4849. G. L. § 4506. P. S. § 3927. V. S. § 3406. R. L. § 3020. 1880, No. 68, § 2. 

5121. Snow fence. When the selectmen judge that a highway is liable to be obstructed by snowdrifts which may be prevented by a high fence on land adjoin-

ing the same, on the application in writing of three or more freeholders of the town, and on notice to and hearing of the owner or occupant of the land, they may cause such fence to be built and maintained. 

P. L. § 4850. G. L. § 4507. P. S. § 3928. V. S. § 3407. R. L. § 3021. 1876, No. 79, § 1. 

5122. Same; damages; appeal. The selectmen shall assess reasonable damages for the erection and maintenance of the high fence and pay or tender the same to the owner or occupant, who, if dissatisfied with such damages, may appeal as in cases of assessment of damages by selectmen in laying out highways. 

P. L. § 4851. G. L. § 4508. P. S. § 3929. V. S. § 3408. R. L. § 3022. 1876, No. 79, §§ 1, 2 

5123. Same; record. The orders of the selectmen as to high fences and the damages shall be recorded in the office of the town clerk. 

P. L. § 4852. G. L. § 4509. P. S. § 3930. V. S. § 3409. R. L. § 3023. 1876, No. 79, § 3. 

5124. Penalty. A person who wilfully injures such high fence shall be fined not more than $100.00.

P. L. § 4853. G. L. § 4510. P. S. § 3931. V. S. § 3410. R. L. § 3024. 1876, No. 79, § 4. 

Floods. 

5125. Location changed. When a highway is made impassable, or of insufficient width for free passage, by a landslide or washout, or a bridge is swept away by a flood, the selectmen may change the location of the highway or bridge to a place less exposed, or where the cost of maintenance will be less, and may discontinue unnecessary parts of the old highway, or may take, damage or affect such land as may be necessary at the location of such slide or washout for the purpose of reestablishing, repairing, rebuilding or protecting such highway or bridge, and at once proceed to build or rebuild such highway or bridge and open the same for work and travel. 

1949, No. 117.

P. L. § 4854. 

G. S. 24, § 24. 

G. L. § 4511. P. S. § 3932. V. S. § 3411. R. L. § 325. 1878, No. 67. 

1850, No. 31, § 1. 

5126. Damages; appeal. The selectmen shall assess and tender compensation to parties damaged by such change previous to opening the highway. A party dissatisfied with the damages may appeal to the county court as in cases of laying out or relocating highways, but the appeal shall not stay the building of such highway or bridge.

P. L. § 4855. G. L. § 4512. P. S. § 3933. V. S. § 3412. R. L. § 3026. G. S. 24, § 25. 

1850, No. 31, § 2. 

Diversion of Streams. 

5127. Proceedings. When it appears necessary in laying out, altering, constructing or repairing a highway, or to protect such highway against flood waters or ice jams, the selectmen or commissioners may change the course of a stream, widen the same, or provide storage for accumulated ice, and for such purposes may purchase or take the necessary land. The proceedings in respect thereto, as to notice to parties interested, assessment of damages, application to the court by parties dissatisfied with the decision of the selectmen, surveys, report and decision of the court upon report of commissioners, and in other respects, shall be the same as in laying out highways. An application to the county court for a new hearing by persons dissatisfied shall not be granted unless the petitioners give notice thereof to one of the selectmen within twenty days after such order is recorded in the office of the town clerk.

P. L. § 4856. 1925, No. 66. G. L. § 4519. P. S. § 3939. V. S. § 3418. R. L. § 3032. 

G. S. 24, §§ 84-89. 1862, No. 12, §§ 1-6. 

5128. Same; limitation. The preceding section shall not authorize the diversion of a stream so that the value of a mill, manufactory or machinery propelled by the water of the stream is diminished, without the consent of all persons interested therein.

P. L. § 4857. G. L. § 4520. P. S. § 3940. V. S. § 3419. R. L. § 3033. G. S. 24, § 90. 

1862, No. 12, § 7.

Drains, Ditches and Watercourses. 

5129. Selectmen may establish. Selectmen may lay out, establish, construct or cause to be constructed and maintained a drain, ditch or watercourse leading from a highway in the town, across the lands of any person to a watercourse, to carry away the surface water from such highway, or other drainage necessary for public health, if they judge the public good or the necessity or convenience of individuals requires the same. 

P. L. § 4858. G. L. § 4521. P. S. § 3941. V. S. § 3420. 1884, No. 13, § 1. 

5130. Notice. Before such drain, ditch or watercourse is laid out and established, the selectmen shall give notice to the person owning or interested in such lands, of a time and place at which they will hear them upon the question of laying out and establishing such drain, ditch or watercourse, and of damages caused thereby. 

P. L. § 4859. G. L. § 4522. P. S. § 3942. V. S. § 3421. 1884, No. 13, § 2. 

5131. Damages; survey; record. When the selectmen order the laying out and establishment of such drain, ditch or watercourse, they shall award such damages to the landowners as they judge reasonable, and cause their order and award, or refusal to award damages, with a survey and description of such drain, ditch or watercourse, to be recorded in the office of the clerk of the town where such highway, drain, ditch or watercourse is situated, within ten days thereafter. 

P. L. § 4860. G. L. § 4523. P. S. § 3943. V. S. § 3422. 1884, No. 13, § 3. 

5132. Benefits considered; town to maintain. The selectmen shall take into consideration in awarding damages the special benefit to the landowners, if any, by reason of the establishment and maintenance of such drain, ditch or watercourse. The town shall construct and maintain it in good and sufficient repair until, upon due notice and hearing, it is discontinued. 

P. L. § 4861. G. L. § 4524. P. S. § 3944. V. S. § 3423. 1884, No. 13, § 4. 

5133. Petition. A landowner dissatisfied with the order laying out and establishing such drain, ditch or watercourse and of such award or refusal to award damages within sixty days may apply by petition to the county court in the same county for a review of the action of the selectmen. Any number of such landowners may join in such petiton and such proceedings shall be had thereafter as in case of laying out a highway by selectmen. 

1945, No. 29, § 54. P. L. § 4862. G. L. § 4525. P. S. § 3945. V. S. § 3424. 1884, No. 13, § 5. 

5134. Same; petition to justice; proceedings. When the landowner is dissatisfied only with the amount of damages awarded or the refusal to award damages, within sixty days after such record is recorded in the office of the town clerk, by petition he may apply to a municipal judge or a justice in some other town in the county for the appointment of commissioners to reappraise such damages, and any number of such landowners may join in such petition. Upon the filing of such petition, the same proceedings shall be had as in case of a petition to such a magistrate to appoint commissioners to appraise damages caused by laying out a highway by selectmen; but such petition shall not delay the construction of such drain, ditch or watercourse. 

P. L. § 4863. G. L. § 4526. 1908, No. 62. P. S. § 3946. V. S. § 3425. 1884, No. 13, § 6. 

Rule of Damages. 

5135. Discontinued highway; set to landowner. When a discontinued highway belongs to the owner of land, through which a highway is laid out, the value thereof may be taken into consideration in estimating the damages sustained by the owner.

P. L. § 4864. G. L. § 4527. P. S. § 3947. V. S. § 3426. R. L. § 3036. G. S. 24, § 76. 

R. S. 20, § 51. 1800, p. 15. 

5136. Allowance lands. When a lot of land remains entire, as originally divided among the proprietors of a town, and is owned by one person, or by per-

sons jointly, to which a quantity of land was allowed for the use of highways more than has been taken up by highways already laid out, and a highway is laid through such lot, such allowance land may be taken into consideration in estimating the damages sustained by the owner.

P. L. § 4865. G. L. § 4528. P. S. § 3948. V. S. § 3427. R. L. § 3037. G. S. 24, § 77. 

R. S. 20, § 52. 

5137. Benefits to landowner considered. In estimating the damages sustained by a person owning or interested in lands, by reason of laying out or altering a highway, the benefits which such person may receive thereby shall be taken into consideration.

P. L. § 4866. G. L. § 4529. P. S. § 3949. V. S. § 3428. R. L. § 3038. G. S. 24, § 78. 

R. S. 20, § 53. 

23 Vt. 361. 

Watering Troughs. 

5138. Selectmen may provide; limitation. The selectmen may provide and maintain as many watering troughs by the side of the highways for the accommodation of the traveling public, as they deem proper, and supply the same with water.

P. L. § 4867. G. L. § 4530. 1915, No. 127. P. S. § 3950. V. S. § 3429. R. L. § 3039. 

G. S. 24, § 91. 1862, No. 13, § 1. 

Notice. 

5139. By selectmen and commissioners; how given. When, under the provisions of this chapter, selectmen or commissioners are required to give notice of the time and place of examination or hearing before them, such notice shall be given, either personally or in writing left with or at the residence of the person required to be notified, or, if he does not reside in the state, with or at the residence of his known agent or attorney, if he has one in the state, and, if not, with or at the residence of the occupant of the land in connection with which the interest of the person required to be notified may arise. When there is no such occupant, such notice shall be left at the office of the town clerk where such land lies. Such notice shall be given at least twelve days prior to the time appointed for such examination or hearing, except as otherwise provided in this chapter. 

P. L. § 4868. G. L. § 4531. P. S. § 3951. V. S. § 3430. R. L. § 3041. G. S. 24, § 93. 

47 Vt. 300.

Highways Not an Encumbrance. 

5140. Unless expressly covenanted against. When real estate is conveyed by deed, the existence of a highway over or upon any part of such estate shall not be treated as a breach of the covenant of seisin or warranty or any covenant against encumbrances contained in such deed, unless the parties to such deed expressly refer to and covenant against such highway.

P. L. § 4869. G. L. § 4532. P. S. § 3952. V. S. § 3431. R. L. § 3042. G. S. 24, § 81. 

1856, No. 31.

CHAPTER 237. REPAIR OF HIGHWAYS BY LOCAL AUTHORITIES. 

Duties of Towns. 

5141. Repairs. A town shall keep in good and sufficient repair at all seasons of the year its highways and bridges, other than those on the state highway system. 

P. L. § 4870. 1933, No. 157, § 4586. G. L. § 4533. P. S. § 3953. V. S. § 3432. R. L. § 3043. 

R. S. 21, § 1. 56 Vt. 487. 70 Vt. 271. 70 Vt. 391. 83 Vt. 421. 

G. S. 25, § 1. 

113 Vt. 511. 

5142. Guards. A town shall erect and maintain in dangerous places on its public highways suitable guards to protect travelers from accident by running over or down embankments, and shall be subject to the provisions of chapter 239 re-

lating to repairs on all highways and bridges, in respect to the provisions of this section.

P. L. § 4871. 1933, No. 87, § 2. G. L. § 4534. P. S. § 3954. R. 1906, § 3820. 

1896, No. 73, §§ 1, 2. 

71 Vt. 473. 

5143. Lighting of bridges and highways. The selectmen may cause covered bridges or highways to be lighted, when the safety or convenience of the public requires it. 

1949, No. 118.

P. L. § 4872. 

R. L. § 2698. 

5144. 

1933, No. 157, § 4588. G. L. § 3975. P. S. § 3473. V. S. § 3023. 1888, No. 125. 

G. S. 15, § 56. 1861, No. 40. 

Covered bridges; view. The selectmen shall cause covered bridges, other than those on the state highway system, located where a highway approaches such a bridge at an angle, to be so constructed, repaired or rebuilt that such bridge shall have the boarded sides thereof contain an opening on the side or sides facing the approach of such highway; the width, length and location of such opening to be determined by the selectmen.

P. L. § 4873. 1933, No. 157, § 4589. G. L. § 3976. 1917, No. 121, § 1. 

Guide Posts. 

5145. Report; penalty. The selectmen shall submit to the town at each annual meeting a report of the highway intersections at which guide posts ought to be erected and upon such report the town shall determine the several places at which guide posts shall be erected and maintained. The vote thereon shall be recorded in the office of the town clerk. The selectmen who neglect to make the report required by this section shall be severally fined $10.00.. 

P. L. § 4874. 1933, No. 157, § 4590. G. L. §§ 4044, 4045, 4047. P. S. §§ 3539, 3540, 3542. 

V. S. §§ 3087, 3088, 3090. 1888, No. 29, §§ 1, 2, 3. R. L. § 2749. G. S. 15, §§ 93, 94. 

R. S. 13, §§ 74, 75. R. 1797, p. 297, § 27. 

5146. Location; specification. At the intersection so determined upon, the selectmen shall erect a substantial post and place thereon a board upon which shall be plainly painted the names of the towns and places with an arrow pointing towards such towns and places to which the highway leads. 

P. L. § 4875. 1933, No. 157, § 4591. G. L. §§ 4046, 4048. P. S. §§ 3541, 3543. 

V. S. §§ 3089, 3091. 1888, No. 29, §§ 3, 4. 

5147. Neglect to erect and maintain; penalty. A town which neglects or refuses to erect and maintain such guide posts shall annually be fined $5.00 for each guide post it so neglects to maintain. In prosecutions under this section any number of offenses in the same year may be joined. 

P. L. § 4876. 1933, No. 157, § 4592. G. L. § 4049. P. S. § 3544. V. S. § 3092. 

1888, No. 29, § 5. 

5148. Exceptions. Where the state highway board has erected guide posts, town line posts and route signs, the town shall not be compelled to erect further guide posts.

P. L. § 4877. 1933, No. 157, § 4593. 

Highway Taxes. 5149. Highway taxes; winter roads. For keeping in repair the highways, towns containing incorporated villages and cities shall annually appropriate a sum not less than a fifth of the grand list of the town or city and all other towns shall annually appropriate a sum not less than a tenth of the grand list of the town, which shall be assessed by the selectmen and collected and paid to the town treasurer as provided by law for the payment of other town taxes, except that portion of such tax belonging to cities and incorporated villages, which is collected and appropriated by such cities and villages as provided by law. A portion of the funds so raised may be used for liquidating bonds or interest of any municipality incurred

for building highways. The sum herein provided for shall not be expended in towns or villages for snow or winter roads, but the town shall appropriate by special tax or otherwise a sum sufficient to care for the same. In towns in which there are incorporated villages, the sum appropriated shall be apportioned between the town and the village in the same manner as the town highway tax is apportioned. 

P. L. § 4878. 1933, No. 80. G. L. § 4535. 1912, No. 138. 1910, No. 125, § 1. P. S. § 3955. 

1906, No. 35, § 4. V. S. § 3433. 1892, No. 56, § 4. 1890, No. 17, § 1. 1886, No. 10. 

1884, No. 12, §§ 1, 3. 1882, No. 10. 1882, No. 18. R. L. § 3044. 1880, No. 64, § 1. 

1864, No. 74, § 1. G. S. 25, § 2. 1858, No. 29. 1851, No. 24, § 1. 1846, No. 30, § 1. 

R. S. 21, § 2. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, §§ 11, 15. 1826, No. 22. 1824, p. 29. 1818, p. 99. 

R. 1797, p. 350, § 5. 1792, p. 48. R. 1787, p. 80. 57 Vt. 31. 

5150. Expenditure, duties of selectmen, bridges. The funds raised from town taxes or apportioned by the state for highway improvement, shall not be used in repairing, building or rebuilding bridges or culverts, exceeding six feet span or the approaches thereto, except the minor repairs on such approaches incident to the maintenance of the surface of the highway thereon, which shall be cared for by the road commissioner with road funds. Bridges and culverts exceeding six feet span, including those in incorporated villages, shall be under the exclusive control of the selectmen, who shall keep the same in repair and rebuild when necessary; provided that the selectmen may authorize a road commissioner to have charge of and repair any bridge and its approaches not over twenty feet in the clear between abutments, and such authorization shall be in writing and recorded in the town clerk's office. The selectmen shall draw all orders on the general fund of the town to defray expenses incurred under this section, and the funds for that purpose shall be raised in the town tax. The provisions of this section shall not apply to state highways. 

1937, No. 109, § 1. P. L. § 4879. 1933, No. 157, § 4595. G. L. § 4536. 

1912, No. 139. 1910, No. 125, § 2. P. S. § 3956. V. S. §§ 3443, 3447. 

1892, No. 56, § 12. 

90 Vt. 114. 

1915, No. 128. 

1894, No. 79, § 6. 

5151. Same; excess. The funds raised from town highway taxes shall not be used for any purpose other than that for which such tax was voted, subject to the provisions of this chapter. If in any year money so voted is not expended, it shall be applied for the same purpose the following year.

P. L. § 4880. G. L. § 4537. 1910, No. 125, § 3. P. S. § 3957. V. S. § 3444. 

1894, No. 79, § 7. 

Road Commissioners. 

5152. Duties and liabilities. The town road commissioner shall superintend the expenditure of the town highway tax, shall have charge of keeping in repair such highways and shall be responsible to the town for damages sustained by it through his fault or neglect in the discharge of his duties. When a road commissioner has expended the money appropriated by the town for the repair of town highways and the amount thereof is not sufficient to complete the ordinary repairs of such highways, he shall notify one of the selectmen of the town of such insufficiency, and shall not be liable to the town for damages occasioned by the want of ordinary repairs of the same after giving such notice, if the selectmen do not furnish means to complete such ordinary repairs. After the giving of such notice, the town shall not be chargeable with the expense of the ordinary repairs of its town highways unless incurred under the direction of the selectmen. 

P. L. § 4881. G. L. § 4538. P. S. § 3958. 1902, No. 61, § 1. V. S. § 3446. 1892, No. 56, § 2. 

1890, No. 17, §§ 3, 4. R. L. § 3051. G. S. 25, §§ 5. 35, 36, 40. 1860, No. 28, § 1. 

1850, No. 26, §§ 1, 3. R. S. 21, § 4. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 12. R. 1797, p. 350, § 6. 

1792, p. 48. R. 1787, p. 80. 2 Aik. 295. 21 Vt. 342. 27 Vt. 544. 45 Vt. 443. 57 Vt. 448 

90 Vt. 114. 

5153. Accounts; settlement. The town road commissioner shall keep accurate accounts, showing in detail all moneys received by him, from whom and when received, and all moneys paid out by him, to whom and for what purpose, and

settle the same with the auditors not less then twenty-five days before the annual meeting. 

P. L. § 4882. G. L. § 4539. P. S. § 3959. V. S. § 3450. 

1884, No. 12, §§ 4, 5. R. L. § 3084. G. S. 25, § 37. 

1892, No. 56, § 14. 

57 Vt. 448. 

5154. Orders. All money expended upon town highways as provided in this chapter, except in cities and incorporated villages and except that expended by and under the direction of the selectmen, shall be drawn from the town treasury upon the orders of the town road commissioner to the party entitled thereto; and the commissioners shall keep a printed blank book of such orders, on the stubs of which shall be a counterpart of the order so given. An order drawn by a town road commissioner payable to himself shall not be paid by the town treasurer until approved in writing by one of the selectmen. Before receiving the amount of the same, the person in whose favor such order is drawn shall indorse such order. 

P. L. § 4883. G. L. § 4540. 1917, No. 119. 1910, No. 126. P. S. § 3960. R. 1906, § 3843. 

V. S. § 3451. 1892, No. 56, § 15. 1884, No. 12, § 2. 90 Vt. 114. 

5155. Incorporated villages not affected. Nothing in this chapter shall be construed to affect the rights and powers conferred on incorporated villages by their charters to elect or appoint street commissioners, and collect and disburse highway taxes.

P. L. § 4884. G. L. § 4542. P. S. § 3962. V. S. § 3454. 1892, No. 56, § 18. 1884, No. 12, § 9. 

Towns Having Two Road Commissioners. 

5156. Highway districts. As soon as possible, the selectmen of a town electing two road commissioners, shall divide the town into two highway districts, and cause a record of such division to be made in the office of the town clerk. Such division shall not be changed unless by a vote of the town at its annual meeting. 

P. L. § 4885. G. L. § 4543. P. S. § 3963. 1902, No. 55, § 2. 

5157. Division of money and machinery; patrol; apportionment; highway board. As in their judgment seems best, the selectmen shall divide between such districts the moneys received from the town highway tax and the use of the town road making tools and machinery; provided however, that the selectmen of a town whether it has one or two road commissioners, with the approval of the highway board, may designate a part or all of its state aid highways to be maintained for a term not exceeding three years by patrolmen employed by the highway board for that purpose. The selectmen shall apportion an equitable part of the total town highway tax to be expended together with the money furnished by the state on such state aid highways in conformity with such rules and regulations as may be made by the highway board governing such plan of maintenance. The selectmen shall draw their orders for the amount thus apportioned on the approval of such board.

P. L. § 4886. 1933, No. 157, § 4602. 1919, No. 111, § 1. G. L. § 4544. 1917, No. 254, § 4451. 

1912, No. 140. P. S. § 3964. 1902, No. 55, § 4. 

5158. Same; town road commissioner. With the approval of the selectmen, town road commissioners shall have the same powers that selectmen have under the provisions of this section respecting the designation of state aid highways to be patrolled, the apportionment of money for the same and the drawing of orders for the amount thus apportioned. The provisions of this section relating to the maintenance of state aid highways by patrolmen shall extend to roads in incorporated villages constituted as highway districts and the trustees thereof shall have the same powers and duties relating thereto as the selectmen have in towns. 

P. L. § 4887. 1933, No. 157, § 4603. 1919, No. 111, § 1. G. L. § 4544. 1917, No. 254, § 4451. 

1912, No. 140. P. S. § 3964. 1902, No. 55, § 4. 

5159. Duties and liabilities of commissioners. Such road commissioners shall have the same powers, perform the same duties and be subject to the same liabilities in their respective districts as town road commissioners. 

P. L. § 4888. G. L. § 4545. P. S. § 3965. 1902, No. 55, § 3.

Union of Towns for the Improvement of Highways. 

5160. Method. Two or more towns or cities and towns, by vote of their respective boards of aldermen and selectmen, may unite for the purpose of improving the grades, drainage, surface, length or otherwise of any portion of a highway passing into or through each of them. When such union has been effected, the officials herein named shall constitute a joint committee which shall be the agent of the several municipalities and may cause the work agreed upon to be done under the charge of a roadmaker or engineer of experience and skill. 

P. L. § 4889. G. L. § 4546. P. S. § 3971. 1902, No. 60, § 1. 

5161. Apportionment of expense. The expenses incurred under the preceding section may be apportioned on the basis of the grand list of each municipality, or in proportion to the benefit received by each, as may be agreed upon by the committee before the work is begun. Each board named in the preceding section may accept donations for such work, and, when the same is completed, or from time to time as the work proceeds, draw orders upon their respective treasurers, and deliver the same to the person agreed upon to superintend the work. A town or city shall not expend more than one per cent of its grand list in any one year under the preceding section, unless a larger sum has been voted at a regular or special meeting warned for that purpose.

P. L. § 4890. G. L. § 4547. P. S. § 3972. 1902, No. 60, § 2. 

Gravel for Highways. 

5162. May be taken within road limits. When necessary, a road commissioner or person in charge of building or repairing a highway may take gravel, earth, stone or other material, to repair or build a highway under his charge, that lies within the limits of the highway, whether the same is to be used at the place where the same is taken or elsewhere on such highway. 

P. L. § 4891. G. L. § 4551. 

V. S. § 3465. R. L. § 3092. 

1917, No. 254, § 4458. P. S. § 3980. 1904, No. 1, § 1. 

1866, No. 25, § 3. 

5163. Same; outside of road limits. When, in the opinion of the road commissioner or of any person in charge of the building or repairing of a highway, it is necessary to take gravel, earth or stone that does not lie within the limits of the highway and is not a part of improved residential property, to repair or build a highway under his charge and the owner thereof will not permit him to take the same without paying more than, in his judgment, he ought to pay, the commissioner or person may call on the selectmen, and they shall at once make an examination of the matter.

P. L. § 4892. G. L. § 4552. 

V. S. § 3466. R. L. § 3093. 

5164. Notice to landowner; damages. When the selectmen judge the public good requires the taking of such material, on notice to the owner or occupant of the land, they shall appraise the damages by the year or otherwise and shall pay or tender the same to the owner or occupant and may then direct the road commissioner or the person in charge of the building or repairing of a highway, to take the amount of material so appraised. 

1912, No. 142, § 1. P. S. § 3981. 1904, No. 1, § 1. 

1866, No. 25, § 1. 

P. L. § 4893. G. L. § 4553. 1912, No. 142, § 2. P. S. § 3982. 1904, No. 1, § 1. V. S. § 3467. 

R. L. § 3094. 1866, No. 25, § 1. 

5165. Appeal. When the owner or the occupant of the land is not satisfied with the decision of the selectmen either as to the necessity for the taking or the amount of damages appraised, or both, he shall have the same remedy as in case of laying out a highway by the selectmen. 

P. L. § 4894. G. L. § 4554. 1912, No. 142, § 3. P. S. § 3983. V. S. § 3468. R. L. § 3095. 

1866, No. 25, § 2. 

5166. Passing over land. When, in the opinion of a road commissioner or person in charge of building or repairing a highway, it becomes necessary to pass

over land which is not a part of improved residential property, to take gravel, earth or stone from land not owned by the person whose land it is desired to pass over, and the owner of such land will not permit such road commissioner or person in charge to pass over such land without paying more than, in his judgment, he ought to pay, the commissioner or person in charge of such building or repairing may call on the selectmen of such town, and they shall at once make an examination of the matter.

P. L. § 4895. G. L. § 4555. 1915, No. 130, § 1. 

5167. Notice; right of way; damages. When the selectmen judge that the public good requires the passing over such land, on giving six days' notice to the owner or occupant thereof, they shall lay out a right of way across such land sufficient for the passing over the land with the necesary teams and vehicles to carry such material, appraise the damages occasioned thereby and pay or tender the same to the owner or occupant. 

P. L. § 4896. G. L. § 4556. 1915, No. 130, § 2. 

5168. Appeal; not to delay work. When the owner or occupant is not satisfied with the decision of the selectmen as to the amount of damages appraised or as to the necessity for such right of way, he shall have the same remedy as in case of laying out a highway by the selectmen. An appeal taken from the decision of the selectmen shall not hinder or delay the repairing or building of such highway. 

The road commissioner or person in charge thereof, after tender of the damages to the owner or occupant of such land, may proceed to use the right of way, and the question of damages may thereafter be heard in the county court on appeal. 

P. L. § 4897. G. L. § 4557. 1915, No. 130, § 3. 

Miscellaneous Provisions 

5169. Loose stones; penalty. When there are loose stones in the traveled portion of the highway, the road commissioner shall cause the same to be removed at least once a month while the highway is bare of snow and drawn to some proper place to dispose of them. A road commissioner shall not be required to draw the stones from such highway where timber land borders on the sides of such highway. 

A road commissioner who fails to comply with a provision of this section shall be fined $5.00.

P. L. & 4898. G. L. § 4558. 1917, No. 254, § 4465. P. S. § 3973. 1906, No. 109, § 1. 

V. S. § 3460. 1884, No. 12, § 6. R. L. §§ 3068, 3069. 1870, No. 50. G. S. 25. § 67. 

1858, No. 23, § 2. 1852, No. 32, § 1. 

5170. Tools and materials. When it is for the interest of the town, selectmen or road commissioners may purchase tools necessary in making and repairing highways, and shall have credit for the same and for the expense of keeping them in repair. They may purchase, at the expense of the town, timber, plank and other materials necessary for repairing highways and bridges. 

P. L. § 4899. G. L. § 4559. P. S. § 3974. V. S. § 3461. 1884, No. 12, § 7. R. L. § 3067. 

G. S. 25, § 25. R. S. 21, § 22. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 20. 1805, p. 8. 

R. 1797, p. 357, § 15. 

5171. Road machines. Selectmen may purchase road machines for the use of the town, and, in making out the highway tax bills, may lessen the rate per cent of highway taxes, so that the aggregate of the same and expense of road machines shall not exceed the amount required by law to be raised for highways. The selectmen shall not be authorized to so deduct the expense from the highway tax of more than one road machine in any one year, unless two or more machines can be bought for not more than $250.00.

P. L. § 4900. G. L. § 4560. P. S. § 3975. V. S. § 3462. 1884, No. 21. 1882, No. 17. 

5172. Repair by selectmen; when. In the absence of any officers or agents appointed by the town, or in case of the refusal or neglect of such officers or agents to keep the highways and bridges in good repair, the selectmen of such town shall

have charge of the same and shall keep the highways and bridges in such town in good and sufficient repair at all seasons of the year. 

P. L. § 4901. G. L. § 4561. P. S. § 3976. V. S. § 3463. 1884, No. 12, § 8. R. L. § 3061. 

1874, No. 54. 

65 Vt. 247. 

5173. Removal of grass and weeds; proceedings. Annually, on or before 

July 20, the owners or occupants of lands bounding highways, except timber lands, shall cut or cause to be cut the grass and weeds growing within the bounds of such highways. When an owner or occupant fails to comply with the provisions of this section, the selectmen shall notify such owner or occupant, in writing, that, unless the provisions of this section are complied with within ten days after the delivery of such notice, they will forthwith cause such grass and weeds to be removed at the expense of such owner or occupant. Such notice may be given by personal delivery or by mailing the same to such owner or occupant at his last known post office address. When such owner or occupant is a corporation, the delivery of such notice shall be to any officer of such corporation. 

P. L. § 4902. G. L. § 4563. P. S. § 3978. 1906, No. 110, §§ 2, 3. 

5174. Town may recover expense; penalty. When an owner or occupant fails to comply with the provisions of the preceding section for ten days after the delivery of such notice, the selectmen shall forthwith cause such grass and weeds to be removed at the expense of such owner or occupant. In case of the refusal or neglect of such owner or occupant to pay such expense, the town shall thereupon be entitled to recover the same, with costs, from such owner or occupant, in an action of tort on this statute, and such owner or occupant shall be fined not more than $20.00 nor less then $5.00.

P. L. § 4903. G. L. § 4564. P. S. § 3979. 1906, No. 110, § 4. 

5175. Embankment; application to selectmen; notice. When, in the opinion of a road commissioner or person in charge of building or repairing a highway, it is necessary for the protection of a highway to erect an embankment upon the banks of a stream, and the owner of the stream or of the lands adjacent will not permit him to enter upon the lands for such purpose, or requires him to pay therefor a greater sum than in his judgment, he ought to pay, the commissioner or person shall call out the selectmen, who shall make an examination of the premises. 

P. L. § 4904. G. L. § 4565. 1917, No. 254, § 4472. P. S. § 3984. 1904, No. 1, § 1. 

V. S. § 3469. R. L. § 3096. 1870, No. 52, § 1. 

5176. Same; notice to landowner; damages. When the selectmen judge the public good requires such embankment, on notice to the owner or occupant of the stream and the lands adjacent, they shall assess the damages therefor and pay or tender the same to the owner or occupant and then direct the commissioner or person to erect the embankment. 

P. L. § 4905. G. L. § 4566. P. S. § 3985. V. S. § 3470. R. L. § 3097. 1870, No. 52, § 1. 

5177. Appeal. When the owner or occupant is not satisfied with the amount of damages allowed by the selectmen, he shall have the same remedy as in case of the appraisal of selectmen in laying out a highway. 

P. L. § 4906. G. L. § 4567. P. S. § 3986. V. S. § 3471. R. L. § 3098. 1870, No. 52, § 2. 

5178. Powers of cities and incorporated villages. The proper officers of cities and incorporated villages shall have like power to repair highways under their care and obtain materials therefor, which towns and town officers have, but this section shall not be so construed as to conflict with the act of incorporation of or laws specially applicable to such municipal corporations. 

P. L. § 4907. G. L. § 4568. P. S. § 3987. 1904, No. 1, § 1. 

Sidewalks and Parks. 

113 Vt. 511. 

5179. Sidewalks; appropriation. A town may appropriate a portion of the highway fund, not to exceed ten per cent of the total amount thereof, to be expended in constructing and maintaining sidewalks. 

P. L. § 4908. G. L. § 4569 P. S. § 3988. V. S. § 3472. 1890, No. 19, § 1.

5180. Same; expenditure. All moneys appropriated under the preceding section shall be expended by the selectmen or road commissioner, as the town may vote, in constructing and maintaining sidewalks, wherever the public good requires. 

P. L. § 4909. G. L. § 4570. P. S. § 3989. R. 1906, § 3870. V. S. § 3473. 1890, No. 19, § 2. 

5181. Sidewalks and footpaths. The town road commissioner may construct and repair suitable footpaths or sidewalks within the limits of public highways in an unincorporated village and thickly populated sections of his town, when, in his judgment, the public convenience requires the same, and the expense thereof shall be paid from the highway taxes of the town. 

P. L. § 4910. G. L. § 4571. P. S. § 3990. 1896, No. 71, § 1. 

5182. Parks and sidewalks; notices. At the request of ten or more citizens, the selectmen may lay out parks and sidewalks within the limits of the highway where it will not conflict with the travel in such highway, and post notices forbidding driving on them except where it is necessary to cross the sidewalk for entering private grounds.

P. L. § 4911. G. L. § 4572. P. S. § 3991. V. S. § 3474. 1894, No. 83, § 1. R. L. § 2709. 

1880, No. 130. 

5183. Penalty. A person who wilfully drives over or upon a park or sidewalk so posted, except where it is necessary to cross such sidewalk for the purpose of entering private grounds. shall be fined not more than $5,00 nor less than $1.00. 

P. L. § 4912. G. L. § 4573. 1917, No. 254, § 4480. P. S. § 3992. V. S. § 3475. 

1894, No. 83, § 2. 

CHAPTER 238. PROTECTION OF HIGHWAYS. 

Encroachments. 

5184. No adverse possession. A right or interest within the limits of a highway shall not be acquired by possession or occupation. 

P. L. § 4913. G. L. § 4631. P. S. § 4045. V. S. § 3506. R. L. § 3125. G. S. 25, § 65. 

1858, No. 27. 55 Vt. 412. 73 Vt. 258. 74 Vt. 343. 83 Vt. 54. 

5185. Same; easement. A public easement in a stream shall not be lost or abridged by prescription or adverse possession. 

P. L. § 4914. G. L. § 4632. P. S. § 4046. V. S. § 3507. R. L. § 3126. 1869, No. 33. 

83 Vt. 54. 

Injuries to Highways. 

5186. Obstructions; penalty; notice. A person who encloses a part of or obstructs or encroaches upon a highway right-of-way by fences, buildings, piles of wood, lumber or logs, waste stones from quarries or material of any kind, to the detriment of the highway right-of-way, or fills or places an obstruction in a ditch or gutter that is for drawing water from a highway right-of-way, or a person who deposits in or upon a highway right-of-way, rubbish, such as stones, brush, garbage or any noxious thing, shall forfeit not more than $25.00 for each offense, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the town or state. By their order the selectmen or highway board may require such person to remove the same, within such time as they think reasonable, not exceeding six months, and give notice of the order to such person. 

1945, No. 93, § 1. P. L. § 4915. G. L. § 4633. P. S. § 4047. R. 1906, § 3906. 

1904, No. 83, §§ 2, 3. V. S. § 3508. R. L. § 3127. G. S. 25, § 66. 1847, No. 26. 

R. S. 21, § 35. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 14. R. 1797, p. 357, § 16. 1792, p. 54. 

R. 1787, p. 80. 

75 Vt. 385. 

5187. Same; penalty. A person who fails to remove the fence, building, nuisance, encroachment or other obstruction mentioned in the preceding section. within the time limited shall forfeit to the town or state not more than $25.00 for every thirty days' neglect to comply with such order, with costs, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute in the name of the town or state. Such action shall

be commenced within six months from the time when the forfeiture is incurred. 

Such forfeiture shall be expended in repairing highways in the town or state. 

1945, No. 93, § 2. P. L. § 4916. G. L. § 4634. P. S. § 4048. R. 1906, § 3907. 

1904, No. 83, § 2. V. S. § 3509. R. L. § 3128. G. S. 25, § 66. 1847, No. 26. 30 Vt. 401. 

75 Vt. 385. 92 Vt. 440. 

5188. Same; removal; costs. The selectmen or highway board may remove such fence, building, nuisance, encroachment or other obstruction mentioned in section 5186 at the expense of the person who erected, caused or continued the same, and may recover such expense of the person so offending, with costs, in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the town or state. After the removal, the forfeiture provided in the preceding section shall not be incurred. 

1945, No. 93, § 3. P. L. § 4917. G. L. § 4635. P. S. § 4049. R. 1906, § 3908. V. S. § 3510. 

R. L. § 3129. G. S. 25, § 66. 1847, No. 26. R. S. 21, § 35. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 14. 

R. 1797, p. 357, § 16. 1792, p. 54. R. 1787, p. 80. 75 Vt. 385. 

5189. Obstructing travel; penalty. A person who places or causes to be placed an obstruction or encroachment in a public highway, with intent to hinder or prevent public travel thereon, or to injure or impede a person traveling thereon, shall be fined not more than $100.00 nor less than $10.00. 

P. L. § 4918. G. L. § 4636. P. S. § 4050. 1896, No. 74, § 1. 

5190. Obstructing sidewalks; penalty. A person who unnecessarily leaves or drives an animal or rides a bicycle upon a public sidewalk, or unnecessarily obstructs such sidewalk by permitting an animal owned or used by him to stand upon the same, shall be fined not more than $25.00 nor less than $5.00. 

P. L. § 4919. G. L. § 4637. 1917, No. 254, § 4540.- P. S. § 4051. V. S. §§ 4792, 4793. 

1894, No. 82. 1882, No. 52, §§ 1, 2. 

5191. Wilful obstruction; penalty. A person who wilfully fells trees, lays timber or places an obstruction or other nuisance, so as to impede the passing in a highway, shall forfeit to the town, to be expended in the repair of highways, not more than $25.00, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the town, with costs.

P. L. § 4920. 

G. S. 25, § 69. 

1792, p. 55.

G. L. § 4639. P. S. § 4053. R. 1906, § 3912. V. S. § 3512. R. L. § 3131.. 

R. S. 21, § 37. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 14. R. 1797, p. 358, § 17. 

55 Vt. 484. 57 Vt. 147. 97 Vt. 342. 

5192. Injuring highway; penalty. A person who wantonly or wilfully injures a highway, causeway or bridge, by destroying or removing plank, posts, timber or stones thereof, or by digging pits therein for gravel, clay or for other purpose, shall forfeit to the town, to be expended in repairing highways, not more than $50.00, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the town, with costs.

P. S. § 4054. R. 1906, § 3913. 1904, No. 83, § 2. V. S. § 3513. 

R. S. 21, § 38. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 14. 

54 Vt. 403. 55 Vt. 484. 69 Vt. 63. 

P. L. § 4921. G. L. § 4640. 

R. L. § 3132. G. S. 25, § 70. 

R. 1797, p. 358, § 17. 

5193. Liability for damages. A person guilty of an offense prohibited in either of the two preceding sections, besides the penalty, shall be liable to the town, or to any individual, in an action of tort on this statute, for damages sustained by either, in consequence of the acts therein prohibited, with costs. 

1947, No. 202, § 5137. P. L. § 4922. G. L. § 4641. P. S. § 4055. V. S. § 3514. R. L. § 3133. 

G. S. 25, § 71. R. S. 21, § 39. 1831, No. 4. 1830, No. 12, § 14. R. 1797, p. 358, § 17. 

97 Vt. 342. 

5194. Permit for laying pipes and wires. A person or corporation that desires to dig up a public highway in a town or an incorporated village having control of the highways therein, for the purpose of laying pipes or wires within the limits of such highway, shall first obtain from the selectmen of such town or the trustees of such village, a written permit stating the place where and the time when such digging may be done.

P. L. § 4923. G. L. § 4642. 1910, No. 128.

5195. Same; action. After such permit has been granted, the digging up and replacing of such highway, shall be done under the supervision of the selectmen or trustees, as the case may be, who shall have full authority in the premises. 

Upon failure of a person or corporation to perform such work in a manner satisfactory to the selectmen or trustees, the selectmen or trustees may complete such work at the expense of the person or corporation and recover such expense in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of such town or village, with costs. 

P. L. § 4924. G. L. § 4642. 1910, No. 128. 

5196. Exception. The foregoing provisions shall not apply to cities and shall not prevent a person or corporation from temporarily digging up, without first obtaining such permit, a highway for the purpose of repairing a break in a pipe; but in all such cases the work shall be completed to the satisfaction of the selectmen or trustees as provided in the case of digging up highways under this section. 

P. L. § 4925. G. L. § 4642. 1910, No. 128. 

5197. Injury to highway; penalty. A person who injures a public highway by obstructing or diverting a stream, watercourse or sluice, or by dragging logs or timber on the road surface, shall be fined $10.00 for each offense. Such person shall be further liable to the town for the damage to such highway, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the town. 

P. L. § 4926. G. L. § 4643. P. S. § 4056. R. 1906, § 3915. 1904, No. 38, §§ 1, 5. 

V. S. § 3515. R. L. § 3134. 1866, No. 24, §§ 1, 2. 69 Vt. 63. 97 Vt. 336. 

5198. Width of wheel rims. Freight vehicles, carts or carriages carrying more than one ton and less than two tons shall have the tires and felloes of their wheels at least two inches wide and if carrying more than two tons and less than three tons the tires and felloes of their wheels shall be at least three inches wide and if carrying more than three tons and less than four tons, the tires and felloes of their wheels shall be four inches wide and if carrying more than four tons and less than six tons the tires and felloes of their wheels shall be five inches wide; but the selectmen may give a person permission to use vehicles, carts or carriages in their respective towns with the tires or felloes of their wheels of less width than is provided in this section, when in their opinion, the highways would not be injured thereby. 

1947, No. 202, § 5142. P. L. § 4927. 1925, No. 70, § 80. 1919, No. 120., §1. G. L. § 4644. 

1917, No. 129, § 1. P. S. § 4057. 1898, No. 66, § 1. 1896, No. 75, § 1. V. S. § 3516. 

R. L. § 3135. 1876, No. 78. 1872, No. 36, § 1. 

5199. Same; penalty; damages. A person who violates a provision of the preceding section shall be fined not more than $50.00 nor less than $5.00. Such offender shall be further liable to the town in which the offense is committed for the damages to a public highway by reason of such violation to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the town, provided the action is brought within six months after such offense is committed. 

P. L. § 4928. G. L. § 4645. 1917, No. 129, § 2. P. S. § 4058. V. S. § 3517. R. L. § 3136. 

1872, No. 36, § 2. 

5200. Injury by wheels; penalty. A person who injures a public highway by using wagons with wheels held fast by chains or otherwise shall be fined $10.00 for each offense.

P. L. § 4929. G. L. § 4646. 1917, No. 254, § 4549. P. S. § 4059. 1904, No. 83, § 1. 

5201. Town not liable to offender. Towns and other corporations shall not be liable for damage to a person or his property by reason of the insufficiency or want of repair of a bridge or culvert while traveling on the highway or bridge in violation of the provisions of section 5198. 

P. L. § 4930. G. L. § 4647. P. S. § 4060. V. S. § 3518. R. L. § 3137. 1872, No. 36, § 3, 

5202. Unorganized towns and gores; duties of supervisors. When an encroachment or nuisance is made or put on a highway in an unorganized town or gore on complaint thereof made to a municipal judge or justice in the county, such

magistrate may issue his warrant to the supervisor for such unorganized town or gore, authorizing him to cause the encroachment or nuisance to be removed. Such supervisor shall have the same power to remove the encroachment or nuisance as is given to selectmen in this chapter, and he shall proceed in like manner. 

P. L. § 4931. G. L. § 4648. 

1912, No. 42, § 40. 1908, No. 62. P. S. § 4061. V. S. § 3519. 

R. L. § 3138. G. S. 25, § 72. R. S. 21, § 40. R. 1797, p. 359, §18. 1792, p. 55. 

Use of Chains, Shoes and Sled Brakes. 

5203. Regulations; damages. When a person chains the wheels of a vehicle used on a highway or uses a shoe less than two and a half feet long and seven inches wide or sets the brake so tight as to stop the revolution of such wheel and thereby damages the highway, the road commissioner of the town liable to maintain such highway may repair the damage so caused and recover the expense thereof, with costs, of the person causing the damage, in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the town.

P. L. § 4932. G. L. § 4653. P. S. § 4062. 1900, No. 50, § 1. V. S. § 3520. 1882, No. 15. 

5204. Sled brakes. On the written petition of five or more freeholders of the town, the selectmen shall examine a highway described in the petition. If they find that the use of a self-acting sled brake should be prohibited thereon, they shall post notices to that effect in conspicuous places at each end of that portion of the highway on which such use is prohibited. 

P. L. § 4933. G. L. § 4654. P. S. § 4063. V. S. § 3521. 1888, No. 30, § 3. 

5205. Failure to act; penalty. When the selectmen fail to make such examination and take such action as the public good requires within a reasonable time, they shall be severally fined $5.00.

P. L. § 4934. G. L. § 4655. P. S. § 4064. V. S. § 3522. 1888, No. 30, § 4. 

5206. Penalty for use after prohibited. A person who uses a self-acting sled brake upon a highway where the use of the same is prohibited and notices thereof posted, as provided in section 5204, shall be fined $5.00 for each offense. 

P. L. § 4935. G. L. § 4656. P. S. § 4065. V. S. § 3523. 1888, No. 30, §§ 1, 2. 

Bridges. 

5207. Speed limit; liability to town. A person who drives a horse or other beast, faster than a walk, on a bridge with string pieces thirty feet long between the supports, or on a bridge supported by one or more arches, or drives on any such bridge more than one load team, or more than twenty head of cattle, horses or mules, on any one tier of string pieces, or on any one arch at the same time, shall forfeit $5.00, with damages for injuries thereby done to the bridge which may be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name and for the benefit of the state or municipal corporation liable for the repairs of the bridge, with costs. 

P. L. § 4936. 1925, No. 70, § 112. 1919, No. 117, § 1. G. L. § 4659. P. S. § 4068. 

V. S. § 3527. R. L. § 3139. 1870, No. 54. G. S. 25, § 73. R. S. 21, § 41. 1830, No. 13. 

1829, No. 13, § 1. 38 Vt. 666. 54 Vt. 638. 

5208. Same; bridge more than eighty feet long. A person who drives a horse or other beast faster than a walk, on a bridge more than eighty feet in length shall forfeit $5.00, with damages for injuries thereby done to the bridge, which may be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the state or municipal corporation liable for repairs of the bridge, with costs. 

P. L. § 4937. 1925, No. 70, § 113. 1919, No. 117, § 2. G. L. § 4660. P. S. § 4069. 

V. S. § 3528. R. L. § 3140. 1870, No. 55, § 1. 

5209. Same; arrest; penalty. A person who wilfully violates the provisions of the preceding section may be apprehended without warrant, by an officer authorized to serve criminal process or by a watchman of such bridge, and taken forthwith before a justice's or municipal court in the town or city where so arrested. Such person may be there detained until warrant in due form of law can be

issued against him, and, upon conviction of such offense, shall be fined not more than $10.00, but an arrest without warrant is not hereby authorized except on the day such offense is committed, and when the officer making such arrest has reason to believe and does believe that the person so arrested will escape from the jurisdiction unless arrested without process.

P. L. 4938. G. L. § 4661. 1908, No. 62. P. S. § 4070. V. S. § 3529. 1886, No. 17. 

5210. Notice to be posted. A person shall not be liable as provided in the three preceding sections, unless the selectmen of the town, or the directors of the corporation, cause to be posted and kept at each end of such bridge, in some conspicuous place, a board with the substance of such prohibitions printed legibly thereon.

P. L. § 4939. G. L. § 4662. P. S. § 4071. V. S. § 3530. R. L. § 3141. 1870, No. 55, § 2. 

G. S. 25, § 74. R. S. 21, § 42. 1829, No. 13, § 1. 38 Vt. 666. 

5211. Powers of state highway board on state aid highways. On all highways that have been selected as state aid highways under the provisions of section 4918, and the bridges thereon, the highway board shall have concurrent authority and jurisdiction with selectmen in all matters within the authority and jurisdiction of such selectmen under the provisions of this chapter. If a person named in an order made by the board, under the authority of this section, neglects or refuses to comply therewith within the time prescribed by law, the board may report such neglect or refusal to the state's attorney of the county wherein the highway or bridge mentioned in such order is located. In the name of the state, the state's attorney may institute proceedings under the provisions of this chapter and the forfeitures so recovered shall be payable to the state. 

P. L. § 4940. 1933, No. 157, § 4656. G. L. § 4663. 1912, No. 144. 

Miscellaneous. 

5212. Penalty for destroying notice. A person who wilfully removes, defaces or destroys a notice posted by the selectmen as provided in this chapter shall forfeit $10.00, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the town or corporation in which such highway or bridge is located, with costs. 

P. L. § 4941. G. L. § 4664. P. S. § 4072. R. 1906, § 3932. V. S. § 3531. R. L. § 3142. 

G. S. 25, § 75. R. S. 21, § 43. 1829, No. 13, § 2. 

5213. Limitation of actions. A person shall not be liable to a prosecution for the penalties mentioned in the six preceding sections, unless the same is commenced within thirty days from the time the offense is committed. 

P. L. § 4942. G. L. § 4665. P. S. § 4073. V. S. § 3532. R. L. § 3143. G. S. 25, § 76. 

R. S. 21, § 44. 1829, No. 13, § 3. 

5214.

Jurisdiction. The forfeitures imposed by this chapter may be recovered before a justice or a municipal court although the magistrate is an inhabitant of the town in which the offense was committed. 

P. L. § 4943. G. L. § 4666, 1908, No. 62. P. S. § 4074. V. S. § 3533. R. L. § 3144. 

1865, No. 26. 

CHAPTER 239. COUNTY ROAD COMMISSIONERS AND LIABILITY OF TOWNS. 

County Road Commissioners. 

5215. Appointment; vacancies. The county court in each county shall appoint three county road commissioners annually, no two of whom shall be residents of the same town, for their respective counties, who shall be sworn to the faithful performance of their duties, and shall hold their office for one year and until their successors are appointed and qualified. Such court shall have power to remove the commissioners or either of them at any time, and shall fill all vacancies. 

P. L. § 4944. G. L. § 4603. P. S. § 4016. V. S. § 3477. R. L. § 3100. 1880, No. 62, § 1. 

86 Vt. 540.

5216. Defective roads and bridges; proceedings. When a highway or bridge is out of repair or unsafe for travel, any three citizens or taxpayers in the state may give notice of such insufficiency to one of the selectmen of the town in which such highway or bridge is situated, in writing duly signed, setting forth in general terms the location of such highway or bridge and the nature of such insufficiency. When such town neglects for thirty-six hours thereafter to commence work upon such highway or bridge, or fails to continue such work in good faith and with reasonable dispatch until such highway or bridge is put in good and sufficient repair, such citizens may file with one of the road commissioners for the county in which such highway or bridge is situated a written complaint signed and sworn to, setting forth in general terms the location of such highway or bridge and the nature of the insufficiency complained of, and stating that they have personally examined the same. The complainants shall also give the commissioners security by deposit or otherwise for the costs of proceedings under such complaint. 

P. L. § 4945. 1933, No. 157, § 4661. G. L. § 4604. P. S. § 4017. V. S. § 3478. 

1882, No. 13, § 1. R. L. § 3101. 1880, No. 62, § 2. 71 Vt. 473. 101 Vt. 181. 

5217. Same; notice and hearing. The commissioners receiving such complaint and deposit or other security shall forthwith notify one of the selectmen of such town, and one or more of the persons filing such complaint, of the time and place when and where the commissioners will meet for the examination of such highway or bridge and for hearing upon such complaint. In pursuance of such notice, the commissioners, or a majority of them, shall examine such highway or bridge, and hear all parties interested as to whether the public good demands that such highway or bridge be repaired, as to its condition and the repairs necessary to make the same safe and convenient for public travel, and the amount required for such repairs.

P. L. § 4946. 1933, No. 81, § 1. 

1882, No. 13, § 1. R. L. § 3101. 

G. L. § 4605. P. S. § 4018. V. S. § 3479. 

1880, No. 62, § 2. 101 Vt. 181. 

5218. Same; report. If, after such examination and hearing, the commissioners are of opinion that the highway or bridge is out of repair or unsafe for travel, and that the public good demands that such highway or bridge be repaired, they shall determine what work or repairs are necessary and fix the amount required therefor, not exceeding $750.00. They shall make a written report of their findings, containing a full description of the repairs or work determined upon and a statement of the amount required therefor, and shall conclude with an order requiring such town to make such repairs or perform such work within such time as they judge reasonable, not exceeding thirty days, unless, in the opinion of the commissioners, it is impracticable to complete the work in that time, but no town shall be required to expend more than $1,500.00 for such work in any year except that no town will be required to expend more than twenty per cent on its grand list in any one year. 

1951, No. 109. P. L. § 4947. 1933, No. 81, § 2. 1931, No. 70. 

G. L. § 4606. P. S. § 4019. V. S. § 3480. 1884, No. 14, § 1. 

1882, No. 13, § 1. R. L. § 3101. 1880, No. 62, § 2. 

101 Vt. 181. 

5219. Same; filing report; effect. The commissioners shall forthwith file their report in the office of the clerk of the county in which the highway or bridge is situated, and deliver a copy of such report to one of the selectmen. Such report shall be final and conclusive as to the necessary repairs and the amount required therefor.

P. L. § 4948. G. L. § 4607. P. S. § 4020. V. S. § 3481. 1882, No. 13, § 1. R. L. § 3101. 

1880, No. 62, § 2. 101 Vt. 181. 

5220. Repairs not made; proceedings. At the expiration of the time limited for such repairs, without further complaint, and upon such notice to the town as they deem reasonable, the commissioners shall ascertain if their order has been complied with by such town. If they find such town has failed to do the work ordered by them, they shall forthwith appoint an agent to expend upon such highway the amount fixed in their report. They shall cause the agent to give a suffic-

ient bond conditioned for the faithful performance of his duties and shall file in the office of the county clerk a certificate, stating that their order has not been complied with, and that judgment should be entered against such town for the amount named in their report, and give the name of the person appointed to expend such amount.

P. L. § 4949. G. L. § 4608. P. S. § 4022. V. S. § 3483. 1882, No. 13, § 2. R. L. § 3102. 

1880, No. 62, § 3. 71 Vt. 473. 101 Vt. 181. 

5221. Judgment against town; execution. The clerk shall thereupon enter judgment in favor of the commissioners against such town for the amount named in the report, with costs. He shall issue execution therefor, returnable in thirty days from the date of such judgment. In such execution, the officer collecting the same shall be directed to pay the amount of such judgment to the agent appointed by the commissioners, and the costs to the county clerk. 

P. L. § 4950. G. L. § 4609. P. S. § 4023. V. S. § 3484. 1882, No. 13, § 2. R. L. § 3102. 

1880, No. 62, § 3. 101 Vt. 181. 

5222. Agent's duties and pay. The agent shall immediately repair such highway or bridge as directed in the report of the commissioners and receive out of the funds paid him $4.00 per day for his services in making such repairs. When · there is a balance unexpended in his hands, he shall pay the same into the treasury of such town and make return of his doings to the commissioners within ten days. 

P. L. § 4951. 1927, No. 68. G. L. § 4610. P. S. § 4024. V. S. § 3485. R. L. § 3104. 

1880, No. 62, § 5. 

101 Vt. 181. 

5223. Subpoenas; oaths; pay. County road commissioners may sign subpoenas requiring witnesses to appear before them, administer oaths, and shall each receive the sum of $8.00 per day and expenses, while attending to the duties of such office. 

1953, No. 100.

R. L. § 3106. 

P. L. § 4952. G. L. § 4611. 1915, No. 133. P. S. § 4025. V. S. § 3486. 

1880, No. 62, § 7. 

5224. Liability of agent. An agent appointed under the provisions of this chapter who wilfully or negligently fails to expend the money paid to him, or to make return to the commissioners or the court of the amount of money expended by him in making or repairing such highway or bridge, as provided in this chapter, shall be liable to the town in which such highway or bridge is situated in an action of tort on this statute, for all money paid to him and not expended as aforesaid. 

P. L. § 4953. G. L. § 4612. P. S. § 4026. V. S. § 3487. R. L. § 3108. 1880, No. 62, § 9. 

5225. Costs. When complaint is made to the commissioners and they find such highway or bridge in good repair and safe for travel, each party shall pay his own costs, otherwise the costs shall be paid by the town. Costs shall be taxed and allowed in all respects as is provided in case of actions returnable to a justices' court.

P. L. § 4954. G. L. § 4613. P. S. § 4027. V. S. § 3488. R. L. § 3109. 1880, No. 62, § 10. 

5226. Road or bridge between towns or counties. When a highway or bridge complained of is on the line between two towns, and both towns are liable to keep such highway or bridge in repair, notice shall be given to both towns, and they shall be jointly liable the same as if the highway or bridge were in one town. 

Where the highway or bridge complained of is located on the line between two counties, all questions arising under the provisions of this chapter may be tried in the county where the complaint is made.

P. L. § 4955. G. L. § 4614. P. S. § 4028. V. S. § 3489. R. L. § 3110. 1880, No. 62, § 11. 

70 Vt. 278.

Liability of Towns for Damages. 

5227. Bridges and culverts. If damage occurs to a person, or his property, by reason of the insufficiency or want of repair of a bridge or culvert which the town is liable to keep in repair, the person sustaining damage may recover the same in an action of tort on this statute. If the damage accrues in consequence of

the insufficiency or want of repair of a bridge erected and maintained by two or more towns, the action shall be brought against all the towns liable for the repairs; provided however, that the posts or guard shall not be considered as a part of a culvert where the danger is no greater at either end of the culvert than on the highway itself at either side of the culvert. The damage and costs shall be paid by the towns in the proportions in which they are liable for the repairs. The court, in its discretion, may issue execution against each town for its proportion only; provided that the liability hereunder shall not exceed $3,000.00 on account of injury to or death of a person and damage to his property. 

P. L. $4956. 1933, No. 82, § 1. 1921, No. 128, § 1. G. L. § 4615. P. S. § 4029. V. S. § 3490. 

1884, No. 14, § 2. 1882, No. 13, § 4. R. L. § 3111. 1880, No. 62, § 13. G. S. 25, §§ 41, 42, 43.

5228. When one town is without state. When the damage mentioned in the preceding section accrues in consequence of the insufficiency or want of repair of a bridge built and maintained by two or more towns, one or more of which are without this state, the action shall be brought against the town or towns within this state liable for repairs. The proportion of the damage with costs shall be paid by such town or towns in the proportion in which it or they are liable for the repairs. The court, in its discretion, may issue execution as provided in the preceding section.

P. L. § 4957. G. L. § 4616. P. S. § 4030. V. S. § 3491. 1894, No. 84. 

86 Vt. 540. 

5229. Liability of town for damages; notice before action. A person shall not recover against a town or other corporation for such damage sustained in consequence of the passing on a bridge or culvert of a carriage of a total gross weight exceeding twelve thousand five hundred pounds. An action shall not be had or maintained in any court against a town for injuries received or damages sustained through the insufficiency of a bridge or culvert, unless notice is first given in writing, signed by the party so injured or claiming damage, to one or more of the selectmen of the town in which the bridge or culvert is situated, within twenty days of the time of the occurrence of such injury or damage, stating the time when and the place where the injury was received, and pointing out in what respect the bridge or culvert was insufficient or out of repair, and stating that such person will claim satisfaction of the town. 

P. L. 4958.

5230. Contents of notice; exception. Such notice shall contain a description of the injury received or damage sustained. If bodily injuries are claimed, the part of the body injured shall be stated, with the extent and effect of the injury upon the health of the person so injured, but the provisions in relation to notice. shall not apply to a person who in consequence thereof is bereft of reason.

Towns Indictable for Neglect to Make Repairs. 

5231. Fine; expenditure of same. When a town, liable to keep in repair a highway or bridge, fails so to do, it may be indicted for such neglect by the grand jury of the county, and may be fined at the discretion of the court, whether any special damage has been sustained or not. The fine so assessed shall be expended in repairing the highway or bridge, under the direction of a commissioner appointed by the court.

P. L. § 4960. G. L. § 4619.

A town shall not be indicted for not 

5232. Notice before indictment. keeping in repair its highways and bridges unless a notice describing the highway or bridge complained of and describing in general terms its defects, signed by some person residing in the state, is delivered to one of the selectmen at least twelve days before the return date of the complaint to the court to which such complaint is made. 

1947, No. 202, § 5176. P. L. § 4961. G. L. § 4620. P. S. § 4034. V. S. § 3495. R. L. § 3113. 

1878, No. 66. G. S. 25, § 60. 1841, No. 19. 1840, No. 11. 

5233. Joint liability; expenditure of fine. When towns, jointly liable, fail to keep in repair a bridge, they may be indicted by the grand jury of any county in which either of the towns is situated, and may be fined at the discretion of the court. The fine and costs shall be paid by the several towns in the proportion in which they were assessed for the building of the bridge; but in making the apportionment, the court may take into consideration what has already been paid by any town and issue execution against each for its proportion. The fine shall be expended in repairing such bridge under the direction of a commissioner appointed by the court.

P. L. § 4962. G. L. § 4621. P. S. § 4035. V. S. § 3496. R. L. § 3114. G. S. 25, § 61. 

R. S. 21, § 31. 

5234. Notice of indictment; collection of fine. When an indictment is presented to the county court, as provided in this chapter, the clerk shall issue a citation to the town indicted, and it shall be served as writs are by law required to be served against towns, at least twelve days before the date of trial. The execution issued for the fine and costs shall be levied and collected like executions in civil causes against towns. 

1947, No. 202, § 5178. P. L. § 4963. G. L. § 4622. P. S. § 4036. V. S. § 3497. R. L. § 3115. 

G. S. 25, § 62. R. S. 21, § 32. 

5235. Description of highway. In such indictments, it shall not be necessary to state the exact length or width of the highway, but it shall be sufficient to describe it as a highway leading from some particular place to some other particular place therein mentioned.

P. L. § 4964. G. L. § 4623. P. S. § 4037. V. S. § 3498. R. L. § 3116. G. S. 25, § 63.

P. L. § 4965. G. L. § 4624. P. S. § 4038. V. S. § 3499. R. L. § 3117. G. S. 25, § 64.
