CHAPTER 240. TOLL BRIDGE COMMISSIONERS 

5237. Commissioners. By virtue of their office, the members of the state highway board shall be toll bridge commissioners.

P. L. § 4966. 1923, No. 7, §§ 4, 32 (Par. 3). G. L. § 4731. 1915, No. 254, § 1. 

1912, No. 262, § 1. 

5238. Further powers of commissioners. With the approval of the governor, the board may agree with the New Hampshire commissioners as to the division of

the expense of freeing any and all toll bridges now existing between such states and as to the expense of maintaining such bridges as public highways and, in the event such agreement is made, with the approval of the governor, may expend not to exceed $10,000.00 in any one year for the purpose of freeing such bridges. 

P. L. § 4967. G. L. § 4732. 1915, No. 254, § 2. 1912, No. 262, § 2. 

5239. Powers of eminent domain. The board shall have the same power to take land or property for the purpose of this chapter as is given in chapter 236. 

P. L. § 4968. G. L. § 4733. 1912, No. 262, § 3. 

5240. Reimbursement of towns. The board shall determine the number of bridges between the states of Vermont and New Hampshire which have been previously operated as toll bridges and have been acquired by purchase, in whole or in part, by any town in the state and opened to public travel without toll, the amount paid for such bridges and by whom paid, the cost of maintenance and by whom borne and the cost of construction and maintenance and by whom paid, or any bridge which may have been erected as a free bridge to replace a former toll bridge between such states. With the approval of the governor, from the appropriations in their hands, the board may reimburse in whole or in part such towns as may have acquired, maintained or erected such bridges. 

P. L. § 4969. 1921, No. 143, § 1. G. L. § 4734. 1915, No. 255, § 1. 

5241. Payment. With the approval of the governor, the auditor of accounts shall issue his warrants against the moneys annually available for the purpose of this chapter for such sums as may be required to pay the expenses and awards of the board.

P. L. § 4970. G. L. § 4735. 1917, No. 58. 1912, No. 262, § 4. 

5242. Duties of the state highway board. The highway board shall have general supervision over such portion of toll bridges freed under the provisions of this chapter as shall lie within the state of Vermont and which the state by agreement is bound to maintain. 

1947, No. 202, § 5186. P. L. § 4971. G. L. § 4737. 1915, No. 254, § 4. 

5243. Report. The board shall make a report biennially to the general assembly and shall include therein an account of all moneys expended and such other matter as it shall deem pertinent. 

P. L. § 4972. G. L. § 4738. 1915, No. 1, § 227. 1915, No. 254, § 3. 1912, No. 262, § 5. 

Toll Bridges. 

5244. Crossing toll bridges without paying toll; penalty. When the tollhouse of any bridge corporation, entitled to take toll for the crossing of its bridge, is situated in this state, a person with the intent to avoid the payment of such toll, shall not cross such bridge without paying the lawful toll. A person so offending shall forfeit $2.00 for every such offense, with costs, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, for the benefit of such corporation. 

P. L. § 4973. G. L. § 4763. P. S. § 4133. V. S. § 3566. R. L. § 3177. 1880, No. 63. 

TITLE 29. CONSERVATION AND DEVELOPMENT OF NATURAL RESOURCES. 

CHAPTER 273. STATE GEOLOGIST Board of Geographic Names. 

6257. How constituted; duties and powers. The state librarian, the state geologist, an officer of the Vermont Historical Society, a member of the department of highways, and a member of the office of the secretary of state are hereby constituted a board of geographic names and such board shall serve without expense to the state. The board is authorized to furnish for any federal or state

publication the proper terminology and spelling of any geographic name in Vermont and may advise the United States Post Office Department regarding the proper selection and spelling of the name of a Vermont post office or any railroad company regarding the use and spelling of the name of a Vermont station. The board is authorized to function in collaboration with the United States Board on Geographic names and to conduct its operations similarly. The names used in the topographic maps of the state now being prepared by the United States Geological Survey in cooperation with the state shall be spelled in accordance with the recommendations of the board. 

1953, No. 175. P. L. § 428. G. L. § 411. 1917, No. 269, § 1. 

TITLE 33. STATE FOREST SERVICE. 

CHAPTER 302. FORESTRY 

7076. Brush fires, penalty. Fires kindled for the purpose of burning brush or for other lawful purpose shall be kindled only at such times and under such conditions as will enable the parties starting them to keep them entirely under control. Except as provided in this section, a person shall not kindle or authorize another to kindle a fire in the open air for the purpose of burning brush, weeds, grass or rubbish of any kind, between March 15 and November 15 except where there is snow on the site, in any year, without first obtaining permission from the firewarden or deputy warden of the town, or the state forester, or his deputy, stating when and where such fire may be kindled. Whenever such permission is granted, such warden, or state forester or his deputy within twelve hours, shall issue a written permit for record purposes stating when and where such fire may be kindled. Such permission shall not be required for the kindling of a fire in a plowed field, garden, public highway or railroad right of way at a distance of not less than two hundred feet from any woodland, timberland or field containing dry. grass or other inflammable material. A person who violates a provision of this section shall be fined not more than $200.00 or imprisoned not more than six months, or both. Whenever the state forester shall deem that the public safety of any town or portion of a town of this state does not require the protection provided by this section, he may cause the town firewarden of any such town to post notices to that effect in not less than five conspicuous places in such town. The provisions of this section will not apply whenever (1) such notices have been posted; nor (2) to fires built in stone arches at state recreational areas; nor (3) to fires built in special containers used for burning brush, waste, weeds, grass or rubbish when conditions are deemed satisfactory to the town firewarden; nor (4) to areas within cities or villages maintaining a fire department. 

1953, No. 87. 1949, No. 183. 

1947, No. 202, § 6624. 1943, No. 70, § 1. 1939, No. 100, § 1. P. L. § 4587. G. L. § 477. 

P. S. § 362. 1904, No. 16, § 9. 

7077. Refusal of permit. With the written approval of the governor, during periods of extreme fire hazard, the state forester may notify town firewardens that for a specified period no brush burning permits shall be issued. The wardens shall issue no permits during the specified period. 

1939, No. 100, § 3. 

7078. Fires in woods; penalty. No one shall build a fire in the woodlands. of another without the permission of the owner, lessee, holder of right of way or his authorized agent between April 1 and November 1. A person who builds a fire in or adjoining any woods shall totally extinguish such fire before leaving it. A person who violates a provision of this section shall be imprisoned not more than thirty days or fined not more than $50.00, or both. 

1951, No. 168. P. L. § 4588. G. L. § 478. P. S. § 358. 

1904, No. 16, § 5.

CHAPTER 322. BEASTS RUNNING AT LARGE 

7577. In public highway or yard; penalty. A person who knowingly permits cattle, horses, sheep or swine to run at large in a public highway, or yard belonging to a public building, without the consent of the selectmen, shall be fined not more than $10.00 nor less than $3.00.

CHAPTER 327. FAIRS AND THEATRES. 

Fairs. 

7649. Regulation of shows, games of chance. The officers of a town fair association may regulate and prevent theatrical, circus or mountebank exhibitions and shows, or traffic in fruits, goods, wares and merchandise of whatever description, the trading of horses and games of chance, on fair days, within a distance of two hundred yards in any highway leading to or passing a fairground, if in the opinion of such officers the same would obstruct or interfere with the free and uninterrupted use of such highways. The police employed by such association shall have the same power in respect to such matters on such highways as they have within such grounds.

P. L. § 8319. G. L. § 6778. P. S. § 5683. V. S. § 4877. 1894, No. 115. 

CHAPTER 328. REGULATIONS OF DANCE HALLS, BOWLING ALLEYS, POOL HALLS AND COASTING. 

Coasting on Sleds. 

7663. Coasting may be prohibited; notice. The selectmen shall forbid coasting upon the highways when it endangers the lives or property of travelers, and shall post notices to that effect in two or more conspicuous places in the vicinity. However, such prohibition shall not apply to highways in villages or cities having regulations respecting coasting. 

P. L. § 8317. G. L. § 6768. P. S. § 5675. V. S. § 4871. R. L. § 4076. G. S. 15, §§ 54, 55. 

1859, No. 42, §§ 1, 2. 

7664. Coasting after notice; penalty. A person who thereafter coasts on a highway specified in such notice shall be fined $2.00.

P. L. § 8318. G. L. § 6769.. P. S. § 5676. V. S. § 4872. R. L. § 4077. G. S. 15, § 55. 

1859, No. 42, § 2. 

CHAPTER 331. OUTDOOR ADVERTISING 

7676. Definitions. As used in this chapter, the following words shall mean: 

I. "Display" means erecting, maintaining, pasting, painting and posting an advertisement or sign out of doors, or erecting or maintaining a billboard or other structure designed and intended for the display of advertising matter where the same may be seen by the public, or allowing such advertisement, billboard or other structure, erected or displayed, to remain exposed in whole or in part to public view, including the act itself and the causing of such act to be done; 

II. "Highway" means a public road;

III. "Thickly settled part of a town" means any part of a town along a highway where, within a space not exceeding one thousand feet along the center line of the traveled part of the highway, there are located not less than seven houses or places of business;

IV. "Business part of a town" means any part of a town along a highway where within a space of not exceeding one thousand feet along the center line of the

traveled part of the highway, there are located not less than three places of business;

V. "Place of business" means a commercial or industrial establishment or enterprise other than farming which is carried on at least five months during the calendar year. 

113 Vt. 64. 

1941, No. 187, § 1. P. L. § 8338. 1929, No. 28, § 12. 1925, No. 32, § 9. 

7677. Fees, when due. The obligation to pay the license and permit fees required by law shall apply and be in force for such time as such advertisement or sign or a part thereof remains visible and so long as a board or structure or apart thereof erected or built for the purpose of displaying advertising matter thereon remains exposed to public view.

P. L. § 8339. 1929, No. 28, § 12. 1925, No. 32, § 9. 

7678. License to do business. A person, firm or corporation shall not engage in the business of outdoor advertising for direct profit through rentals or compensation received for the erection, maintenance, or display of painted bulletins, poster panels, or other outdoor advertising devices, upon real property, until such person, firm or corporation shall secure from the secretary of state a license to engage in the business of outdoor advertising. The fee for such license shall be $25.00 annually, payable in advance on March 1.

P. L. § 8340. 1929, No. 28, § 1. 

113 Vt. 64. 

7679. Permits. A person, firm or corporation, licensed under the preceding section, shall not erect or maintain an outdoor advertising structure, device or display, until such person, firm or corporation shall obtain from the secretary of state a permit for the erection of such structure, device or display and pay the proper fee therefor.

P. L. § 8341. 1929, No. 28, § 2. 1925, No. 32, § 1. 1921, No. 44, § 1. 

113 Vt. 64. 

7680. Same; application. Application for such permit shall be in writing, signed by the applicant or his duly authorized agent, upon forms furnished by the secretary of state in such form and requiring such information as the secretary may prescribe. Each application shall have attached thereto the written consent of the owners of the property on which such structure, device or display is to be erected or maintained.

P. L. § 8342. 1929, No. 28, § 2 1925, No. 32, § 1. 1921, No. 44, § 1. 

113 Vt. 64. 

7681. Same; exceptions, location of signs. A person, firm or corporation not engaged in the business of outdoor advertising for direct profit, shall not erect, maintain or continue to maintain any outdoor advertising structure, device or display, other than upon property within five hundred feet of the building or structure where the goods advertised are manufactured or offered for sale, or where the business advertised is carried on, until such person, firm or corporation shall obtain from the secretary of state a permit in the same manner as provided by the two preceding sections. Advertisements or signs for which a permit is not required under the foregoing provisions of this section shall not be subject to the restrictions specified in sections 7689 and 7690. However, no such advertisement or sign shall be located or maintained outside a city or incorporated village less than ten feet from the nearer edge of the traveled part of the highway, except on a building or structure where the goods advertised are manufactured or offered for sale or where the business advertised is carried on. 

1941, No. 187, § 2. 1939, No. 221, § 1. P. L § 8343. 

113 Vt. 64. 

1933, No. 146, § 1. 1929, No. 28, § 3. 

7682. Same; contents. If the size and location of each advertisement, billboard or other structure is not contrary to the provisions of this chapter, upon receipt of an application and the required permit fee, the secretary of state shall cause to be issued a permit for the erection or continued maintenance of the outdoor advertising structure, device or display named in the application, which permit shall state the location and size of such structure, device or display and the

location upon which it is to be erected or maintained, and shall be in force until 

March 1 next following the date thereof. An advertisement displayed upon such structure or device shall be exempt from the payment of any other permit fee during the period covered by such permit. The location of such advertising structure, device or display, either commercial or otherwise, shall not conflict with any municipal ordinance or regulation, provided a certified copy of such municipal ordinance or regulation shall have been filed in the office of the secretary of state prior to the erection of such structure, device or display. When a permit is issued, such secretary shall issue a suitable tag bearing numbers corresponding to the permit numbers and any structure, device or display erected, constructed or maintained shall have upon its face or front support such tag in a readily legible form, except on signs erected for one month only. Such tags shall be furnished by the state to be attached to such structure, device or display by the permittee. 

1939, No. 221, § 2. P. L. § 8344. 1929, No. 28, § 5. 1925, No. 32, § 3. 1921, No 44, § 3. 

7683. Same; fees. The fees for such permits, except in the case of permits issued for the period of one month only, shall be payable when issued and annually thereafter on March 1 and as follows: at a straight rate of two and a half cents for each square foot with a minimum fee of $1.00 for each panel or separate display. A separate fee shall be paid for each side of each panel, bulletin or sign used or intended to be used for outdoor advertising. 

1939, No. 221, § 3. P. L. § 8345. 1931, No. 13, § 1. 1929, No. 28, § 4. 1925, No. 32, § 2. 

1921, No. 44, § 2. 

7684. Limitation. No sign or advertisement shall be erected which shall contain more than six hundred square feet, nor any advertisement consisting of a row of signs to be read or to be seen in series. This shall not be construed to affect strip signs advertising seasonable articles sold at roadside stands or tourist homes. 

1939, No. 221, § 4. P. L. § 8346. 1931, No. 13, § 1. 1929, No. 28, § 4. 

7685. Permits. Permits may be issued for a period not exceeding one month covering outdoor advertising designed to advertise for a period not exceeding one month and the fee therefor shall be six mills per square foot and payable in advance. Such permits shall not be renewable during the year in which they are issued. 

1941, No. 187, § 3. P. L. § 8347. 1931, No. 13, § 1. 1929, No. 28, § 4. 

7686. Square feet how computed. In computing the measurements specified in this chapter, the framework of the panel shall not be included. 

P. L. § 8348. 1931, No. 13, § 1. 1929, No. 28, § 4. 1925, No. 32, § 2. 1921, No. 44., § 2. 

7687. Exempt signs. The following signs shall be exempt from the license and permit fee requirement of this chapter: 

I. An advertising sign from a municipality for an advertisement owned by it and advertising its industries or attractions and maintained at either public or private expense;

II. Signs and other devices on or in the rolling stock, station, subways or structures of or used by common carriers; 

III. Highway lighthouse signs, with no advertising thereon, located at places of danger, provided the written consent of the state highway board is obtained as to location, plan and style of erection; 

IV. Purely direction signs erected by the owner or proprietor of the place of business to which the sign directs, and erected and maintained in the city or town in which such place of business is located, or in the adjoining town or towns in this state, but if so erected in such adjoining town or towns then not more than two such signs in any such adjoining town; and 

V. Purely direction signs erected and maintained by municipal or state officials. 

1945, No. 179, § 1. 1941, No. 187, § 4. 

1929, No. 28, § 6. 1925, No. 32, § 4. 

1939, No. 221, § 5. 

1921, No. 44, § 4. 

P. L. § 8349. 1931, No. 13, § 4. 

113 Vt. 64.

1941, No. 187, § 4. 1939, No. 221, § 5. 

1925, No. 32, § 4. 1921, No. 44, § 4. 

P. L. § 8349. 

113 Vt. 64. 

1931, No. 13, § 4. 

7688. Purely direction signs; removal of; when. A purely direction sign erected after March 8, 1945, shall not contain more than thirty square feet. The secretary of state may order the removal of a purely direction sign containing more than thirty square feet erected after such date. If the person, firm or corporation in control of or owning such sign fails to remove such sign within thirty days after such order of removal has been sent to such person, firm or corporation by the secretary of state by registered mail, the secretary of state may cause such sign to be removed by the state highway department. The expense of such removal may be collected from the person, firm or corporation owning or controlling the same in an action of tort on this statute. 

1945, No. 179, § 1. 

1929, No. 28, § 6. 

7689. Restrictions. Advertisements and signs shall not be displayed within three hundred feet of any public park, playground, or cemetery, or located within three hundred feet from a highway intersection or where a highway crosses a railroad track outside of a city or an incorporated village or the business part of a town, the distance to be measured from the center of the used part of the highway and the center of the railroad right of way, except as provided in section 7681. 

Signs of three hundred square feet or over shall be located at least three hundred feet from the center of the traveled part of the highway and signs under three hundred square feet other than signs to be erected and maintained for only one month shall be located as many linear feet from the center of the traveled part of the highway as there are square feet in the face of the sign but no sign shall be located nearer the center of the traveled part of the highway than thirty-five feet. 

1939, No. 221, § 6. P. L. § 8350. 1933, No. 146, § 2. 1929, No. 28, § 7. 1925, No. 32, § 5. 

113 Vt. 64. 

7690. Same; when not applicable. The provisions of the preceding section shall not apply to signs or advertisements located inside of a city or an incorporated village, or upon the walls of a building in which the goods advertised are offered for sale or the business advertised is conducted, or to signs erected by the state or a town which solely indicate highway directions, traffic regulations and dangerous places, or to signs located within a thickly settled or business part of the town and within three hundred feet of the center of the main entrance of a house or place of business within such part of the town. 

1941, No. 187, § 1. 1939, No. 221, § 6. P. L. § 8350. 1933, No. 146, § 2. 1929, No. 28, § 7. 

1925, No. 32, § 5. 113 Vt. 64. 

7691. Bond; conditions. A license shall not be granted to a person, firm or corporation having his or its principal place of business outside of the state for the display of an advertisement or the erection of a billboard or other structure designed and intended for the display of advertising matter until such person, firm or corporation has filed with the secretary of state a bond satisfactory to him and running to the state. Permits shall not be granted to such person, firm or corporation for the display of an advertisement for a period not exceeding one month until such person, firm or corporation making application for such permit has filed with the secretary of state a bond satisfactory to him and running to the state. Such bonds shall be in such sum as the secretary shall determine, conditioned to observe, obey and fulfill all requirements of law and the regulations and orders of the secretary relating to the display of advertisements, and otherwise conditioned as he may determine. They shall remain in force and effect so long as any obligation to the state remains unsatisfied. 

P. L. § 8351. 1931, No. 13, § 2. 1929, No. 28, § 8. 1925, No. 32, § 6. 

7692. Removal orders. The secretary of state may order the removal or change in location of an advertisement or sign, when in his opinion such advertisement or sign obstructs a clear view along a highway, or is within the legal limits of a highway, or is displayed contrary to the provisions of law. 

P. L. § 8352. 1929, No. 28, § 9. 1925, No. 32, § 7. 

113 Vt. 64.

7693. Same; failure to obey. If the person, firm or corporation in control of or owning an advertisement or sign which has been ordered removed or changed in its location, as provided in the preceding section, does not remove such advertisement within thirty days after such order of removal or change has been sent to such person, firm or corporation by the secretary by registered mail, the secretary may cause such advertisement or sign to be removed. The expense of such removal may be collected from the person, firm or corporation owning or controlling the same in an action of tort on this statute, or from the sureties on the bond filed by such person, firm or corporation. 

P. L. § 8353. 1929, No. 28, § 9. 1925, No. 32, § 7. 

113 Vt. 64. 

7694. Removal. The state highway department or selectmen of the town wherein such advertisement or sign is located shall remove or cause to be removed such advertisement or sign which is located within the prohibited areas defined in sections 7681, 7689 and 7690, except such as are permitted under the provisions of the following section. 

1941, No. 187, § 5. 

1925, No. 32, § 8.

7695. Signs within limits of highway; exceptions. With the consent of the trustees of an incorporated village or the selectmen of a town, the secretary of state may permit the erection of advertising signs which are designed to benefit local residents or industries, within the limits of highways, but not in any location where such signs will obstruct the view along a highway. 

P. L. § 8355. 1929, No. 28, § 11. 

7696. Name of owner. All advertisements and all billboards and structures designed for the display of advertising matter thereon shall show the name of the person, firm or corporation displaying such advertisements or owning or leasing such billboards and structures, as well as a permit number tag. 

1939, No. 221, § 8. P. L. § 8356. 1929, No. 28, § 13. 1925, No. 32, § 10. 

7697. Penalty. A person who erects, maintains, displays or allows to remain in view an advertisement, sign, billboard or any structure designed for the display of advertising matter contrary to the provisions of this chapter shall be fined not more than $100.00, or imprisoned not more than thirty days, or both.

P. L. § 8357. 1929, No. 28, § 14. 1925, No. 32, §11. 1921, No. 44, § 5. 

7698. Construction. The several sections and provisions of this chapter are hereby declared independent and severable and the invalidity, if any, of any part or feature thereof shall not affect or render the remainder of the chapter invalid or inoperative.

P. L. § 8358. 1929, No. 28, § 16. 

CHAPTER 332. STANDARD TIME. 

7699. Standard time, determination. The standard time within the state of Vermont shall be based on the mean astronomical time of the seventy-fifth degree of longitude west from Greenwich, known and designated as "United 

States Standard Eastern time."

P. L. § 8359. 1921, No. 261, § 1. 

7700. Use as to train movements, laws and contracts. In all laws, statutes, orders, decrees, rules and regulations relating to the time of performance of any act by any officer or department of this state, including the legislative, executive and judicial branches of the state government, or of any county, city, town or district thereof, or relating to the time in which any rights shall accrue or determine, or within which any act shall or shall not be performed by any person subject to the jurisdiction of this state, and in all the public schools and institutions of the state, or of any county, city, town or district thereof, and in all contracts or choses in action made or to be performed in this state, it shall be understood and intended that the time shall be the United States Standard Eastern time. 

1947, No. 155, § 1. P. L. § 8360. 1921, No. 261, § 2.

TITLE 39. IMPRISONMENT AND PARDONS. 

CHAPTER 348. COUNTY JAILS. 

Employment of Prisoners. 

8006. By sheriff. A male person imprisoned under sentence in a county jail, if physically able to work, may be required by the sheriff of the county to perform not more than ten hours of manual labor, within or without the walls of such county jail, each day of such imprisonment, except on Sundays and legal holidays. 

P. L. § 8833. G. L. § 7258. 1915, No. 223, § 1. P. S. § 6104. 1906, No. 200, § 1. 

79 Vt. 521. 80 Vt. 175. 

8007. Classification of labor; regulations. Such labor to be performed in a county shall be classified and fixed from time to time by the sheriff and shall be subject to such rules and regulations as are made by the sheriff to secure humane treatment of such prisoners and to provide employment for them within or without the walls of such jail; but the rules and regulations in respect to the care of persons employed on the public highways as herein provided shall be subject to the approval of the governor and the attorney general. 

P. L. § 8834. 1933, No. 157, § 8472. G. L. § 7259. 1917, No. 243, § 1. 1917, No. 244, § 26. 

1917, No. 254, § 7062. 1915, No. 1, § 173. 1915, No. 223, § 2. 1912, No. 244, § 1. 

1910, No. 236. P. S. § 6105. 1906, No. 200, §§ 2-5, 8. 

80 Vt. 175. 

8008. Work on highways. The sheriff may require and compel able-bodied prisoners so confined upon conviction for crime to work in the improvement of the public highways, within a radius of thirty miles from such jail and outside of a city or incorporated village. The state highway commissioner shall designate, subject to the approval of the sheriff, the highways and portions thereof in such county upon which such labor shall be employed and such portion so designated and approved shall be under the control of such commissioner or his assistants during the time such work is in progress and he or his assistants shall direct the manner in which the work shall be done, but the care of such prisoners while so employed shall be under the direction of the sheriff or his deputies or other qualified officers. 

The sheriff is authorized to expend such sum out of the public moneys in the treasury of the county as is required for the purchase of material and tools adapted to the work provided by this and the preceding section. 

P. L. § 8835. 1933, No. 157, § 8473. G. L. § 7259. 1917, No. 243, § 1. 1917, No. 244, § 26. 

1917, No. 254, § 7062. 1915, No. 1, § 173. 1915, No. 223, § 2. 1912, No. 244, § 1. 

1910, No. 236. P. S. § 6105. 1906, No. 200, §§ 2-5, 8.

CHAPTER 353. EMPLOYER'S LIABILITY AND WORKMEN'S COMPENSATION. 

Definitions. 

8054. Words and phrases. Unless the context otherwise requires, words and phrases used in this chapter shall be construed as follows: 

1915, No. 164, § 58.

VI. "Public employment" means employment by the state, as provided by the provisions of this chapter, and by any of the public corporations mentioned in section 8080, but does not include the employment of public officials who are elected by popular vote or who receive salaries exceeding $2,000.00 a year; 

1953, No. 125, § 1. 1927, No. 98, § 1. 

Application of Chapter. 

8062. When state not an employer. The provisions of this chapter shall not be construed so as to make the state an employer where it only renders state aid to a municipality or approves of its plans or supervisors. 

P. L. § 6494. 1927, No. 98, § 4. 

106 Vt. 259.

8063. State as employer; regulations. The state highway board and sergeant at arms shall be deemed the agencies of the state as employer under the provisions of this chapter as to all state employees in their respective departments and are authorized to make rules and regulations as to details of administration subject to the approval of the commissioner of industrial relations. 

1947, No. 202, § 8226. 1939, No. 182, § 1. P. L. § 6495. 1927, No. 98, § 5. 

108 Vt. 396. 

106 Vt. 259. 

8065. Money for state workmen's compensation. $15,000.00 of the moneys annually appropriated to the highway department and $300.00 of the moneys annually appropriated to the sergeant at arms shall be annually available for the purposes of carrying out the provisions of this chapter relating to workmen's compensation for state employees. 

1939, No. 182, § 2. P. L. § 6497. 1933, No. 157, § 6171. 1927, No. 98, § 7. 

State and Municipal Employees. 

106 Vt. 259 

8080. Same; certain state employees. The provisions of this chapter shall apply to employees in the state department of highways and to employees employed by the sergeant at arms, other than office employees, and to employees other than officials, except in the case of road commissioners or selectmen as provided in subdivision VI of section 8054, as hereinbefore defined of towns, town school districts, incorporated school districts, incorporated villages and fire districts. Policemen, firemen, and others entitled to pensions shall be deemed employees within the meaning of this chapter. If any policeman, fireman or other person entitled to a pension claims compensation under the provisions of this chapter, there shall be deducted from such compensation any sum which such policeman, fireman or other person may be entitled to receive from any pension or other benefit fund to which the municipal body may contribute; but the provisions of this chapter shall not apply unless and until the governing officials of such municipal body so vote. 

1953, No. 125, § 2. 1947, No. 156, § 1. 1939, No. 182, § 3. P. L. § 6505. 

1927, No. 98, § 2. G. L. § 5769. 1915, No. 164, § 5. 106 Vt. 259. 

8081. Same; definition. "Employees of the state," as used in section 8082, shall be construe to included all the officers and employees of all state departments, boards, commissions, institutions, and state agencies, who perform daily duties for the state and whose principal source of income is paid from the state treasury, except members of the general assembly and officers and employees thereof, municipal judges, assistant judges of the county court, judges of probate, state's attorneys, sheriffs, high bailiffs, county treasurers, county clerks, or any of their deputies or subordinates, and excepting prisoners or wards of the state, or any person engaged by the state under retainer or special agreement, and excepting employees in the state department of highways other than office employees, and excepting employees of the sergeant at arms other than office employees. 

1947, No. 156, § 2. 

8082. Board to award compensation. The commissioner of industrial relations, the state treasurer and the state auditor of accounts shall constitute a board which, upon petition and hearing, may award compensation and medical and hospital benefits to employees of the state, other than those state highway employees and employees of the sergeant at arms covered by section 8080, receiving personal injuries by accident, arising out of and in the course of their employment to the amounts not exceeding those provided for payment of injuries under the provisions of this chapter and not exceeding the limits under the provisions of this chapter as to time. Such commissioner shall be chairman of the board. The decisions of the board shall be final. 

1947, No. 156, § 3.

TITLE 41. CRIMES AND OFFENSES. 

CHAPTER 367. TRESPASSES AND MALICIOUS INJURIES. 

Posting Bills and Advertisements, and Injuries to Trees and Timber. 

8395. On telephone, telegraph or electric poles. A person who paints or posts a sign, advertisement or notice on a telegraph, telephone or electric light pole shall be fined $5.00 for each offense.

P. L. § 8532. G. L. § 6948. P. S. § 5834. V. S. § 5014. 1890, No. 44, §§ 3, 4. 

1882, No. 75. § 3, 4. 

8396. Advertisements; penalty. A person who by himself, servant or agent, advertises an entertainment or occupation, by throwing posters, bills or advertising sheets of any kind, loose in a public street or highway, shall be fined not more than $50.00 nor less than $5.00, with costs.

P. L. § 8533. G. L. § 4658. P. S. § 4067. V. S. § 3525. 1886, No. 19, § 2. 

8397. Marking shade trees. A person who marks a public shade tree except for the purpose of protecting it and under a written permit from the tree warden, if there is one, shall be fined not more than $50.00.

P. L. § 8534. G. L. § 6950. P. S. § 5836. 1906, No. 9, 1904, No. 76, § 5. 

8398. Advertisements on fences, etc., without owners's consent. A person who advertises his wares or occupations, by painting or posting notices of the same on fences or other private property, or on rocks or other natural objects, without leave of the owner, shall be fined $10.00.

P. L. § 8535. G. L. § 6951. P. S. § 5837. V. S. § 5016. R. L. § 4202. 1864, No. 28. 

8399. Injuries to public shade trees. A person who wilfully or negligently injures, defaces, breaks or destroys, by any means whatsoever, a shade or ornamental tree within the limits of a public way or place, or who negligently suffers a horse or other beast driven for or by him, or a beast belonging to him and lawfully in a public way or place, so to injure such a tree, shall be fined not more than $100.00 nor less than $5.00, and in addition thereto shall be liable to the town or village where the damage was done, for the damages occasioned thereby, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute. 

P. L. § 8536. G. L. § 6952. P. S. § 5838. 1906, No. 99, § 1. 1904, No. 76, §§ 6, 7. 

V. S. § 5017. 1884, No. 22, § 1. R. L. § 4203. 1876, No. 119. 1867, No. 11. 

G. S. 113, § 45, 46, 47. 1858, No. 5. 

8401. Injuries to fruit trees. A person who wilfully and maliciously injures or destroys fruit trees on private land or in a public highway or common shall be imprisoned in the state prison not more than one year or fined not more than $100.00.

P. L. § 8538. G. L. § 6954. P. S. § 5840. V. S. § 5019. R. L. § 4205. 

G. S. 113, § 39. 1859, No. 21, § 1. R. S. 95, § 23. R. 1797, p. 187, § 1. 

Public Highways and Places. 

1864, No. 27. 

R. 1787, p. 160. 

8415. Camping in public highway after notice. Three or more persons traveling together from town to town upon the public highway with horses, wagons or other vehicles, shall not stop or camp in a public highway for more than one hour at a time after being notified by the owner or occupant of land adjoining such highway or the selectmen of the town to remove. A person who violates the provisions of this section shall be fined not more than $20.00 nor less than $5.00. 

P. L. § 8550. 1933, No. 157, § 8198. G. L. § 6966. 1908, No. 169. 

Automobiles Discarded Within Highway Limits. 

8417. Penalty. A person who leaves an automobile within the limits of any public highway for a period of five days shall be fined not more than $25.00 nor less than $5.00.

P. L. § 8552. 1931, No. 87, § 1.

8418. Removal. The selectmen of a town, trustees of an incorporated village or city council of a city shall cause such automobile to be removed at the expense of the owner.

P. L. § 8553. 1931, No. 87, § 2. 

8419. Forfeiture. If an automobile so removed in accordance with the preceding section is not claimed by the owner within thirty days after such removal and the expenses of removal and storage of the same paid, then such officers may proceed to sell the automobile the same as finders of property may sell property under chapter 325.

P. L. § 8554. 1931, No. 87, § 3. 

Injuries to Guideposts, Road Signs, Water Pipes and Boundaries. 

8420. Damages; penalty. A person who wilfully and maliciously defaces, injures or destroys a guidepost or milestone, or wilfully and maliciously cuts, injures or destroys leaden or other pipes used as an aqueduct, or wilfully and maliciously removes or alters a boundary of lands, or defaces, alters or removes marks upon a tree or other monument, designating a point, course or line in the boundary of a lot or tract of land, or dividing line between towns or societies, shall be imprisoned not more than ninety days or fined not more than $20.00, or both. The offender shall be liable to the owner in an action of tort on this statute for the damages.

P. L. § 8555. G. L. § 6968. P. S. § 5852. V. S. § 5028. R. L. § 4214. G. S. 113, § 49. 

R. S. 95, § 24. R. 1797, p. 189, § 2. 33 Vt. 443. 111 Vt. 274. 

8421. Wilful injury to sign; penalty. A person who wilfully removes, injures or defaces a railroad grade crossing warning sign maintained by a town, incorporated village or city, shall be fined not more than $10.00. 

1947, No. 202, § 8574. P. L. § 8556. G. L. § 6969. 1917, No. 254, § 6783. 1915, No. 114. 

8422. Same. A person who unlawfully removes, injures or defaces a drawbridge warning sign maintained by a town shall be fined not more than $10.00 for the use of the town placing or maintaining such sign. 

1947, No. 202, § 8575. P. L. § 8557. G. L. § 6970. 1917, No. 118, § 3. 

TITLE 43. DEPARTMENT OF PUBLIC SERVICE. 

Part II. Railroads. 

CHAPTER 404. CONSTRUCTION AND OPERATION OF THE ROAD. 

Right to Alter Highway or Turnpike. 

9505. Laying road upon turnpike or bridge. When it is necessary for a railroad corporation to lay out its road upon or by the side of a turnpike or way, or upon a bridge owned by a town or turnpike corporation the railroad corporation shall give notice thereof to one of the selectmen of the town or to one of the directors of the turnpike. When the railroad corporation cannot agree with the selectmen or with the turnpike directors on a road to be worked or a bridge to be built by the railroad corporation, in place of such road or bridge, or on the amount of damages, the public service commission shall direct it to build such road or bridge, as a substitute for the road or bridge so located upon, as the interest of the public and the parties require. Upon the construction of such road or bridge, the road or bridge so located upon shall vest in the railroad corporation. 

P. L. § 6223. G. L. § 5159. P. S. § 4417. 1906, No. 118, § 8. V. S. § 3833. R. L. § 3377. 

G. S. 28, § 34. 1849, No. 41, § 27. 21 Vt. 590. 27 Vt. 103. 55 Vt. 484. 57 Vt. 144. 

9506. Appeal. A railroad corporation, town, turnpike corporation or individual owning land adjacent to such road or bridge so entered upon, crossed or

altered, shall have the same right of appeal from the decision of the selectmen or the commission as is provided in this chapter in case of the appraisal of land damages by commissioners.

P. L. § 6224. G. L. § 5160. P. S. § 4418. 1906, No. 118, § 9. V. S. § 3834. R. L. § 3377. 

G. S. 28, § 34. 1849, No. 41, § 27. 

9507. Alteration of course of turnpike or way at crossing. A railroad corporation may alter the course of a turnpike or other way where it is crossed by its railroad, for the purpose of facilitating the crossing of the same, if it can agree with the selectmen of the town in which such way is situated or the directors of the turnpike corporation as to such alteration or the manner of crossing. When it cannot agree, the public service commission shall determine the same. 

P. L. § 6225. G. L. § 5161. P. S. § 4419. 1906, No. 118, § 10. V. S. § 3835. R. L. § 3378. 

G. S. 28, § 35. 1849, No. 41, § 28. 

9508. Alterations to be recorded. Alterations made in a turnpike or way as provided in this chapter, if made by the commission, shall be signed by it. If such alterations are agreed upon by the parties, the agreement shall be signed by the selectmen of the town or the turnpike directors, and duly recorded in the town clerk's office. 

1947, No. 202, § 9640. P. L. § 6226. G. L. § 5162. P. S. § 4420. 1906, No. 118, § 11. 

V. S. § 3836. R. L. § 3379. G. S. 28, § 37. 1849, No. 41, § 30. 

9509. Additional lands. Such railroad corporation may take such additional lands for the purposes mentioned in the three preceding sections as the commission judges necessary. Unless the lands so taken are purchased or given, compensation therefor shall be determined by the commission, as in other cases, and made by the railroad corporation to the owners and persons interested in the lands. 

When compensation is made, the same shall become part of such turnpike or way, and may be held for highway purposes, and such landowners and such corporation shall have the same right of appeal as in other cases of land damages. 

P. L. § 6227. G. L. § 5163. P. S. § 4421. 1906, No. 118, § 12. V. S. § 3837. R. L. § 3380. 

G. S. 28, § 36. 1849, No. 41, § 29. 64 Vt. 52. 

Crossing Highways and Turnpikes. 

9510. Turnpike or way crossing railroad. When a turnpike or other way is laid out after the laying out and constructing a railroad, so as to cross such railroad, the turnpike or other way may pass over, under or cross the railroad, and shall be so built as not to obstruct or injure it. In case the turnpike or other way is laid out to cross the railroad at grade, on application of the parties owning or operating the railroad, the commission, upon notice and hearing, shall determine whether such crossing ought to be at grade. If the commission determines that the crossing ought not to be at grade, it shall be constructed over or under the railroad. 

P. L. § 6228. G. L. § 5164. P. S. § 4422. 1906, No. 118, § 13. V. S. § 3838. 

1886, No. 20, § 1. R. L. § 3381. G. S. 28, § 33. 1849, No. 41, § 26. 

59 Vt. 320. 82 Vt. 316. 111 Vt. 425. 

58 Vt. 234. 

9511. Same; application to public service commission. The application to the commission, provided in the preceding section, may be made at any time. before the expiration of the time fixed in the order laying the turnpike or way for beginning work thereon.

P. L. § 6229. G. L. § 5165. P. S. § 4423. 1906, No. 118, § 14. V. S. § 3839. 1886, No. 20, § 2. 

111 Vt. 425. 

9512. Railroad crossing turnpike or way. A railroad may be so laid out as to cross a turnpike or other way, if the commission judges it necessary. The corporation may raise or lower the turnpike or way, for the purpose of having its railroad pass over or under it. However, such corporation shall restore the turnpike or way as far as practicable, and so as not to impair its usefulness. 

P. L. § 6230. G. L. § 5166. P. S. § 4424. 1906, No. 118, § 15. V. S. § 3840. 1888, No. 21, § 1. 

R. L. § 3382. G. S. 28, § 35. 1849, No. 41, § 28.

9513. Highway changed; notice; hearing. When a highway is so located that such railroad cannot be judiciously constructed across or upon the same without interfering therewith, the railroad corporation, with the consent of the commission, may cause such highway to be changed so that such railroad may be constructed on the best site for that purpose. However, such corporation shall put such highway as near as practicable in as good a situation and repair as it was previous to such alteration, under the direction of the commission. Such construction or alteration shall not be made without notice to all parties interested and a hearing before the commission.

P. L. 6231. G. L. § 5167. P. S. § 4425. 1906, No. 118, § 16. V. S. § 3841. 

1888, No. 21, § 1. 

9514. Crossings; repairs at. When a railroad corporation has constructed a railroad across a public highway by passing upon, over or under the traveled path thereof, the corporation shall keep in good and sufficient repair and rebuild when necessary, bridges, culverts, crossings and other constructions made for the accommodation, safety and convenience of the public travel on the highway, over or upon such railroad.

P. L. § 6232. 1931, No. 102, § 1. G. L. § 5168. P. S. § 4426. 

G. S. 28, §§ 38, 40. 1855, No. 28, § 1. 1852, No. 26, § 1. 

75 Vt. 6. 93 Vt. 461. 

V. S. § 3844. R. L. § 3383. 

42 Vt. 370. 60 Vt. 121. 

9515. Expense; apportionment; state and municipality railroad.

When made necessary by reason of highway improvement incident to increased load or speed of motor vehicular traffic, upon petition of the railroad corporation owning or operating such crossing or of the municipality in which such crossing is located or of the highway board, to the public service commission, after hearing and due notice to the parties in interest, the cost of such repairing and rebuilding may be apportioned between the state, the municipality with its consent and the railroad in such proportion as the commission finds just and equitable. 

P. L. § 6233. 1931, No. 102, § 1. G. L. § 5168. P. S. § 4426. 

G. S. 28, §§ 38, 40. 1855, No. 28, § 1. 1852, No. 26, § 1. 

75 Vt. 6. 93 Vt. 461. 

V. S. § 3844. R. L. § 3383. 

42 Vt. 370. 60 Vt. 121. 

9516. Maintenance, road bed; underpass. The maintenance of the road bed of the highway through an underpass shall be borne by the state or municipality or both, unless, upon petition, the commission shall order otherwise. 

P. L. § 6234. 1931, No. 102, § 1. G. L. § 5168. P. S. § 4426. 

G. S. 28, §§ 38, 40. 1855, No. 28, § 1. 1852, No. 26, § 1. 

75 Vt. 6. 93 Vt. § 491. 

V. S. § 3844. R. L. § 3383. 

42 Vt. 370. 60 Vt. 121. 

9517. Expense; state highways. When such highway is controlled and maintained by the state, such expense shall be apportioned solely between the state and the railroad.

P. L. § 6235. 1931, No. 102, § 1. G. L. § 5168. P. S. § 4426. 

G. S. 28, §§ 38, 40. 1855, No. 28, § 1. 1852, No. 26, § 1. 

75 Vt. 6. 93 Vt. 491. 

V. S. § 3844. R. L. § 3383. 

42 Vt. 370. 60 Vt. 121. 

9518. Liability; railroad. A railroad corporation constructing a railroad across a highway shall be liable to the town, within whose limits the crossings are, for damages occasioned to such town by reason of such corporation not complying with the provisions of the four preceding sections. Such liability shall extend to expenses incurred by the town in connection with actions against such corporation by reason of such noncompliance.

P. L. § 6236. 1931, No. 102, § 1. G. L. § 5168. P. S. § 4426. 

G. S. 28, §§ 38, 40. 1855, No. 28, § 1. 1852, No. 26, § 1. 

75 Vt. 6. 93 Vt. 491. 

V. S. § 3844. R. L. § 3383. 

42 Vt. 370. 60 Vt. 121. 

9519. Release of corporation from liability. The liability of the corporation shall continue although the railroad has been abandoned, unless the selectmen of the town consent in writing that the corporation be released therefrom, or unless the corporation or its assigns restore such crossing to its original state

of usefulness and permanency. Such written consent shall be recorded in the record of deeds in the town clerk's office by such selectmen. 

P. L. § 6237. G. L. § 5169. P. S. § 4427. V. S. § 3845. R. L. § 3383. G. S. 28, §§ 38, 40. 

1855, No. 28, § 1. 1852, No. 26, § 1. 42 Vt. 370. 55 Vt. 484. 60 Vt. 121. 75 Vt. 6. 

9520. Towns may make repairs and recover expenses. When the selectmen of a town in which such crossing is located are of opinion that such bridge, culvert, crossing or construction requires repairing or rebuilding in order to be safe for travel thereon, they may notify the corporation required by this chapter to repair or rebuild the same, by leaving a written notice to that effect with the president, superintendent of such road, or the clerk of such corporation. When such corporation does not repair or rebuild the same within one month after such notice, the town may apply by petition to the public service commission to be heard thereon. Upon due summons and hearing, the commission may order and compel such repairs or rebuilding to be made as are necessary for the safety of the traveling public.

P. L. § 6238. G. L. § 5170. P. S. § 4428. 1906, No. 118, § 17. V. S. § 3846. R. L. § 3384. 

G. S. 28, §§ 39, 42. 1855, No. 28, § 3. 1852, No. 26, § 2. 42 Vt. 370. 75 Vt. 6. 

9521. Contract between town and company not affected. Nothing in the seven preceding sections shall interfere with an express contract made by a railroad corporation and a town relative to keeping in repair or rebuilding any such bridge, culvert, crossing or other construction."

P. L. § 6239. G. L. § 5171. P. S. § 4429. V. S. § 3847. R. L. § 3385. G. S. 28, § 43. 

1855, No. 28, § 4. 

Warning Boards, Gates, Signals and Flagmen. 

9522. Warning boards at crossings. A railroad corporation shall maintain warning boards of such description as the public service commission approves at every public highway or street where the same is crossed by its railroad at grade. 

P. L. § 6240. G. L. § 5172. P. S. § 4430. 1906, No. 118, § 18. V. S. § 3848. 1888, No. 27. 

R. L. 3386. G. S. 28, § 54. 1849, No. 41, § 39. 100 Vt. 299. 

9523. Bell to be rung or whistle blown. A bell of at least thirty pounds weight shall be placed on each locomotive engine, and be rung at the distance of at least eighty rods from the place where the railroad crosses a road or street at grade, and be kept ringing until it has crossed such road or street. A steam, compressed air whistle or horn may be blown instead of ringing such bell. The public service commission is directed to see that such steam, compressed air whistle or horn is of adequate loudness to give proper warning in the interest of public safety. 

1953, No. 52. P. L. § 6241. G. L. § 5173. P. S. § 4431. V. S. § 3849. R. L. § 3387. 

G. S. 28, § 55. 1849, No. 41, § 38. 37 Vt. 330. 72 Vt. 190. 97 Vt. 342. 

9524. Penalty. When a person or corporation owning or operating a railroad unreasonably neglects or refuses to comply with the provisions of the two preceding sections, such person or corporation shall be fined not more than $1,000.00.

P. L. § 6242. G. L. § 5174. P. S. § 4432. V. S. § 3850. R. L. § 3388. G. S. 28, § 56. 

1849, No. 41, § 40. 

9525. Gates; electric signals; flagmen. When requested in writing by three or more freeholders of a city or village to order a gate or electric signal to be erected, or a flagman to be stationed at any railroad crossing at grade within such city or village, or when so requested by three or more freeholders of a town to order an electric signal to be erected at any railroad crossing at grade within such town, the public service commission shall visit such place, first giving the parties making such request and the railroad corporation notice thereof. If the public safety requires, such commission shall order the corporation operating such railroad to do any act at such place needful for the protection of the public, and may make any other order or further order it deems necessary in the premises. The

commission shall not make further order without first visiting the place and giving the railroad corporation and the parties making request an opportunity to be heard thereon. 

1947, No. 202, § 9657. P. L. § 6243. 

V. S. § 3851. 1888, No. 26, § 1. 

G. L. § 5175. 

73 Vt. 52. 

P. S. § 4433. 1906, No. 119, § 1. 

86 Vt. 347.

Obstructing Crossings. 

9526. Penalty. A person, corporation or the agents or employees thereof, owning or operating a railroad, who wilfully or negligently obstruct a public highway or farm crossing with engines, tenders or cars, shall be fined not more than $20.00 nor less than $5.00.

P. L. § 6244. G. L. § 5176. P. S. § 4434. R. 1906, § 4287. V. S. § 3853. 1882, No. 39, § 1. 

R. L. § 3391. G. S. 28, § 44. 

9527. Obstructing crossing more than five minutes; penalty. When a railroad crosses a highway or road required for farm use at rail level, the company owning and operating such railroad shall not, nor shall its officers, agents or employees permit an engine, tender or car, or any portion thereof, to stand on any part of such highway or road for a longer period than five minutes at any one time, or in shunting, to obstruct public traffic for a longer period than five minutes at any one time. A person or corporation violating the provisions of this section shall be fined not more than $50.00 nor less then $5.00. 

1947, No. 202, § 9659. P. L. § 6245. G. L. § 5177. 1908, No. 104, § 1. P. S. § 4435. 

V. S. § 3854. 1882, No. 39, § 2. R. L. § 3392. G. S. 25, § 68. 1856, No. 34, § 1. 

93 Vt. 340. 99 Vt. 366. 

9528. Nuisance on highway; penalty. A person or corporation owning or operating a railroad or the agents or employees thereof, shall not allow a handcar or other nuisance to be left on the railroad track or at the side of such track within the bounds of a highway crossed by such railroad. An agent or employee of a railroad corporation who violates the provisions of this section shall be fined not more than $20.00 nor less than $5.00. 

1947, No. 202, § 9660. P. L. §§ 6246, 6248. G. L. §§ 5178, 5180. P. S. §§ 4336, 4338. 

V. S. §§ 3855, 3857. R. L. §§ 3393, 3395. 1880, No. 69, §§ 1, 3. 

9529. Liability for neglect. A person or corporation that violates the provisions of the preceding section, shall be liable for all injuries and damages occasioned by reason of a horse or team becoming frightened thereby, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, brought by the party injured or damaged. 

P. L. § 6247. G. L. § 5179. P. S. § 4437. V. S. § 3856. R. L. § 3394. 1880, No. 69, § 2. 

9530. Obstructing sight of cars at crossing; penalty. A person who leaves wood, lumber or other material on the line of a railroad or highway, at or near the crossing of a highway, so as to prevent or obstruct the sight of cars when approaching the crossing, shall be fined not more than $50.00 nor less than $5.00. 

P. L. § 6249. G. L. § 5181. P. S. § 4439. V. S. § 3858. R. L. § 3396. G. S. 28, § 45. 

1855, No. 25, § 1. 

9531. Nonremoval after notice; penalty. A person who does not remove such obstruction after notice from one or more of the selectmen or from a police officer of the town in which the crossing is situated, shall be fined not more than $50.00 nor less than $5.00 for every twenty-four hours such obstruction remains thereafter. 

1947, No. 202, § 9663. P. L. § 6250. G. L. § 5182. P. S. § 4440. V. S. § 3859. R. L. § 3397. 

G. S. 28, § 46. 1855, No. 25, § 2. 

Farm Crossings, Cattle Guards and Fences. 

9541. Farm crossings and cattle guards; construction and maintenance. A person or corporation owning or operating a railroad shall construct and maintain farm crossings of the road for the use of the proprietors of lands adjoin-

ing the railroad, and cattle guards at all farm and road crossings sufficient to prevent cattle and animals from getting on the railroad. 

P. L. § 6260. G. L. § 5192. P. S. § 4450. V. S. § 3871. R. L. § 3407. G. S. 28, § 47. 

1849, No. 41, § 44. 27 Vt. 140. 38 Vt. 394. 56 Vt. 390. 61 Vt. 268. 

71 Vt. 301. 72 Vt. 263. 82 Vt. 316. 91 Vt. 379. 

67 Vt. 664. 

9542. Same; commission, when parties disagree. When the parties cannot agree upon the plan, manner or number of the farm crossings, the same shall be determined by the public service commission on petition and after a public hearing. However, if the cost of such farm crossings exceeds the value of the land to be accommodated thereby, the commission need not order such crossings to be made, but shall award reasonable damages in lieu thereof. 

P. L. § 6261. 1933, No. 116. G. L. § 5193. 

V. S. § 3872. R. L. § 3408. G. S. 28, § 49. 

1917, No. 254, § 5072. 

1849, No. 41, § 46. 

P. S. § 4451. 

32 Vt. 43. 82 Vt. 316. 

9543. Cattle guards at highway crossings. The provisions of law relieving parties owning property adjoining a highway from maintaining fences along the line of such highway shall not be held to relieve persons owning or operating a railroad from maintaining cattle guards at points where such railroad crosses a highway at grade.

P. L. § 6262. G. L. § 5194. P. S. § 4452. V. S. § 3873. 1884, No. 24. 

9553. Width and Height of Bridges; Clearance; Exemptions. The structure of all railroad bridges in this state, except as hereinafter provided, shall be hereafter so built as to provide a clear horizontal space from center line of track of five feet six inches at level of top of rail, thence increasing uniformly in width to leave a clear horizontal space of eight feet from center line of track at a height of four feet above top of rail, thence continuing to leave a clear horizontal space of eight feet from center line of track to a height of sixteen feet above top of rail, thence decreasing uniformly in width to leave a clear horizontal space of four feet from center line of track at a height of twenty-two feet above top of rail. Tracks over new railroad bridges shall be not less than fourteen feet center to center. The structure of all bridges over railroad tracks in this state, except the two bridges in the village of Middlebury and the bridge on Stowe street in the village of Waterbury and except as hereinafter provided, shall be hereafter so built as to leave a clear horizontal space over the track from level of top of rail to a height of sixteen feet above top of rail of eight feet from center line of track, thence decreasing uniformly in width to leave a clear horizontal space of four feet from center line of track at a height of twenty-two feet above top of rail. Additional tracks under new bridges shall be not less than fourteen feet center to center. All overhead wires, ropes or similar obstructions shall not be less than twenty-two feet above the top of the rails under same. Buildings, sheds, platforms and other similar construction, except icing platforms, adjacent to tracks at heights exceeding eight inches above top of rail shall be constructed with a horizontal clearance of not less than eight feet from center line of track. The provisions of this section shall not apply to tracks, bridges, buildings, platforms and other structures now in existence which do not provide the prescribed clearances, but shall apply to additional bridges, buildings, platforms and other structures hereafter constructed. 

The Public Service Commission, after notice and hearing, may grant such further exceptions from the provisions of this section as may in its judgement be for the public interest. 

1951, No. 198. 1937, No. 168, § 1. P. L. § 6272. 

1910, No. 152. P. S. § 4462. 1904, No. 91, § 1. 

1886, No. 114, § 1. R. L. § 3418. 1880, No. 72. 

1919, No. 132. G. L. § 5204. 

V. S. § 3883. 1892, No. 63, § 1.
