9554. Bridge at Proctor. The highway bridge crossing the track of the 

Rutland railroad on Church street in the village of Proctor may be constructed with such clearance between the top of the rails and the lowest portion of such bridge as the public service commission shall determine to best protect all persons

from danger of injury, having regard both for railroad employees and for the public using the highway.

P. L. § 6273. G. L. § 5205. 1917, No. 152, § 1. 

9555. Bridge at Norwich. In repairing the overpass of the Connecticut and 

Passumpsic Rivers railroad in the town of Norwich immediately south of the Hanover and Norwich station, known as Bridge No. 6, the overpass may be constructed with such clearance between the top of the rails and the lowest portion of the overpass structure as the public service commission shall determine will best protect all persons from danger of injury, having regard both for railroad employees and for the public using the highway and the railroad station. However, in no case shall such overpass be reconstructed with less than the present clearance. 

P. L. § 6274. G. L. § 5206. 1917, No. 153, § 1. 

9556. Telltale warnings. A person or corporation operating a railroad or a part of a railroad in this state shall place such telltale warnings, or other safety devices of uniform pattern for warning trainmen of their approach thereto, as shall be recommended by the public service commission at the approaches of all its bridges, the highway bridges and all other structures of whatever kind or nature which cover or extend over its tracks that do not leave a clear height of twentytwo feet from the top of the rails and lowest parts of such bridges or other structures directly over such rails. 

P. L. § 6275. G. L. § 5207. P. S. § 4463. 1904, No. 92, § 1. V. S. § 3884. 1892, No. 65. 

1886, No. 114, § 2. 61 Vt. 348. 

CHAPTER 406. GRADE CROSSINGS. 

9625. Petitions for alteration, hearings. The selectmen of a town within which a public highway crosses or is crossed by a railroad, or the general manager or attorney of a railroad corporation whose road crosses or is crossed by a public highway, may bring their petition in writing to the public service commission, alleging that public safety requires an alteration in such crossing, its approaches, the method of crossing, the location of the public highway, the elimination of such crossing, the closing of such public highway crossing and the substitution of another therefor, not at grade, or the removal of obstructions to the sight at such crossing, and praying that the same may be ordered, or such proceedings may be instituted by the commission of its own motion and without petition. The commission shall thereupon appoint a time and place for hearing the petition on notice of not less than ten days to the petitioners, the corporation, the municipality in which such crossing is situated, the owners of the land adjoining such crossing, and adjoining that part of the highway to be changed in grade, and to the attorney general, who shall, by himself or through the state's attorney of the county wherein the crossing is located, represent the interests of the state at such hearing. After such notice and hearing, the commission shall determine what alterations, changes or removals, if any, shall be made and by whom. 

1937, No. 169, § 1. P. L. § 6344. 1923, No. 92, § 2. G. L. § 5294. 1908, No. 108, § 1. 

P. S. § 4544. 1906, No. 125, § 1. 81 Vt. 231. 82 Vt. 145. 82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421. 

90 Vt. 201. 111 Vt. 425. 

9626. Same; apportionment of expense; generally. The commission shall apportion the expense of such alterations, changes or removals, including the damages to any person whose land is taken, and the special damages which the owner of land adjoining the public highway shall sustain by reason of any such change in the grade of such highway, between the state, the town in which the public highway or crossing is located and the corporation owning or operating the railroad which crosses such public highway. Such commission shall order an amount not exceeding forty per cent of the whole expense of such alteration, change or removal, including the land damages, as aforesaid, to be paid by the state, and an amount not exceeding ten per cent of the whole expense of such alteration, change or removal, including the land damages, as aforesaid, to be paid by the town in which such public highway or crossing is located. The remainder

of the expense shall be paid by the corporation owning or operating the road which crosses such public highway.

P. L. $6345. 1931, No. 105. G. L. § 5295. 1908, No. 108, § 2. P S. § 4545. 

1906, No. 125, § 1. 

81 Vt. 231. 82 Vt. 145. 82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421. 114 Vt. 41. 

9627. Apportionment of expense, after constructed highway. When the public highway affected by such last mentioned order has been constructed since the railroad which it crosses at grade, the commission may order an amount not exceeding forty per cent of the whole expense of such alteration, change or removal, including the damages, as aforesaid, to be paid by the state, and an amount not exceeding fifteen per cent of the whole expense of such alteration, change or removal, including the damages, as aforesaid, to be paid by the town in which such public highway or crossing is located. The remainder of the expense shall be paid by the corporation owning or operating the railroad which crosses such public highway. 

P. L. § 6346. 1931, No. 105. G. L. § 5295. 1908, No. 108, § 2. P. S. § 4545. 1906, No. 125, 1. 

81 Vt. 231. 82 Vt. 145. 82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421 

9628. Apportionment of expense, contiguous town benefited. Where such alterations, changes or removals in a railroad-highway crossing located wholly in one town materially benefit a contiguous town or towns, or where such alterations, changes or removals require the construction of an underpass or overpass or a connecting section of public highway or part thereof in a contiguous town or towns, where such alterations, changes or removals are ordered to be made at a railroad-highway crossing located in more than one town, the commission may, in its discretion, on due notice and hearing, severally assess such towns in proportion to the benefits conferred. The total of such assessments shall not exceed ten per cent of the total expense including land and special damages. 

P. L. § 6347. 1931, No. 105. G. L. § 5295. 1908, No. 108, § 2. PS. § 4545. 

1906, No. 125, § 1. 81 Vt. 231. 82 Vt. 145. 82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421. 

9629. Apportionment of expense, street railway. Where the intersection of a steam railroad and a street railway occurs within a public highway and such alterations, changes or removals at such intersected crossings are ordered, the commission, on due notice to the corporation owning or operating such street railway shall assess such corporation such per cent of the total expense, including land and special damages, as is according to the special benefits conferred. 

P. L. § 6348. 1931, No. 105. G L. § 5295. 1908, No. 108, § 2. P. S. § 4545. 

1906, No. 125, § 1. 81 Vt. 231. 82 Vt. 145. 82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421. 

or 

9630. Alterations, crossings; order by public service commission.

When the commission, in the absence of any application therefor, is of the opinion that the public safety requires an alteration in any highway crossed at grade by a railroad, or by railroads belonging to or operated by more than one corporation, an alteration in lands or buildings thereon adjoining or near such highway at or near such crossing in order to afford proper view from the approaches to such crossing, in each direction, of the track or tracks of such railroad or railroads, after hearing had on notice of not less than ten days to the corporation or corporations owning or operating such railroad or railroads, to the selectmen of the town within which such highway is situated, to the owners of the land adjoining such crossing and the owners of such land or buildings thereon adjoining, or near such highway as may be required for or materially affected by a proposed alteration, and to the attorney general, who, by himself or through the state's attorney of the county in which such crossing is located shall represent the interests of the state, it may order such alterations in such highway, and the removal of such obstructions to the view in each direction of the tracks of such railroads, as it deems best, and shall determine and direct by whom, at whose expense and within what time such alterations and removals shall be made. 

1947, No. 202, § 9762. 

1906, No. 125, § 2. 

P. L. § 6352. 1923, No. 95, § 1. G. L. § 5299. P. S. § 4547. 

82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421.

9631. Apportionment; expense. In all cases arising under the preceding section, twenty per cent of the expense, including damages and special damages as set forth in sections 9626 to 9629 inclusive, shall be paid by the state. The remainder shall be assessed upon the railroad corporation or corporations benefited by such order. However, such alterations as are thus made at the primary instance of the commission shall not be ordered so as to direct the construction of more than one bridge in any one year on any one railroad. 

P. L. § 6353. 1933, No. 95, § 1. G. L. § 5299. P. S. § 4547. 1906, No. 125, § 2. 

82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421. 

9632. Where tracks of more than one railroad cross highway. When, upon an application or petition brought under the provisions of section 9625, the commission finds that a public highway crosses or is crossed by the tracks of more than one railroad and the tracks of such railroads are so near together that public convenience requires the work of separating the grades to be done under and in compliance with one order, it shall give notice to all the corporations operating such railroads to appear before it and be heard upon the application. After such notice and hearing, the commission shall determine what alterations shall be made, if any, so as to separate the grades of all such crossings at the same time, and shall determine by whom such work shall be done, and shall apportion the expense to be borne by the railroad corporations between such corporations in such manner as the commission deems just and proper. 

P. L. § 6354. G. L. § 5300. P. S. § 4548. 1906, No. 125, § 3. 

82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421. 

9633. Taking of land, proceedings. When a railroad corporation, a town or any other party is ordered to do the requisite work in the alteration, changes or removals at a railroad-highway crossing, or on lands or obstructions to the view near such crossing and it is necessary to take land to carry out such order, the party ordered to do the work shall cause such land to be surveyed and furnish a copy thereof to the owner of such land or his agent. Whereupon, in case the owner of such land and the parties ordered to do the work cannot agree on the price of the land and the damage to the owners resulting from such taking, either party in interest may make written application to the commission. After hearing had, on notice of not less than ten days to the same parties for whom notice is provided in section 9630, such commission shall first adjudge whether the taking of such land is necessary, and, if so adjudged, shall appraise the same and award such damages for the taking thereof as are just. 

P. L. § 6355. 1923, No. 95, § 2. G. L. § 5301. 1908, No. 108, § 3. P. S. § 4549. 

1906, No. 125, § 4. 81 Vt. 231. 82 Vt. 316. 90 Vt. 201. 

9634. Orders of commission; appeals. The order of the commission relating to any matter upon which it may act under the authority of this chapter shall be communicated in writing to the petitioners and to all persons to whom notice of the hearing on such petition was given. Any person aggrieved by such order, who was a party to such proceedings, may appeal from such order and transfer said cause to the supreme court in the same manner as provided by section 9296. 

1947, No. 202, § 9766. P. L. § 6356. G. L. § 5302. 1908, No. 108, § 4. P. S. § 4551. 

1906, No. 125, § 6. 82 Vt. 145. 82 Vt. 316. 83 Vt. 421. 84 Vt. 326. 

9635. Record of changes. The public service commission shall cause all orders made by it under the provisions of this chapter directing the alteration or relocation of a public highway to be recorded in the office of the town clerk where such public highway so altered or relocated is situated before such highway shall be opened for use by the public.

P. L. § 6357. G. L. § 5303. 1917, No. 148.

Part III. Telegraph, Telephone, Express and Electric 

Companies. 

CHAPTER 409. TELEGRAPH, TELEPHONE AND EXPRESS COMPANIES Definition. 

9698. Town; duties of certain officers. As used in this chapter, the word "town" shall include an incorporated village or city. Where powers are given to or duties imposed upon selectmen with reference to a town, the aldermen of a city or the trustees of a village shall have the same powers and perform like duties in relation to their respective city or village. The powers given or duties imposed by this chapter upon selectmen shall be exercised and performed by the state highway board in relation to state highways.

P. L. § 6413. 1933, No. 157, § 6087. 1931, No. 61, § 5. G. L. § 5648. P. S. § 4836. 

V. S. § 4223. 1894, No. 162, § 4087. 

Lines of Wires Along Highways. 

9699. Construction; restriction. Lines of telegraph, telephone and electric wires may be constructed and maintained by a person or corporation upon or under a highway, in such manner as not to interfere with repairs of such highway or the public convenience in traveling upon or using the same. 

P. S. § 4837.

P L. § 6414. 

1888, No. 32. 

65 Vt. 634. 

1927, No. 97. G. L. § 5649. 

1886, No. 89. 1884, No. 47. 

66 Vt. 208. 67 Vt. 272. 

R. L. § 3633. 

1900, No. 65, § 1. V. S § 4224. 

G. S. 88, § 1. 1847, No. 17, § 1. 

1900, No. 65, § 2. 

66 Vt. 208. 

9700. Location by highway board or selectmen; notice. When it is inconvenient or inexpedient to erect such wires, agreeably to the preceding section, the highway board or the selectmen of the town shall determine upon application where and in what manner such wires shall be erected, giving notice to parties interested or their agents, and shall certify their decision and cause the same to be recorded in the town clerk's office, and such decision shall be final. 

P. L. § 6415. 1933, No. 157, § 6089. G. L. § 5650. P. S. § 4838. 

V. S. § 4225. R. L. § 3634. G. S. 88, § 2. 1847, No. 17, § 2. 

9701. Highway board or selectmen may direct and change manner of crossing highway. The highway board or the selectmen of a town may direct a line of wires to be placed at a greater height or underground where it crosses a street or highway. If a line of wires is erected or maintained upon, under or across a street or highway, contrary to the direction of the highway board or the selectmen, or is not changed when directed by them, they may remove such line, and recover the expense thereof from the person or corporation using the same in an action of tort on this statute.

P. L. § 6416. 

V. S. § 4226. 

9702. 

1933, No. 157, § 6090. G. L. § 5651. P. S. § 4839. 1900, No. 65, § 3. 

1884, No. 23, §§ 1, 2. 

Location near residence. When a person objects to the erection of a line of wires along a street or highway in front of his residence, he may apply to the highway board or the selectmen of the town, who, upon notice and hearing, as provided in section 9700, shall determine upon what streets or highways the same shall pass, or in what manner, if at all, such objection may be obviated. Such decision shall be final.

P. L. § 6417. 1933, No. 157, § 6091. G. L. § 5652. P. S. § 4840. 

V. S. § 4227. R. L. § 3635. G. S. 88, § 3. 1847, No. 17, § 3. 

1900, No. 65, § 4. 

67 Vt. 272. 

9703. Trees not to be injured; exception; penalty. A tree within a street or highway shall not be cut or injured in constructing, maintaining or repairing a line of wires, without the written consent of the adjoining owner or occupant, unless the highway board or the selectmen of the town in which the tree is situated, after due notice to the parties and upon hearing, shall decide that such cutting or

injury is necessary. A person or corporation cutting or injuring such trees shall pay the damages, if any, awarded on such hearing, before cutting or injuring the trees. A person or corporation that violates a provision of this section shall be fined not more than $50.00 nor less than $5.00 for each tree so cut or injured. 

P. L. § 6418. 1933, No. 157, § 6092. G. L. § 5653. P. S. § 4841. V. S. § 4228. 1888, No. 32. 

Poles. 

9704. Straight and painted. A person or corporation owning or operating a line of wires in a city or village shall cause the poles upon which the wires are strung to be kept suitably painted, to the satisfaction of the aldermen of such city or the trustees of such village, if incorporated, and, if unincorporated, to the satisfaction of the highway board or the selectmen of the town in which such village is situated, and shall substitute straight poles in place of crooked ones. 

P. L. § 6419. 1933, No. 157, § 6093. G. L. § 5654. P. S. § 4842. 

1890, No. 44, § 1. 1882, No. 75, § 1. 

70 Vt. 180.

V. S. § 4229. 

9705. Same; penalty. A person or corporation that neglects or refuses, after twenty days' notice in writing given by the highway board, a selectman, alderman or trustee, to paint such poles or substitute straight poles for crooked ones, as provided in the preceding section, shall forfeit $100.00 to the state, town, city or village, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute. 

P. L. § 6420. 1933, No. 157, § 6094. 

1890, No. 44, § 2. 1882, No. 75, § 2. 

G. L. § 5655. P. S. § 4843. V. S. § 4230. 

70 Vt. 180. 

9706. Same; selectmen's powers; expense. The highway board, selectmen, aldermen or trustees may cause such poles to be painted, and may substitute straight poles in place of crooked ones, and may recover the expense thereof in an action of tort on this statute, in the name of the state or of such town, city or village against the person or corporation owning or operating such wires or line of wires.

P. L. § 6421. 1933, No. 157, § 6095. G. L. § 5656. P. S. § 4844. V. S. § 4231. 

1890, No. 44, § 2. 1882, No. 75, § 2. 

9707. Cemeteries. The owner or operator of a line of wires shall maintain straight and painted poles upon which such wires are strung, wherever the same pass in front of or along a cemetery, to the satisfaction of the cemetery trustees, or the highway board, or the selectmen of the town, aldermen of the city or trustees of the village in which such cemetery is located if there are no trustees of such cemetery, and such operator or owner shall substitute straight poles in place of crooked ones. The provisions of the two preceding sections as to notice, penalty and procedure, shall apply to and be deemed a part of this section. 

P. L. § 6422. 1933, No. 157, § 6096. G. L. § 5657. P. S. § 4845. 1906, No. 133, §§ 1, 2. 

Damages. 

9708. Appraisal; payment. When, in the erection of a line of wires, the owner or occupant of lands or tenements sustains or is likely to sustain damages thereby, the highway board or the selectmen of the town, upon notice to parties interested, shall appraise such damages. The same shall be paid before the line is erected, unless petition is made to the county court on the question of damages as hereinafter provided.

P. L. § 6423. 1933, No. 157, § 6097. G. L. § 5658. P. S. § 4846. V. S. § 4232. R. L. § 3637. 

G. S. 88, § 5. 1848, No. 31. 66 Vt. 208. 67 Vt. 272. 

9709. Appeal; proceedings. When either party is dissatisfied with such appraisal of damages, he may apply to the county court by petition in the same manner as is provided for a person dissatisfied with the compensation for damages for the laying out or altering of a highway, and thereupon similar proceedings shall be had. The line shall not be erected until such cause is finally decided, unless the party erecting the same files with the clerk of the court to which such application is made, before the line is erected, a bond to the other party, with sureties approved

by such clerk, conditioned for the payment of such damages and costs as may finally be awarded.

P. L. 6424. G. L. § 5659. P. S. § 4847. V. S. § 4233. 1894, No. 162, § 4097. 

Erecting New Lines. 

9712. New lines may be placed on old poles. When a person or corporation is about to erect a line of telegraph or telephone wires, in and along a highway within a town, in and along which a line of poles has already been erected by another person or corporation for a similar purpose, the highway board or selectmen of such town shall have the right to permit and may require the new line to be attached to the poles already standing, as provided in the following section. 

P. L. § 6427. 1933, No. 157, § 6101. G. L. § 5662. P. S. § 4850. V. S. § 4236. R. L. § 3645. 

1880, No. 54, § 1. 

9713. Same; expenses; repairs. The highway board or selectmen shall ascertain, as near as may be, the original cost of erecting such line of poles, and shall direct such person or corporation as they may require to use such poles, to pay the owners of the line already erected a fair proportion of such expense, not to exceed half the estimated original cost of construction. In no case shall the poles be used until the owners of the new line tender to the original owners of such line of poles the amount so directed. When it is necessary to repair or renew the poles used by two or more persons or corporations, the expense thereof shall be borne equally by the parties using the same. 

P. L. § 6428. 1933, No. 157, § 6102. G. L. § 5663. P. S. § 4851. V. S. § 4237. R. L. § 3646. 

1880, No. 54, § 2. 

9714. Notice; copy. The highway board or selectmen shall give written. notice to the proprietors of both the old and new lines of all their requirements in the premises, and shall also lodge a copy of the notice in the town clerk's office, and their decision shall be final.

P. L. § 6429. 1933, No. 157, § 6103. G. L. § 5664. P. S. § 4852. V. S. § 4238. 

R. L. §§ 3647, 3649. 1880, No. 54, §§ 3, 5. 

9715. Restriction of right to move poles; action. The proprietors of a line of poles so required to be used by another person or corporation shall not take down or alter the position of such poles without obtaining permission of all parties who may have acquired a right to use the same, or the permission of the highway board or selectmen. A person or corporation injured by a violation of this section may recover the amount of the injury in an action of tort on this statute. 

P. L. § 6430. 1933, No. 157, § 6104. G. L. § 5665. P. S. § 4853. V. S. § 4239. R. L. § 3648. 

1880, No. 54, § 4. 

9716. No prescriptive rights. Enjoyment of any length of time of the privilege of maintaining a line of telegraph, telephone or electric wires, poles, conduits, or other apparatus, upon or over the buildings or lands of other persons, shall not give a right to the continued enjoyment of such easement or raise a presumption of a grant thereof.

P. L. § 6431. G. L. § 5666. P. S. § 4854. V. S. § 4240. 1894, No. 87. R. L. § 3640. 

G. S. 88, § 9. 1851, No. 62. 

Town and Private Lines. 

9717. Town lines. For their own use towns may construct telegraph, telephone and electric lines upon and along the highways and public roads within their limits, subject to the provisions of this chapter so far as the same are applicable. 

P. L. § 6432. G. L. § 5667. P. S. § 4855. V. S. § 4241. R. L. § 3642. 1874, No. 42, § 1. 

9718. Private lines. The highway board or selectmen may authorize persons, upon such terms as they prescribe and subject to the provisions of this chapter so far as applicable, to construct for private use such lines along the highways of the town.

P. L. § 6433. 1933, No. 157, § 6107. G. L. § 5668. P. S. § 4856. V. S. § 4242. R. L. § 3643. 

1874, No. 42, § 2. 108 Vt. 242.

9719. Same; highway board or selectmen's control; town's rights. After the erection of such line, the posts and structures thereof within the highways shall be subject to the regulation and control of the highway board or selectmen, who may at any time require alterations in the location or erection of such poles and structures and may order the removal thereof, having first given the parties notice and an opportunity to be heard. The town may attach wires for its own use to such posts and structures under such terms and conditions as they deem just. 

P. L. § 6434. 1933, No. 157, § 6108. G. L. § 5669. P. S. § 4857. V. S. § 4343. R. L. § 3644. 

1874, No. 42, § 2. 108 Vt. 242. 

Penalties. 

9724. Injuries to trees. A person or corporation maintaining or operating a line of wires, that cuts down, mutilates or injures the trees standing upon the lands of another, or a person or corporation that affixes or causes to be affixed to the property of another, a post, structure, fixture, wire or other apparatus for telephonic, telegraphic or other electrical communication, without first procuring the right so to do by application to and determination of the highway board or the selectmen of the town, agreeably to this chapter, or first obtaining the consent of the owner or lawful agent of the owner of such property, shall be fined not more than $100.00.

P. L. § 6439. 1933, No. 157, § 6113. G. L. § 5674. P. S. § 4862. V. S. § 4248. 

1884, No. 114.

TITLE 46. DEPARTMENT OF MOTOR VEHICLES. 

CHAPTER 428. DEPARTMENTAL ORGANIZATION. 

10,044. Definitions. Except as may be otherwise provided herein, and unless the context otherwise requires in statutes relating to motor vehicles and enforcement of the law regulating vehicles, as provided in this and the following title, the following definitions shall apply: 

1925, No. 70, § 3. 

100 Vt. 140. 

I. "Carrying capacity of a motor truck" shall be deemed to be the heaviest net load to be carried; 

1947, No. 88, § 1. 1935, No. 113, § 1. 1933, No. 89, § 2. 

III. "Construction area" shall mean and include all of that portion of a highway while under construction, by order of the state or a municipality thereof, located within properly posted warning signs maintained at each end thereof showing such area to have been designated as a "Construction Area"; 

1939, No. 109, § 1. 

IV. "Contractor's trailers" shall mean trailers while being used by contract highway builders for the purpose of transporting road making appliances or motorized highway building equipment from job to job in connection with the building, repair or maintenance of roads or highways; 

1939, No. 109, § 1. 

XIII. "Motorized highway building equipment" shall include all bulldozers, rollers, scrapers, graders, spreaders, pavers, bituminous mixers, compressors, power shovels, excavators, dumptors, concrete mixers, bucket loaders, snow loaders, rooters and scarifiers, except road oilers and bituminous distributors, which contain as an integral part thereof and within the same unit facilities for generating motor power for propelling the same and while being used exclusively for the building, repair or maintenance of highways, or being transported or moved from job to job in connection with the building, repair or maintenance of highways; tractors used exclusively as a power unit in drawing road making appliances or in the transportation of same or of such tractor from job to job in connection with the building, repair or maintenance of highways; 

1939, No. 109 § 1.

XIV. "Motor truck" shall include any motor vehicle having a carrying capacity of fifteen hundred pounds or more, designed and used for the transportation of merchandise or freight; 

1933, No. 89, § 1. 

XV. "Motor Vehicle" shall include all vehicles propelled or drawn by power other than muscular power, except tractors used entirely for work on the farm, vehicles running only upon stationary rails or tracks, motorized highway building equipment, and road making appliances; 

1949, No. 236. 

1939, No. 109, § 2. G. L. § 4667. 1917, No. 131, § 1. 1910, No. 129, § 1. P. S. § 4075. 

1904, No. 86, § 13 102 Vt. 439. 

1902, No. 64, § 4. 

XXVI. "Road making appliances" shall include all bulldozers, rollers, scrapers, graders, spreaders, pavers, retreading machines, compressors, power shovels, excavators, concrete mixers, snow loaders, bucket loaders, rooters and scarifiers, designed to be and while being drawn by a separate motorized power unit and while being used exclusively for building, repair or maintenance of roads or highways or while being transported from job to job in connection with the building, repair or maintenance of roads or highways, except contractor's trailers; 

1939, No. 109, § 1. 

XXIX. "Tractor" shall include a motor vehicle designed or used as a traveling power plant or for drawing other vehicles, but having no provision for carrying a load except motorized highway building equipment; 

1939, No. 109, § 3. 

XXX. "Trailer" is a vehicle without motive power designed to be drawn by a motor vehicle, and so constructed that no part of its weight rests upon the towing vehicle, excepting however, implements of husbandry and road making appliances; and "semi-trailer" is a vehicle without motive power, designed to be drawn by a motor vehicle, and so constructed that some part of its weight and that of its load rests upon or is carried by the towing vehicle, excepting however, poledinkeys, implements of husbandry and road making appliances. 

1947, No. 202, § 5260. 1937, No. 116, § 1. P. L. § 4986. 1931, No. 72. 

CHAPTER 429. REGISTRATION OF MOTOR VEHICLES AND TRAILERS (PART B. NONRESIDENT AND ZONE REGISTRATION). 

10,086. When registration required. No motor truck used for the transportation of property for hire or profit between points within the state and owned by a nonresident, and no motor vehicle carrying an auxiliary fuel tank or tanks providing an additional supply of motor fuel over and above that provided in the standard equipment of such vehicle shall be operated upon the highways of this state unless and until it has been registered and its operator has been licensed under the laws of this state in the same manner as may be required of like vehicles owned, operated and registered in this state. 

1947, No. 89, § 2. 

1931, No 78, § 1. 

1935, No. 121, § 2.

DEPARTMENT OF PUBLIC SAFETY. 

TITLE 47. 

CHAPTER 434. 

OPERATION OF MOTOR VEHICLES. 

Law of the Road. 

10,219. Special provisions. A person shall not operate or cause to be operated a vehicle of any kind, except motorized highway building equipment or road making appliances within a construction area, upon a public highway of this state. contrary to any of the following provisions: 

1939, No. 109, § 5. 1925, No. 70, § 68. G. L. § 4705. 1917, No. 136, § 2. 1912, No. 147, § 1. 

1912, No. 153. 1910, No. 139. 1910, No. 141. P. S. § 4094. 1906, No. 113, § 7. 

1904, No. 86, § 10.

IV. Through ways. Every vehicle immediately before entering the limits of a through way, defined, designated and warned as provided in section 4992, except at a terminus thereof, shall be brought to a full stop except when the operator is otherwise directed by a police officer or by a traffic regulating sign, device or signal maintained by lawful authority; 

1931, No. 68, § 1. 

105 Vt. 128. 108 Vt. 182. 

XII. Barriers. A person shall not drive any vehicle over any highway across which there is a barrier or a sign indicating that such highway is closed to the public travel except by the written permit of the selectmen; 

XIII. Stopping on curve or hill; parking on highway. A person shall not stop nor park the motor vehicle he is operating within one hundred fifty feet of a curve or on the brow of a hill, nor park nor leave standing any vehicle, whether attended or unattended, upon the paved or improved or the used part of any highway, so as to interfere with traffic on such highway, nor shall any person park a motor vehicle on any portion of a highway right of way within one hundred fifty feet of a railway crossing or highway intersection, or within such portion of a highway right of way as the state traffic committee shall find to be a dangerous location on the basis of an engineering and traffic investigation and designate as a noparking zone by suitable signs placed at each end thereof, and by such additional signs as the committee may determine. The provisions of this section shall not apply when some engine, mechanical or other trouble renders it impossible to move such vehicle on its own power, nor to stopping a motor vehicle at a railway grade crossing, nor to highways within cities and villages, where local traffic ordinances and regulations shall govern; 

1953, No. 186. 1931, No. 79. 106 Vt. 500. 109 Vt. 99. 110 Vt. 55. 112 Vt. 175. 112 Vt. 459. 

XIV. Grade crossing. A motor bus carrying persons for hire or any motor vehicle transporting school children for hire shall not pass over a grade crossing of a railroad operated by steam, electricity or other power without coming to a full stop, nor until, by looking and listening, the operator thereof has determined nothing is approaching on the tracks of such railroad; 

1939, No. 120. 

XVII. School bus; duties of operators of motor vehicles. The operator of a motor vehicle, upon meeting or overtaking a vehicle transporting school children displaying the words "school bus" on the front and rear thereof and which has stopped on the highway for the purpose of receiving or discharging school children shall stop such vehicle immediately and shall keep it stationary until such school bus shall resume motion, or until signalled by the driver to proceed, providing a flashing red signal light as required by the school bus act of 1949 is displayed on the front and rear of such school bus. 

1949, No. 243, § 3. 1947, No. 202, § 5384. 1943, No. 81, § 1. P. L. § 5110. 

General Liability for Damages. 

10,222. Liability of municipalities. The state or a municipal corporation shall not be liable for damage to a person or his property by reason of the insufficiency or want of repair of a bridge or culvert while he is traveling on a highway in violation of any of the provisions of this or the preceding title. 

1947, No. 202, § 5387. P. L. § 5112. 1933, No. 157, § 4810. 1925, No. 70, § 81. 

Speed Limit. 

10,246. Speed limit. Outside the limits of a city or incorporated village the maximum rate of speed for a pleasure vehicle shall be fifty miles per hour, and for a pleasure vehicle with trailer or semi-trailer attached, motor truck or motor bus, such maximum rate of speed shall be forty-five miles per hour. However, when a traffic committee composed of the commissioner of highways and the commissioner of public safety shall determine, upon the basis of an engineering and traffic investigation, that the maximum speed limit of fifty miles per hour, outside

the limits of a city or incorporated village, is greater than is reasonable or safe under the conditions found to exist at any intersection or other place or upon any part of a highway, said committee may determine and declare a reasonable and safe speed limit thereat and may alter or change existing speed limits and which shall, thereafter, be effective at all times when signs in such form as the Traffic Committee shall designate giving notice thereof are erected by the department of highways at such intersection, or other place or part of the highway. Outside the limits of a city or incorporated village, no signs changing a speed limit shall be effective on State highway systems unless and until approved by said traffic committee. 

1949, No. 244. 1947, No. 94, § 2. 1937, No. 126, § 2. 

Motor Truck and Weight Regulations. 

10,254. Length of vehicles; special permit; exceptions. No motor vehicle with or without trailer or semi-trailer attached, which is longer than fifty feet overall, shall be operated upon any highway except under special permission from the commissioner of highways. However, the provisions of this section shall not be construed to prevent the operation of so-called pole dinkeys or pole semi-trailers when being used to support the ends of poles, timbers, pipes or structural members capable, generally, of sustaining themselves as beams between the supporting connections, the overall length of which may exceed fifty feet. 

1949, No. 245. P. L. § 5131. 1933, No. 91, § 1. 

10,255. Weight limit; tires and axles. Except as otherwise provided in this chapter, a person or corporation shall not operate or cause to be operated a traction engine, tractor, trailer, motor truck or other motor vehicle without sufficient weight maintained upon the front axle of such vehicle to make such vehicle safe in operation, nor shall the maximum load on any axle of such vehicle exceed a gross weight of more than six hundred pounds to an inch of tire width computed in conformity with the manufacturer's designated width; nor shall such a vehicle be considered as having three axles if two axles thereof are closer together than forty inches or unless the wheels thereof are at all times in contact with the surface of the road when loaded and are equipped with adequate brakes. 

1941, No. 97, § 1. 1939, No. 117, § 2. 

1949, No. 246.

1912, No. 152, § 1. 

10,256. Same; on highways. Except as provided in section 10,264, a person or corporation shall not operate or cause to be operated a vehicle designated in the preceding section in excess of the total weight, including vehicle, object or contrivance and load, of:

I. Sixteen thousand pounds, upon a highway or bridge in any town, incorporated village or city; 

II. Twenty thousand pounds on state aid highways;

III. Thirty thousand pounds for a single traction engine, tractor, motor truck or other motor vehicle having two axles while the same is operated on state highways, including their connections on state aid highways, through cities, villages, or municipalities;

IV. Forty thousand pounds for a three axle traction engine, tractor, motor truck or motor vehicle being operated on highways as provided in subdivision III herein;

V. Fifty thousand pounds for a combination of a traction engine, tractor, motor truck or other motor vehicle with a trailer or semi-trailer, being operated on highways as provided in subdivision III herein.

10,257. Same; designated for certain highways; notice. On all highways in an incorporated village or city the legal load shall be as prescribed for the state highway system, unless otherwise restricted and posted by the local authorities, as provided in this chapter. With the approval of the commissioner of highways, the selectmen of a town may designate any highway in such town to carry the same legal load as specified in the preceding section for state highways. When a certain highway has been approved by the commissioner as to the legal load limit, then the commissioner shall have such highway posted for such legal load limit. Such notices shall be of a permanent nature, one placed at each end of such highway in each town affected. The notices shall be of a standard size, twenty-four inches by twenty-four inches, and shall contain the words "Legal Load as for State Highways" in letters not less than three inches high. The commissioner shall cause to be filed a certified statement with the town clerk in the town in which posting occurs, describing the highway posted and stating the date of posting. The commissioner shall also have signs erected on each road connecting with such highway, stating the legal load limit of the connecting highway leading from such highway. Such signs shall be the same and placed the same as provided in section 10,259.

10,260. Same; highway or bridge specially designated. 10,263. Construction and maintenance equipment; exception. Nothing contained in sections 10,255 to 10,262 inclusive, shall restrict the weight of snow plows, road machines, oilers, traction engines, tractors, rollers, power shovels, dump wagons, trucks or other construction or maintenance equipment when used by any town, incorporated village, city or the state in the construction or the maintenance of any highway, provided that such construction or maintenance is performed by persons employed by or under contract with such town, incorporated village, city or the state for this purpose. However, any operation of motorized highway building equipment or road making appliances used in construction work contracted by a town, incorporated village, city or the state shall be unrestricted as to weight only within a construction area. 

1947, No. 95, § 1. 1941, No. 97. 1939, No. 117, § 2. 1935, No. 125. P. L. § 5132.

10,264. Permit; application. A person or corporation owning or operating a traction engine, tractor, trailer, motor truck or other motor vehicle that desires to operate the same in excess of the weight limits provided by this chapter shall make written application for such a permit to the state highway commissioner. In his discretion, with or without hearing, such commissioner may issue to such person or corporation a permit authorizing such person to operate such traction engine, tractor, trailer, motor truck or other motor vehicle upon such highways in this state as he may designate and containing the regulations subject to which such traction engine, tractor, trailer, motor truck or other motor vehicle is to be operated. Such permit shall not be granted until satisfactory proof is furnished to such commissioner that such traction engine, tractor, trailer, motor truck or other motor vehicle has been registered and the prescribed fee paid for a gross weight equal to the maximum legal load limit for its class. No additional registration fee shall be payable to authorize the use of such traction engine, tractor, trailer, motor truck or other motor vehicle in accordance with the terms of such permit.

10,269. Prosecution. The state, a person, or a municipal corporation injured by any careless, negligent or unlawful act of or failure of the principal, his agents or servants, to comply with the provisions of such permit or on account of the injury to a bridge or culvert because of the excessive weight allowed by such permit, and, if the death of any person is caused by any careless, negligent or unlawful act as aforesaid, the executor or administrator shall have a right of action in the name of the obligee named in such bond for his, its or their use and benefit against the principal and surety named in such bond, and may prosecute the same to final judgment and execution.

10,270. Special permit; bond. In case of oilers, tractors, rollers, dump wagons and other road machinery and trucks, tractors and trailers owned and operated by the federal government, a permit may be issued by the highway commissioner. The furnishing of a bond in such cases shall be in the discretion of such commissioner.

10,271. Operation of vehicles which may damage surface of road prohibited. A person shall not operate or move upon or over any highway or bridge a vehicle, machine or contrivance which has any flange, ribs, clamps, or other object attached to its wheels or made a part thereof, which will injure, cut into or destroy the surface of such highway or bridge to any appreciable extent. 

P. L. § 5141. 1925, No. 70, § 78. G. L. § 4699. 1927, No. 131, §§ 2, 7. 1912, No. 152, § 1. 

10,272. Tractors and trucks with steel tires; speed restricted. A person shall not operate a traction engine or tractor, with or without trailers, nor a motor truck carrying a weight in excess of four tons including the vehicle, upon any highway or bridge at a speed greater than ten miles an hour, when such vehicle is equipped with iron or steel tires.

10,275. Height and width limits, exceptions. Motor trucks and trailer and loads shall not exceed eight feet in width or twelve feet and six inches in height and motor trucks shall be equipped with a mirror not less than five inches in di ameter so attached that the driver of such motor truck shall at all times have a reflected view of other vehicles approaching from the rear. The height and width limits of this section shall not apply to snow plows, road machines, oilers, traction engines, tractors, rollers, power shovels, dump wagons, trucks, highway building equipment and road making appliances employed on highway maintenance nor on highway construction when operated within a construction area, nor shall such limits apply to traction engine, tractor, trailer or motor truck operated on a public highway under a permit from the commissioner of highways, as provided in section 10,264.

10,276. Liability for damage, defined; limitations. The owner, driver, operator or mover of any motor truck, tractor, trailer, wagon, cart, carriage or other object or contrivance which is moved or operated on any highway in violalation of any of the provisions of sections 10,271 to 10,275 inclusive, and such portion of section 10,246 as pertains to trucks and busses, shall be liable to the state or municipal corporation in which the act is committed for damages to a public highway occasioned by such moving or operating, to be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the same of the state or municipal corporation, or in a action on the bond provided in this chapter in connection with the issuance of permits, provided the action is brought within two years after such act is committed.

CHAPTER 435. OFFENSES, PENALTIES AND JURISDICTION. 

10283. Illegal operation. A motor vehicle shall not be operated on a public highway, as defined in section 10,044, in a careless or negligent manner, nor upon a bet, wager or race, nor for the purpose of making a record, nor in a manner to endanger or jeopardize the safety, life or property of a person.

10,284. Local speed limits; appeal; notice. Nothing contained in section 

10,246 shall be so construed as to affect the rights of the selectmen of a town or the proper officials of a city or incorporated village to make special regulations as to the speed of such motor vehicles upon narrow or dangerous roads or ways, nor to limit the speed of motor vehicles in city or village streets, provided such limit is fixed at not less than twenty miles per hour, and provided further that outside the limits of a city or incorporated village the maximum speed limit shall be determined in accordance with the provisions of Section 10,246 of the Vermont Statutes. Such special regulations shall have the full force and effect of law and shall be subject to an appeal to the state highway commissioner, whose decision in the case shall be final. Such regulations shall be posted conspicuously

by the municipality desiring to enforce the same within the highway limits a reasonable distance from a point where such regulations become effective.

10,310. Placing substances injurious to tires on highway; penalty. A person who wilfully places or causes to be placed upon any highway tacks, nails, wire, scrap metal, glass, crockery or other substance injurious to the tires of vehicles, shall be fined not more than $100.00.

10,314. Speed limit on bridges; liability for damages. A person who drives a motor vehicle, other than a motor truck, tractor or motor bus, faster than eighteen miles an hour, or who drives a motor truck, tractor or motor bus faster than ten miles an hour, on a bridge more than eighty feet in length, or on a bridge with string pieces thirty feet long between the supports, or on a bridge supported by one or more arches, or drives on either of the two last named types of bridges with a loaded motor truck, tractor or motor bus when occupied by another motor truck, tractor or motor bus on any one tier of string pieces, or on any one arch, at the same time, shall forfeit $5.00, with damages for injuries thereby done to the bridge which may be recovered in an action of tort on this statute, in the name and for the benefit of the state or municipal corporation liable for the repairs of the bridge, with costs.

10,316. Traffic rules in city or village on special occasions; penalty.

When it appears that the traffic in such town, village or city will be congested by reason of the holding of a fair or other public occasion, the selectmen of a town, trustees of an incorporated village and the aldermen of a city may make special regulations as to the speed of motor vehicles, may exclude such motor vehicles from certain public highways and may make such traffic rules and regulations as the public good requires, but signs indicating such special regulations must be conspicuously posted in and near all areas affected thereby. 

P. L. § 5180. 1925, No. 70, § 104. G. L. § 4140. 1917, No. 137, § 1. 105, Vt. 327. 

10,317. Same; as to traffic lights and one way streets; penalty. The selectmen of a town, the trustees of an incorporated village and the aldermen or city council of a city may make special regulations as to the operation, use and parking of motor vehicles, as to traffic lights, and as to streets designated for one way traffic in the thickly settled portions of their respective municipalities, but signs indicating such special regulations must be conspicuously posted in and near all areas affected thereby. A person who violates any regulations authorized by this and the preceding section shall be fined not more than $50.00. 

P. L. § 5181. 1931, No. 84, § 1. 1929, No. 71, § 1. 1925, No. 70, § 104.

10,318. Farm tractors; highway equipment; operation on highway; exceptions. A person shall not operate a farm tractor or motorized highway building equipment upon a public highway without first procuring from the commissioner of motor vehicles a license to operate motor vehicles, except that the

operator of a farm tractor may operate the same upon a public highway in going to and from different parts of the farm of the owner of such tractor and for repair purpose to any repair shop without obtaining such license. A person operating a farm tractor upon the public highways in going to and from different parts of the farm of the owner or for repairs to a repair shop or a person operating motorized highway building equipment upon the public highways within a construction area under the provisions of this section shall be subject to all of the provisions of sections 10,282 to 10,288 inclusive, and sections 10,290 to 10,298 inclusive. 

A person operating a farm tractor upon the public highways, except when going to and from different parts of the farm of the owner or to a repair shop, or a person operating, be subject to all of the provisions of titles 46 and 47. side of a construction area, under the provisions of this section, shall, while so operating, be subject to all of the provisions of titles 46 and 47. 

1949, No. 249. 1947, No. 99, § 1. 1939, No. 109, § 6. 

10,319. Night operation. Farm tractors, motorized highway building equipment and road making appliances when operated upon a public highway during the period from thirty minutes after sunset to thirty minutes before sunrise shall comply with the requirements of title 46 relating to head lamp and tail light requirements. 

1947, No. 99, § 2. 1939, No. 109, § 7. 

10,320. Rules of highway board as to abuse of highways. The state highway board shall make and promulgate such rules and regulations as from the then condition of any highway or portion thereof shall be necessary to prevent abuse of such highway or portion thereof during any period of any season of the year specified in such rule or regulation. 

P. L. § 5182. 1931, No. 83, § 1. 1925, No. 70, § 103. 

10,321. Same; where filed and posted. Such rules and regulations shall be filed with the selectmen of the towns in which the highways affected thereby are situated, and shall be posted in two public places in such towns. Conspicuous notices thereof shall be posted by such selectmen at each end of the highway or portion thereof affected.

P. L. § 5183. 1931, No. 83, § 1. 1925, No. 70, § 103. 

10,322. Same; penalty. A person who violates such rules and regulations shall be fined not more than $100.00 for each offense and shall be liable to the state or town in which the damage is done for all damages resulting from the acts constituting such violation. 

1951, No. 220. P. L. § 5184. 1931, No. 83, § 1. 1931, No. 61, § 5. 1925, No. 70, § 103. 

TITLE 49. SALARIES AND FEES. 

CHAPTER 444. 

SALARIES. 

10,446. Mileage certificates. The state highway board shall designate a point centrally located in each town and city and shall determine the distance by the nearest or most feasible automobile or railroad route from the point so selected to the state house at Montpelier and shall certify such distances in terms of miles to the auditor of accounts. When so certified, such mileage shall be used by the committees on state and court expenses in computing mileage paid to members, officials and employees of the general assembly as provided by the preceding section. The mileage of other officials receiving traveling expenses on a mileage basis, and whose official duties require travel direct to Montpelier, or from Montpelier to such towns and cities, shall be based on such certificate, but in no case shall it exceed the actual and necessary mileage, as established by the state highway board. 

1937, No. 222, § 2. 1935 No. 206, § 1. 

10,489. Town road commissioner. The compensation of a town road commissioner shall be fixed by the selectmen, and shall not be less than $2.00 per day for time actually spent, and shall be paid out of the highway fund. 

P. L. § 8987. G. L. § 4541. P. S. § 3961. V. S. § 3452. 1894, No. 80. 1892, No. 56, § 16.

NEW ACTS PASSED BY 1949 GENERAL ASSEMBLY No. 47, 1949. STATE BOND ISSUE FOR CERTAIN STATE HIGHWAY BRIDGES. 

SECTION 1. The state treasurer with the approval of the Governor is hereby authorized to prepare and issue in behalf of the state negotiable bonds in the amount of $2,800,000.00 to defray the expenses incurred for the purpose of this act; (1) For the construction of a highway bridge on route U. S. 2 between the towns of North Hero and Alburg $1,250,000.00; (2) For the construction of a highway bridge on route U. S. 2 between the towns of Grand Isle and North Hero $700,000.00; (3) For the construction of two highway bridges on route Vt. 15 in the town of Cambridge $600,000.00; (4) For the construction of a highway bridge on route Vt. 30 in the town of 

Townshend $250,000.00; (5) The construction to include the cost of rights-of-way, relocations, approaches and appurtenances, as required. 

SEC. 2. There is hereby appropriated the sum of $2,800,000.00 for the purposes set forth in this act and such sums shall be available upon certification by the Vermont state highway board from time to time that a specified amount of the sum appropriated herein is required to carry out the provisions of this act, and the auditor of accounts shall issue a warrant for the amount so certified as necessary. 

SEC. 3. If any part of the appropriation herein made shall, in the judgment of the highway board, not be needed for the construction of the bridge for which it shall have been appropriated but shall be needed for the construction of any other bridges named herein, then the highway board shall adopt a resolution setting forth the facts and thereafter the appropriations herein made shall be allocated to the construction named herein in the proportions and in the manner specified in said resolution. The highway board shall forthwith upon adopting such resolution certify the same to the auditor of accounts. The said highway board may alter the said allocations as many times as may be necessary but shall do the same by resolution in the manner herein provided and shall certify each such resolution to the auditor of accounts as herein provided. 

If in the judgment of the Highway Board, it becomes advisable on the part of said Board to use Federal Highway Funds to partly finance the construction of any or all bridges contained herein, the said Highway Board may apply any balance of funds provided for herein in the construction, rights-of-way, relocations, eliminations, approaches and appurtenances of bridges on the State Highway System which are weak, unsafe, or deficient in the opinion of said Highway Board. If it becomes advisable to the Highway Board to use Federal Highway funds as outlined herein, then said Highway Board shall adopt a resolution setting forth the facts and thereafter the appropriation herein made shall be allocated to the costruction named therein in the proportion and in the manner specified in said resolution. If upon the completion of the construction of the bridges herein described, it is found the sum hereby appropriated is in excess of the cost of construction of said bridges, such excess shall be used for the construction of other bridges in the state highway system as shall be determined by the Highway Board. 

SEC. 4. The bonds issued pursuant to this act shall be designated as the Vermont bridge bonds; shall be issued in coupon form, payable to the bearer in denomination of $1,000.00 each; shall be payable serially in substantially equal amounts annually, the first of such annual payments to be made not later than five years after the date of such bonds and the last of said payments to be made not later than ten years after said date. The principal and interest of such bonds shall be payable in lawful money of the United States, and for such payments the full faith and credit of the state is hereby pledged. Such bonds shall be signed by the

treasurer and countersigned by the secretary of state, and shall bear the seal of the state and the interest coupons thereon shall bear the facsimile signature of the treasurer. Said bonds shall be registered in the office of the secretary of state. The date of issue, place of payment, rate of interest, time of maturity and other particulars as to the form of such bonds, within the limitations mentioned herein, shall be determined by the state treasurer with the approval of the governor as they may deem for the best interest of the state. Such bonds shall contain on the face thereof the statement that they are issued for the purposes mentioned in, under the authority of, and in conformity with this act, and that the form and other particulars and details thereof have been duly determined by the treasurer with the approval of the governor and such statements shall be conclusive evidence of the liability of the state to any bona fide holder thereof, and the bonds so issued shall be the lawful obligation of the state. Said bonds shall not be sold or the proceeds thereof used except for the purpose herein stated. 

SEC. 5. The state treasurer with the approval of the governor is hereby authorized to sell such bonds at such prices, in such amounts and at such times and in such manner, with or without advertising the same, as he shall determine to be for the best interests of the state, at public sale. The treasurer shall keep an accurate record of each and every bond, when issued and the number of each bond, when and where payable, to whom sold, and the rate of interest thereon, and shall keep an accurate record of all payments of interest and principal, and when coupons are paid shall deface and cancel the same. Such bonds shall be exempt from taxation in this state.

SEC. 6. The proceeds arising from the sale of such bonds shall be applied to the purposes herein named, but no purchasers of such bonds shall be in any way bound to see to the proper application of the proceeds thereof. The payment of the interest thereon and of principal thereof as they fall due shall be a first and prior charge upon the revenues of the state, and such payments shall be made by the state treasurer as the same fall due without further order or authority. The amount necessary each year to pay the maturing principal and interest of all such bonds then outstanding shall be included in and made a part of the annual appropriation bill for the expense of state government, and the principal and interest on such bonds as may be issued while the legislature is not in session until provision for the payment thereof shall have been made as hereinbefore provided, shall be paid from the receipts of the motor vehicle department from motor vehicle registrations, operators' licenses, transfers and fees, and from the receipts from the tax on gasoline and the amounts necessary to pay such matured principal and interest are hereby appropriated. All sums received as premium and accrued interest from such sale are hereby appropriated for the payment of maturing interest on such bonds.

SEC. 7. Any amount of the proceeds from the sale of bonds as herein provided not available to the department of highways and bridges may be placed on deposit or otherwise invested by the treasurer and the earnings thereof shall be used to reduce the interest and principal of the bonds provided for in this act. 

SEC. 8. The construction provided for in this act shall be commenced on one of the bridges not later than October 1, 1949, on two other of the said bridges not later than July 1, 1950 and on the two remaining bridges not later than October 1, 1950, or not later than twelve months after the final determination, if subsequent to such date, of any legal proceedings wherein such construction or part thereof is enjoined or the availability of rights-of-way delayed the order of such construction to be determined by the highway board.

No. 80, 1949. DUMPING REFUSE; AUTHORITY TO REGULATE; PEN- ALTY. 

A town or incorporated village shall have the power to make, establish, alter, amend or repeal regulations for garbage and refuse disposal, and to regulate the dumping of garbage and refuse within its limits, and to impose penalties for the breech thereof, not to exceed a fine of $100.00 for each offense. Justices of the peace and municipal courts shall have concurrent jurisdiction of offenses hereunder. 

No. 113, 1949. DISTRICT HIGHWAY MEETINGS. 

The selectmen, trustees, road commissioners, patrolmen and street commissioners of the various towns, incorporated villages and cities shall meet with the state commissioner of highways in district meetings at least once biennially to consider such matters pertaining to highways as he may present to their attention, and to discuss such methods of highway improvement and maintenance as may be of special interest to them, and give particular attention to long range plans toward the improvement of back roads. The time and place of such meetings shall be as directed by the commissioner of highways, and the said selectmen, trustees, road commissioners and street commissioners shall be entitled to receive their per diem and necessary expenses from their several towns, incorporated villages and cities for attendance at such meetings.

No. 114, 1949. STATE AID CONSTRUCTION APPROPRIATIONS USED TO RETIRE TOWN BONDS OR NOTES. 

Money available to a town from the state under the provisions of section 4916 and 4924 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947, may be used for the retirement ment of any bonds or notes issued subsequent to May 1, 1949, the proceeds of which were used for highway construction projects on its state aid highways, provided the town shall raise and apply on the retirement of bonds or notes an amount equal to at least one-half the amount provided by state funds. 

No. 243, 1949. SCHOOL BUSES AND REQUIREMENTS THEREFOR. 

SECTION 1. Any motor vehicle of the pleasure car type, or any other motor vehicle of proper construction to safely and comfortably transport seven or more children to or from school and used for that purpose by a town, city or school district, or by an individual for compensation paid by such municipality, shall be a "School Bus."

SEC. 2. In addition to equipment required by subdivision XXVIII of Section 

10,044 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947, any motor vehicle as described in Section 1, not of the pleasure car type shall be equipped as follows: IX. A sign on the front and rear bearing the words "School Bus" with letters not less than four inches in height with a stroke of not less than one-half inch. 

SEC. 4. Motor vehicles of the pleasure car type shall display the signs required in Section 2, subdivision IX, when transporting children.

NEW ACTS PASSED BY 1951 GENERAL ASSEMBLY No. 108, 1951. HIGHWAY ACT. 

SECTION 1. Highway fund. All fees received by the commissioner of motor vehicles as head of the department of motor vehicles, all revenues derived from the tax on motor fuel, the revenue derived from town assessments under section 4967, the moneys received by the highway department for rental of machinery and equipment, for services rendered and from all other sources, and funds receive from municipalities and public utilities cooperating with the highway board in any highway or grade crossing work shall be paid into the state treasury and shall be known as the highway fund. The state highway board shall, unless otherwise provided, and subject to the provisions of No. 35 of the Acts of 1949, control and direct the expenditure of the portion of this fund appropriated by the legislature for state highways, state aid highways, town highways, and the highway department. Funds received from municipalities and public utilities cooperating with the highway board in any highway or grade crossing work are hereby appropriated for the work or project for which such funds are contributed. 

SEC. 2. Town Highways. Construction and Maintenance. The appro priation for town highways shall be available to towns and incorporated villages for construction and maintenance on town highways and bridges, and so much thereof as may be necessary shall be apportioned by the highway board on the basis of $150.00 per mile for each mile of town highway to those towns and incorporated villages expending from their own funds on such highways, exclusive of winter maintenance, a sum aggregating not less than $25.00 per mile of such highways in the respective towns and villages as certified under oath by the selectmen or trustees thereof on forms furnished by the highway board and containing information in respect thereto as required by such board. The expenditure of each town's apportionment shall be laid out and expended under the charge of a committee consisting of the road commissioner, selectmen or trustees and the district engineer having jurisdiction in such town as a representative of the state highway board.

Upon written application of the selectmen or trustees of a town or village and the recommendation of the district engineer, all or a portion of such town's or village's apportionment may be used for the improvement of state aid highways in such town or village. Not more than one-third the apportionment to a town or village may be used for winter maintenance. Any balance of the town highway appropriation may be used by the highway board to assist towns and villages in the construction, repair and maintenance of town highways and bridges. 

1953, No. 264.

SEC. 3. Wherever the words "maintenance fund" appear in the Vermont 

Statutes, Revision of 1947, and amendments thereto, the words "highway fund" shall be substituted therefor. 

SEC. 4. State aid highways; maintenance. The appropriation for state aid highway maintenance shall be used to assist towns and villages in the maintenance of their state aid highways, which shall include patrol, surface treatment, resurfacing, dust laying, winter maintenance, traffic control devices, replacement, repair and any other items of maintenance work deemed necessary by the highway board. The board shall make such rules and regulations for assisting towns and villages in the maintenance of state aid highways and on such terms as they shall deem for the best interests of the state. 

SEC. 5. See Section 4911 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

SEC. 6. Wherever in the revised statutes of 1947 or the amendments thereto and the acts of 1949, the words "district commissioners" or "district highway commissioners" occur they are hereby amended to read "district engineer".

SEC. 7. Sections 4913 and 4914 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947, are hereby repealed.

SEC. 8. See Section 4916 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

SEC. 9. See Section 4922 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

SEC. 10. See Section 4923 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

SEC. 11. Sections 4924 and 4925 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947, are hereby repealed.

SEC. 12. See Section 4930 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

SEC. 13. See Section 4931 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

SEC. 14. See Section 4944 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

No. 170, 1951. CLOSING OF HIGHWAYS BY STATE HEALTH COM- MISSION. 

CHAPTER I 

SEC. 12. Quarantine. The Commission, in case of the prevalence of epidemics or contagious diseases, may quarantine towns, close highways and prohibit railway trains and other common carriers from stopping at quarantined places and may establish such quarantine regulations against other states and countries as will prevent the introduction of contagious and infectious diseases therefrom into this state.

CHAPTER IV 

SEC. 46. Sewers and plumbing; orders; appeal. The local board may require an owner of a dwelling house or other building, abutting on the public street in which there is a public sewer, to have all drains and sewers from such house or building connected with such street sewer. When such board deems it necessary it shall provide such house or building with plumbing in accordance with the plumbing regulations of the commission and shall make the necessary connections between the plumbing and such public sewer. Either party may appeal to the commission. After proper hearing, inspection and consideration, such commission shall decide as to the necessity of providing such plumbing and making such connections, and its decision shall be final.

NEW ACTS PASSED BY 1953 GENERAL ASSEMBLY No. 91, 1953. ADVANCE PAYMENT OF STATE FUNDS TO TOWNS. 

SECTION 1. Advance funds; approval. On a road or bridge construction project which has been approved by the state highway board to be paid wholly or partially from money available to towns under chapter 233 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947, including the one-half cent gasoline tax allocated to towns by the statute, and the total cost of such project is in excess of $1,200.00, a town shall be entitled to receive from the state in advance of the start of construction, a sum of money equal to seventy-five per cent of the total amount the state is obligated to pay toward the cost of each road or bridge construction project. 

SEC. 2. Application; reports; receipts. The advance payment provided for in section 1 shall be granted upon written application of the local town authorities who have made the application and shall be covered by reports as required to be filed with highway board under section 4919 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947, monthly after the job starts. The balance due from the state on any such construction project shall be paid after the project has been completed and all reports mentioned above for the whole job have been received and approved by the state highway board and the auditor of accounts is hereby directed to issue his warrant for payment.

SEC. 3. Records. Money advanced to towns under section 1 of this act shall be used only for projects for which the advance was designated and each town shall keep separate records and accounts of each project included in this act. 

SEC. 4. Liens. Payments not satisfactorily covered by reports under sections 1 and 2 shall become a lien on all funds due such towns from the state, except funds due the town school district, after the expiration of one year from the date the advance payments were made and upon a certificate from the commissioner of highways being filed with the state treasurer and auditor of accounts that the delay in submitting covering papers or refunding any unexpended balance is due to failure of the town officers to reasonably fulfill their obligations. 

SEC. 5. Notification. Whenever an application is approved which would come under the provisions of this act it shall be the duty of the commissioner of highways to notify such town that the state will advance seventy-five per cent of its obligation upon proper application. 

SEC. 6. Construction. The word "town" or "towns" as used herein shall be construed as also including incorporated villages and cities. 

SEC. 7. Repeal. No. 115 of the Acts of 1949 is hereby repealed. 

No. 158, 1953. SIRENS AND SIGNAL LAMPS ON MOTOR VEHICLES. 

SECTION 1. Use of sirens and red signal lamps; restrictions. No motor vehicle shall be operated upon a highway of this state equipped with a siren, or red signal lamp visible from the front of such motor vehicle, except school buses, garage wrecking equipment, ambulances, snow removal equipment, or state and municipally owned motor vehicles used by enforcement officers, privately owned vehicles owned by duly elected or bonded law enforcement officers or members of a fire department in the performance of their official duties or fire trucks owned and operated by volunteer fire companies. However, the commissioner of public safety may, in his discretion, issue to enforcement officers or members of a fire department a permit to have installed on their privately owned motor vehicles a siren or red signal lamp. 

SEC. 2. Same; exceptions. Except as provided in the preceding section, no vehicle shall be operated upon a highway in this state equipped with other than a clear white, green or amber lens. However, the commissioner of public safety may,

in his discretion, issue a permit to motorized highway building equipment, road making appliances or other emergency vehicles to have installed a blue signal lamp visible from the front. 

SEC. 3. Inspection of permits for sirens, red or blue signal lamps.

Permits issued by the commissioner of the department of public safety in accordance with the provisions hereof shall be carried in some easily accessible place in the vehicles authorized to use said equipment and shall be available for inspection at any or all times by a state police or motor vehicle inspector, or during the inspection by the authorized personnel of an official inspection station designated as such by the motor vehicle department, as provided by section 10,154 of the 

Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947. 

SEC. 4. Penalty. A person who violates a provision of this act shall be fined not more than $25.00 or imprisoned not more than thirty days, or both. 

No. 212, 1953. ASSIGNMENT OF STATE AID CONSTRUCTION APPRO- PRIATIONS. 

SECTION 1. State aid funds; assignment. For the purpose of constructing a superior-to-gravel surfaced road on a state aid highway, the selectmen of a town are authorized to assign to the state highway board the sums of money, or a part thereof, to become due such town under the provisions of section 4916 of the Vermont Statutes, Revision of 1947, as amended by section 8 of No. 108 of the 

Acts of 1951, for not to exceed four future years, and also, for a like purpose, to assign to the state highway board the sums of money, or a part thereof, to become due such town under the provisions of No. 35 of the Acts of 1949 for not to exceed four future years. All of such assignments shall be in repayment of sums of money advanced to such town by the state highway board out of currently available funds for construction of state aid highways.

SEC. 2. Referendum. This act shall take effect when approved by a major ity of the legal voters of the town present and voting at any properly warned regular or special meeting of the voters of said town.
